The 95% ranges hold the truth for about 94% of trainers. A synthetic league with a known truth (tests) shows the fit recovers effects when fighters move (r above 0.6) and claims little when they do not.
- **The result is mostly "cannot tell".** On the demo only about 1 of 150 trainers is clearly above or below average and about 10 more lean one way; the ranges are about ±85 Elo wide. The page says so up top and shows the range for every row instead of a bare ranking.
- **Does changing trainer matter?** A plain comparison: rating change over the next six fights for fighters who changed head trainer (+4.9) against fighters who stayed (−0.7): +5.6 ± 1.9 Elo. Real but small, and not causal (fighters switch after bad runs).
- **Underdog lifters** (wins against Elo expectation when under 40%) is shown as a list to watch: at these sample sizes it is mostly luck, and the page says so.
- **Limits:** a fighter's ability is treated as fixed through a career; the standard error ignores how fighter and trainer effects overlap; the penalty is a judgment (a typical trainer within about 60 Elo); the effect is of the head trainer only.

Both: Arabic (128 strings, machine-translated; number-agreement strings use plural forms), sitemap, ⌘K, nav (Upset watch under Fights, Trainer impact under Camps and money), tests (`tests/upsets.test.ts`, `tests/trainer-impact.test.ts`, `tests/trainer-moves.test.ts`; mutation-checked), axe clean and no overflow at six widths on every new or changed page.

## 28. Ask the data (round 13, 2026-10-03)

**What it is** (`lib/ask/`, `/ask`, `/api/ask`, a link from the home page, ⌘K, nav). A question in plain English or Arabic, answered from the database and never from a model's memory. Three steps:
1. **Plan.** The question becomes up to three calls to a fixed set of read-only tools: `fighters` (filters and sort), `record_list` (the 16 all-time lists), `fighter`, `head_to_head`, `rankings`, `champions`, `bouts` (search completed fights), `events`, `fight_of_the_year`, `upset_watch`, `trainers`, `money`. With an API key Claude picks the tools and arguments; without one, or when it fails or is rate-limited, patterns do (`rules.ts`, English and Arabic). Every call is checked against the tool's declared arguments, so anything a model makes up (an extra argument, an unknown tool, a number out of range) is dropped: it can run these queries and nothing else.
2. **Run.** The tools run over the in-memory world. Their tables are always shown beside the answer, with links, so any answer can be checked.
3. **Answer.** With a key Claude writes two to four sentences from the tool results only; **any figure in its answer that is not in the results throws the answer away** for the rule-based one. Without a key a sentence per tool is used. The page says which wrote it ("Written by AI" or "Put together by rules"), and that no key is set when that is so.

**Cost and abuse.** At most two model calls per question, each counted by `lib/ai-guard.ts` (per-client and daily limits, so a refused call falls back to rules and says so, and is not cached); answers are cached per language and day; two parallel identical questions share one run. The question is capped at 300 characters and the prompts tell the model the question and results are data, not instructions (a test sends an injection and checks that nothing but the tools can be reached). Pages with a question are `noindex`.

**Honest limits.**
- **The AI path has not run against the real API** (no key here): it is tested against a mocked model (prompts, plan validation, grounding, fallbacks, limits, caching), not against Claude's actual behaviour. Expect to tune the prompts on first real use.
- The rule planner covers the question shapes above; anything else gets "I could not match that" rather than a guess. It is the floor, not the product.
- A model can still choose a poor tool for a question; the tables make that visible, and the grounding check stops invented numbers but not a misread question.
- Machine-translated Arabic (91 strings + plural forms) and Arabic patterns are limited to the example shapes and a handful of phrasings.
- Tests (`tests/ask.test.ts`, 17, mutation-checked): plan cleaning, every pattern and example in both languages, each tool against a raw count of the data, grounding, the AI path with a fake model, limits, caching, injection, API.

**Scale (160,000 bouts):** the data work for a question is 7 to 200 ms (the cold all-time list is the slowest); a model call, when there is one, is far longer.

## 29. Fixing the finish estimate the Track record page caught (round 11, 2026-10-03)

§22 published, against the model's own name, that its early-finish estimate was off: it expected about 55% of fights to end by stoppage and about 40% did, and the fight previews' "how it could end" split inherits that. This round fits it.

- **What was wrong:** the estimate was a hand-set rule, `0.22 + 0.35 × (both fighters' KO rates) + 0.2 × (both KO-loss rates)`, clamped to 10-85%. On the demo league's held-out fights it scored **worse than predicting the base rate** (log-loss 0.750 against 0.674) and averaged 55.6% against 40.3% actual. In this league a fighter's past KO rate barely predicts whether *this* fight ends early, so the rule's confident spread (some upcoming fights at 72% to 82%) was not backed by anything.
- **The fit** (`runFinishFit` in `lib/fit.ts`, part of `npm run model:fit`): a logistic regression of "ended by stoppage" on the two fighters' summed KO rates and summed KO-loss rates, trained on the first 75% of bouts and tested on the last 25% (the same bouts and split as the win model, checked by a test). It is recommended only if it beats the hand-set rule by `MIN_GAIN` (0.002) on held-out log-loss, the same parsimony rule as the win model. On the demo league: log-loss 0.6723 (base rate 0.6743), averaging 41.6% against 40.3% actual, coefficients +0.233 per KO rate and +0.091 per KO-loss rate (small: the signal is weak here). It is applied to every finish estimate when recommended.
- **How it is applied:** `FinishModel` and `stoppageProbability(a, b, finish = activeFinish())` in `lib/model.ts`; `applyFittedFinish` in `lib/model-fit.ts` runs with the win-model weights when the world is built, independently of them (a report can fit one and not the other; an older report with no finish section changes nothing). Predictions take the finish model as a parameter, and the matchup lab is handed it as a prop so the browser agrees with the server. `modelVersion()` in the ledger includes the finish model, and is **unchanged while none is fitted**, so existing ledger fingerprints keep their meaning.
- **The Track record page** now says whether the finish estimate is fitted, and after a fit shows the gap closed: predicted 41.6% against 40.3% actual, verdict "calibrated" (before: 55.6%, "runs high").
- **Tests** (`tests/finish-fit.test.ts`): the hand-set rule is exactly what it was when nothing is fitted; a fitted model is a clamped logistic (never 0 or 100%); predictions use the active model, an explicit one overrides it; the fit uses the same bouts and split as the win model, beats the hand-set rule, averages what happened; the recommendation needs a real gain (0.0015 is noise, more than 0.002 counts); applying a report sets, clears or leaves alone correctly; the page's verdict goes from "runs high" to "calibrated"; the fingerprint is stable without a finish model and changes with one. Always recommending the fit, or never clearing a stale one, each fails the suite. Passes with and without a `data/model-fit.json` on disk.
- **What it means for the product:** stoppage language on the site ("Stoppage likely", "Expect a stoppage") now appears only when the data supports it. On the demo league that is almost never, because the simulator does not tie finishing to past KO rate; on a real feed `npm run model:fit` will find whatever relationship exists, and the Track record page will show whether it held.
- **Not done:** more features for the finish estimate (scheduled rounds, division, rating gap, referee and judge tendencies) are plausible but each must earn its place on held-out data; the finish model is not yet shown on the Data page's model report (the CLI output is the report for now).

## 30. Night in review (round 11, 2026-10-03)

The recap says what one fight changed; the night in review says what the whole card did. It sits at the top of every completed event page and is built from the recap of each decided fight on the card (`lib/night.ts`), so it cannot disagree with the bout pages. Facts first, sentences second, in both languages; no key needed.

- **What it reports, most newsworthy first:** new champions and title defences (with the belt in the reader's language); **the night's biggest surprise**, the winner the model gave the lowest chance when that was under 40%; how many fights were decided and how many ended inside the distance; the fastest finish (earliest round, then the clock); how many winners the model picked (its pre-fight call, the same number each bout page shows); the biggest rise within a division's top 15, counting a new entry as a rise from just outside; the longest win streak run up on the night (3+); and unbeaten runs (5+ fights) that ended. A card with no decided fight, and any upcoming or cancelled card, has no review.
- **Tests** (`tests/night.test.ts`, every completed card in the league): decided and early-finish counts, new champions and defences agree with the recaps; the surprise is the card's least likely winner and equals the number on its bout page; the model's record equals the sum of its calls on the card's fights; nothing ended in an earlier round than the "fastest finish"; the climb is a real rise within the top 15 that appears in a bout recap; the streak is the card's longest; both languages complete with no leftover placeholders and no English sentence words in Arabic; a new champion always leads. Picking the most likely winner as the surprise, or the slowest finish as the fastest, each fails the suite. About 100 cards are built in 200 ms.
- **Wording:** names are used instead of pronouns and counts avoid number-noun agreement (Arabic "عدد النزالات المحسومة: 14") so no gendered or mis-agreed forms are possible.
- **Not done:** the same summary on the home page for "last night", an optional AI rewrite of these facts into a paragraph, and the "fights decided" count excluding draws and no-contests by design (a draw is neither decided nor listed).

## 31. Accessibility and design pass, round two (round 14, 2026-10-03)
A second sweep of every route built since round 9 (36 routes, both languages, both rail states): axe 0 violations; reflow at five widths; text spacing at two. Found and fixed a regression I introduced in round 9 (the heatmap's hidden text widened `/analytics` to 423 px at 320 px), a long-Arabic-name overflow, 16 unnamed tables, two placeholder-only inputs, 6 px slider hit areas that read out bare numbers, silent odds changes in the what-if lab (now a live region), and several sub-24 px targets. `tests/a11y.test.ts` gained rules for table names, input labels, svg descriptions, the heatmap cell and the slider size. Not done: a real screen reader, the full visual design review, poster contrast. Details in `docs/accessibility.md` (round 14).

## 32. The smoke check: every page, both languages, on a real server (round 11, 2026-10-03)

CI proved the code compiles and the unit tests pass, but nothing ever rendered a page: a build succeeds even when a page throws at runtime, and a template can print "undefined" with every test green. `npm run build && npm run smoke` (run in CI after the build) closes that gap.

- **What it does** (`scripts/smoke.ts`, `lib/smoke.ts`): seeds a throwaway database with the demo league (clock pinned to 2026-10-03), starts `next start` on a free port, and requests a representative page of every kind in **English and Arabic**: the home page, every navigation entry, fighters (top-rated, retired, women's, almost no fights), a matchup, plain-English search, bouts (decision, knockout, title fight, draw, cancelled, no result yet, upcoming), a fight preview, events (completed, upcoming, postponed, cancelled), people (trainer, manager, judge, referee), organisations (gym, promotion, sanctioning body), belts, division rankings (men's and women's), all-time lists, fight of the year; plus the JSON and image endpoints and an unknown URL. About 115 requests, under 10 seconds.
- **What it checks per page:** HTTP 200; `<html lang>` and `dir` match the URL (an English page for an Arabic URL, or Arabic that is not right-to-left, fails); at least one `<h1>`; and in the text a reader sees (scripts and styles stripped) none of: the word `undefined`, `NaN`, `[object Object]`, `Infinity`, `null`, an unfilled `{placeholder}`, or an error page. APIs must return valid JSON, the portrait a real SVG, and an unknown URL a real 404 with `noindex`.
- **It cannot be outgrown:** a test fails if a new `[param]` page is added under `app/[locale]` without a sampler in `lib/smoke.ts`, or if a navigation entry is missing from the route list.
- **Proven, not assumed:** on the demo league all 114 checks pass. With two bugs injected on purpose (a fighter page printing "undefined", and a people page throwing) the check reported exactly those pages in both languages, with the offending text shown; reverting them returned it to 114 of 114.
- **Finding, not fixed (framework behaviour):** for an unknown URL or a fighter, event or bout that does not exist, the server returns the correct **404 status and a `noindex` tag**, but the HTML body is empty; the not-found page ("Not on the card") reaches the visitor only through the streamed payload that the browser renders. A visitor with JavaScript sees the page in both languages (checked in Chromium); one without sees a blank page. Next's streaming documentation describes this class of behaviour. Impact is small (search engines get 404 and noindex; no-JavaScript visitors on a missing page), so it is recorded here and the smoke check asserts what is true: a 404, `noindex`, and the heading present in the response.
- **Not done:** it samples one page per kind rather than every fighter (a data-dependent crash on a rare record would need a bigger sample or the real feed's edge cases); it does not run a browser, so client-only breakage is not seen; with a real data feed the sample should be re-checked against that feed's rarest records.

## 33. Native Arabic review (round 15, 2026-10-03)
I cannot be the native speaker, so this builds what a real review needs and refuses to claim one has happened. `lib/i18n/review.ts` (checks, review status, import), `lib/i18n/review-sheet.ts` (the offline sheet), `scripts/i18n-review.ts` (`export`, `import`, `status`, `qa`), `i18n/ar.review.json` (who approved or edited what, with a hash of the exact Arabic they saw), a "Arabic review" panel on `/data`, `docs/arabic-review.md`.
- **Nothing is "reviewed" unless a person says so,** and an approval stops counting the moment the text changes (it becomes "changed since review"). Today: 0 of 1,533 strings and 0 of 1,848 names reviewed, and the page says so.
- **The sheet** is one offline HTML file: strings ordered most-seen first, filters, in-place editing with live placeholder checks, plural forms with example numbers, flag-with-note, a terminology tab, a names tab, seven questions for decisions only a native speaker can make (Latin abbreviations, register, gender, number and plural forms, boxing terms, name conventions, statistics wording), and progress saved in the browser. The reviewer returns one JSON file.
- **Import validates** (unknown keys, changed placeholders or tags, missing plural forms, empty text are rejected one string at a time; wrong format or no reviewer name refuses the whole file) and is idempotent. A test caught a real bug here: a file that edited and then approved the same string recorded the hash of the old text.
- **Mechanical checks** found one hard issue and 78 notes across all strings; my own skim of the 25 gendered pairs and 40 random strings found the Arabic grammatical and idiomatic, with one real defect (a dropped "after 2015", fixed). That is spot-checking by a non-native reader, not a review, and the docs say so.
- **Names now have a durable home** (round 16): `i18n/names.ar.json` is committed and loaded into the database whenever it opens, a reviewed database name is never overwritten by an unreviewed file copy, the names commands and the review import save back to the file, and a test checks the file covers every name the demo league can show. A sheet is tied to a dictionary build, but imports go by key, so an older sheet still imports (changed strings are simply compared by hash later).
- Tests: `tests/arabic-review.test.ts` (11): each check, status and staleness, import rules and idempotence, the sheet (offline, valid script, safe against markup in the data), the real dictionary, and the real CLI run on a copy (export, import, names, glossary, status); seven mutations broken on purpose, all caught.

## 34. Deployment readiness (round 14, 2026-10-03)

**What is here.** `Dockerfile` (two stages, production dependencies plus `tsx` for the maintenance scripts, runs as the unprivileged `node` user), `.dockerignore`, `GET /api/health` (200 once the database is open and the world is built, 503 with no detail otherwise), a CI job that builds the image, runs it on an empty volume and waits for the health check, and `docs/deploy.md` (persistence, settings, backups, updating).

**Decisions.**
- **One instance, one volume.** The database, the live ledger and the AI cost counters are all per process. Scaling out is a design change (shared store), not a setting; the doc says so.
- **`next start`, not `output: "standalone"`.** The app reads fonts, the fitted model and the glossary by path at run time; standalone tracing would have to be taught each. The cost is image size (about 600 MB of `node_modules`).
- **The fitted model lives in the volume.** `data/model-fit.json` is a symlink to `/data/model-fit.json`, so `npm run model:fit` inside the container survives a redeploy with no code change.
- **Health reports counts only.** No paths, versions or error text; the reason goes to the server log.

**Checked here:** the production build with a production-only install (`npm ci --omit=dev` plus `tsx`) starts, builds the world, answers `/api/health` and pages, and `model:fit` writes through the symlink into the volume. **Not checked here:** the image itself (no Docker daemon in this environment). The CI `docker` job is the first real build; read its result before trusting the Dockerfile. No hosting provider is chosen.

**Tests:** `tests/health.test.ts` (2, mutation-checked: a leaked error message fails it).

## 35. A committed home for the Arabic names (round 16, 2026-10-03)
The 1,848 Arabic name spellings lived only in the local, gitignored database, so a fresh checkout had none and any reviewed name would be lost with the database. They are now in `i18n/names.ar.json` (one name per line, sorted, with source and reviewed flag), `lib/i18n/names-file.ts` loads the file into `name_translations` whenever the database opens (a name a person reviewed in the database is never overwritten by an unreviewed file copy), and `i18n:names` gained `save`, `load` and `check` (and saves after `import` and `auto`); the review import writes reviewed names back to the file. Tests (`tests/names-file.test.ts`, 6, mutation-checked): load rules, never-shrink save, deterministic sorted output, malformed entries ignored, the committed file (well formed, no review claimed, covers every name the demo can show, loaded on open), and the real commands on a copy. All names are still machine transliterations and none is reviewed.

## 36. Settling the research conflicts (round 17, 2026-10-03)
The first harvest ended with 18 conflicting claims (10 subjects). Looking at each showed that only some were disagreements, so the fix is mostly in how the pipeline reads claims, plus a recorded way to rule out the rest.
- **What the 18 were.** Three subjects (Canelo, Joshua and Fury "2024") were Forbes and Sportico describing different twelve months, not conflicts. One was a floor against a round figure (Crawford–Spence "at least 650,000" against 700,000; Pacquiao–Hatton "over 800,000" against 830,000 and 850,000), one a computed figure counted as a source (buys times price), and four were one source against two independent ones (Usyk's purse, De La Hoya–Mayweather price, Hagler's guarantee, Ruiz's purse). One, Ruiz–Joshua II's site fee, could not be settled by evidence.
- **Pipeline changes.** Earnings carry a `list` and are compared only within it; `promote` writes one row per list. Each value on a claim has its own status, so a disputed value no longer blocks the verified ones next to it (this also fixed a claim being held back whole). `data/research/decisions.jsonl` records an exclusion with a coded reason, a sentence, evidence links, who and when; a decision can only remove a figure from the comparison, never add one, claims are named by a hash of their values (an edited claim makes the decision stale and a test fails), and the digest `docs/research-results.md` lists every decision.
- **Tie-break evidence** (added as ordinary claims and re-checked by code): HBO's $54.95 suggested price (Multichannel News, before the fight) and F4W's $12 million Hagler guarantee each outvoted the dissenter; Front Office Sports' ~700,000 buys outvoted the floor figure; a second outlet (EssentiallySports) took Sportico's Canelo $73 million and Joshua $60 million from one source to two.
- **Result:** 432 claims: 275 verified, 118 single source, 32 unconfirmed, 7 excluded, **0 conflict** (was 18). Eight decisions on record.
- **Honest limits.** The checker counts sites, not origins: the Ruiz II site fee's three "$40 million" sources all trace to one reporter (Mike Coppinger, The Athletic), and the rival $100 million is an unnamed "British media" report that ESPN passed on; I excluded it as hearsay and the figure is the best-supported, not a confirmed one. Ruiz's purse ($9 million against a pre-fight "about $10 million") is the weakest call; the $10 million guarantee stays in as its own single-source figure. Three pages (CNBC, Forbes, BoxingScene) answered 403, so they were treated as closed and not worked around. None of this reaches the demo database: its boxers are fictional, so `promote` matches 0 of the real claims until the real feed is loaded; matching, per-value publishing and per-list rows are tested on fixtures.
- Tests: `tests/research.test.ts` (+5, now 15; four mutations caught: exclusion ignored, list dropped from the key, claim-level promotion, no per-list merge), including one that fails while the committed research has an open conflict or a stale decision.

## 37. The finish estimate learns from the matchup (round 15, 2026-10-03)

§29 fitted the early-finish estimate on two inputs (both fighters' KO rates and KO-loss rates). §29 left open whether anything else earns a place. This round tried each candidate on the same held-out fights and kept what clears the same bar as everywhere else (`|z| >= 2` on the training fights, then the fit must beat the hand-set rule by `MIN_GAIN`).

- **Tried** (added one at a time to the two-input fit, held-out log-loss gain): rating mismatch +0.0031; weight class (light or heavy) +0.0010 to +0.0025; scheduled rounds −0.0017 to −0.0004; title fight −0.0006; women's fights −0.0002. Rounds, title and sex added nothing and are not in the model.
- **Kept:** `mismatch` (the plain-Elo gap before the fight, 0 even to 1 certain; z 3.4, so lopsided fights end early more often) and `weight` (the division's limit, 0 at 150 lb to 1 at 220 lb and no limit, negative below; z 5.1, heavier divisions end early more often). **Dropped:** the KO-loss rate (z 0.2 once the others are in; its coefficient is exactly 0). The KO rate stays (z 2.9).
- **Result on the demo league (held-out, 1,587 fights):** log-loss 0.6681 against 0.6723 for KO rates alone, 0.6743 for the base rate and 0.7501 for the hand-set rule; the average estimate is 42.2% against 40.3% that happened. The finish estimate is still weak: it separates fights a little, it does not call them.
- **Where the inputs come from.** `Features.weightLb` is set from the fighter's division (`featuresOf`) and, in the backtest, from the division of **that fight** (a fighter who moves up was not at the new weight before). Two classes in one fight: the heavier limit. Unknown weight counts as mid-range. The ratings are those before the fight. No call signature changed.
- **Old reports still work:** a `model-fit.json` from before this applies with the new terms at zero. `npm run model:fit` prints each input's z, whether it was kept, and the KO-rates-only comparison.
- **Honest limits.** The demo league is synthetic: its generator may build these effects in, so the *sizes* above are not evidence about real boxing; the *procedure* (propose, test on later fights, keep only what earns its place) is what carries over. Mismatch and weight class are the obvious candidates; referee and judge tendencies, scheduled rounds against real round-by-round data and styles are still untried. The fit is not yet shown on the Track record page beyond "fitted to results".
- **Tests:** `tests/finish-fit.test.ts` (12, mutation-checked: inverting the mismatch, keeping every input and a heavyweight counted as lighter each fail) and `tests/finish-division.test.ts` (2, a hand-made league where a fighter moves up: using the fighter's current class instead of the fight's fails).

## 38. Real data, no spend: the adapter and the readiness list (round 16, 2026-10-03)

**Why now.** The accountability work (§22-§26) grades a fictional league; the live ledger only starts its clock when real upcoming fights are loaded. Real data is the gating item, and most of the work can be done before paying anyone.

**What was built.**
- `lib/providers/boxing-data-api.ts`: an adapter for the Boxing Data API written against the vendor's *published* docs (fighters, fights, events, the `/fights/schedule` endpoint for upcoming fights), selected by `BOXING_PROVIDER=licensed`. `lib/providers/licensed.ts` now builds it from `BOXING_API_*` settings.
- **Catches built in:** a request budget (default 90, under the free tier's 100 a month); a refusal to fill the database until `BOXING_API_STORAGE_CONFIRMED=1` (the vendor's storage terms are unconfirmed, §4; **changed in §45: storing is now on by default, provisionally**); the key never appears in an error or a log; a failure is loud, not a half-loaded league; a fighter that cannot be fetched drops only its own bouts.
- **Every approximation is counted** (`notes()`): the feed has no birth dates (only an age), no corner colours, no round times, a generic `PTS` result, no draw value, a free-text location, and null height or reach for some fighters. The mapping approximates the same way each time and reports how often.
- `npm run vendor:sample -- --fights 10`: fetches a small sample (about 20 requests), writes a gitignored FeedData file and prints the approximations; `npm run data:check -- --file <sample>` validates it without touching the database.
- `docs/real-data-readiness.md`: the first-run checklist (the assumptions the docs only imply), the table of approximations and what to do about each, the gaps in Ringside itself, and the decision gate before any money.

**Found while doing it.**
- **The footer said "fictional, simulated data" on every page, unconditionally.** It would have been false the day real data loaded. It and the Data page's demo note now hang on `isDemoData()` (`lib/seo.ts`). Verified by serving the demo league as a non-demo provider: 20 section pages carry no demo wording, are indexable and have a sitemap; demo mode is unchanged.
- **Ringside cannot say "unknown".** Height, reach, birth year and turned-pro are required numbers, so the adapter fills missing ones (reach from height, then the division median, then 175 cm) and counts them. The honest fix is nullable columns and a dash in the UI; it reaches into many files and is the most important code change before real use.
- Upcoming fights come from a separate endpoint; without it the ledger would have nothing to predict.

**Honest limits.** The adapter has only ever talked to a mock built from the documented example shapes. Its assumptions (auth header names, array-shaped lists, `location` format, event times in UTC, how a draw looks, outcomes beyond the six documented) are listed in the readiness doc to check on the first free-tier run. Event dates come from a UTC instant, so an evening card in the Americas can land a day late, which matters because the ledger grades on the last snapshot strictly before the event date. Nothing was bought, sent or fetched from the vendor.

**Tests:** `tests/boxing-data-api.test.ts` (17, mutation-checked: dropping the storage guard, an off-by-one budget, uncounted PTS, no draw inference, the key in an error message, and a wrong "active" window each fail one) and an `isDemoData` assertion in `tests/i18n.test.ts`.

## 39. Accounts, the leaderboard and community edits (round 18, 2026-10-03)
The last roadmap item that did not need data or a person, built so that it does not need a hosting choice either (`docs/accounts.md` has the design and the operator commands).
- **What exists.** Sign-in by name and password (no email: nothing to verify, nothing personal to lose); server-side pick'em picks that lock when fight day begins; `/leaderboard`; `/contribute` and `/review` for sourced trainer/manager history; an account menu in the header; `npm run accounts` for operators. 22 new tests (`tests/accounts.test.ts`), 338 in all.
- **Accounts in their own file.** `accounts.db` beside `DATABASE_PATH`, because `ringside.db` is rebuilt and deleted at will here. Everything in it refers to bouts and boxers by external id, so a rebuilt sports database orphans nothing. Approved edits are replayed into `team_stints` (source "Community edit", link and quote stored) every time the sports database opens, and vendor re-ingests leave them alone (a test re-runs the ingest to prove it).
- **Decisions.** (1) *No email.* A reset code from the operator replaces "forgot password"; the cost is an operator in the loop, the benefit is no mail service, no verification, no address to leak. (2) *Scoring rule with no way to game it:* a right pick is worth `1 + the model's doubt`, a wrong one 0, so the likelier side is always the best pick on average (tested across the whole range), and the model is scored on the same fights as the bar to beat. (3) *Picks freeze at the start of fight day, deletion included,* or a losing pick could be quietly dropped. (4) *Editors cannot approve their own proposals; admins can;* nobody is an editor until an operator says so. (5) *The server can check a contributor's quote against the live page,* with the research pipeline's polite fetcher, which needed hardening first (below).
- **Found and fixed while building.** The fetcher followed redirects anywhere and trusted a hostname's spelling, which is fine for a researcher's own list and not for addresses strangers type: it now has a strict mode (same-site redirects only, at most three, every host and hop resolved and refused if private) used only for contributions. The overlap warning for a second head trainer never fired (the query lacked its second parameter); a test caught it. `useSyncExternalStore` looped because its server snapshot built a new object each call. Two quick clicks on picks overwrote each other (the second used the first render's picks). The new header link pushed every page past 320 px, in both languages (the site had been clean since round 9), fixed with an icon-only link below `sm` and tighter spacing; axe is 0 on the five new pages in both languages and signed in.
- **Verified:** typecheck, lint, 338 tests, production build, `npm run smoke` 126/126 (the new pages included), the whole flow by hand in the browser (sign up, pick, reload, propose, check the source, approve, see it on the fighter page), axe 0 and no overflow at 320/390. Mutation-checked: same-origin check off, picks never lock, own proposal reviewable, non-http links allowed, private players listed, deletion keeping the user, password change keeping sessions, locked picks deletable, no site-wide hashing budget: each fails a test (the first non-http mutation survived until a test used `ftp:`).
- **Not done / limits:** no CAPTCHA (bulk sign-ups from many addresses are slowed, not stopped); usernames are not moderated (an operator can disable one); limits are in memory per process; the client address comes from `X-Forwarded-For`; no two-factor; the 159 new Arabic strings are mine and unreviewed like the rest; the source check was run live against Wikipedia (a real quote: found; an invented one: missing; the cloud-metadata address and localhost: refused) and that run found the footnote-marker problem, which `checkQuote` now ignores ("Boxing [ b ] is a combat sport" matches a copied "Boxing is a combat sport"), but other sites' markup may still differ from what a person copies; pick'em for fights with no ledger snapshot treats the model as 50/50. Not started: notifications when picks are graded, a public profile page, email.

## 40. Poster contrast, and three home directions to choose from (round 19, 2026-10-03)
Two items left over from the first design pass (DESIGN.md "Still to do").
- **Poster text contrast was never measured; now it is, and it failed.** On some of the six generated colour schemes the poster's header lines were 2.4-2.5:1 (the postponed notice 2.2:1). A dark band behind the header fixes it (5.3:1 or better, both languages). The measurement is a browser run; a test carries the same arithmetic per scheme (`lib/poster-colors.ts`), and removing the band makes it fail at the measured 2.46:1.
- **Three alternative home pages**, built from the real components and data and viewable in both languages at `/design/home?v=a|b|c`: A (the next fight is the page), B (a control-room dashboard), C (the question box with a live answer under it). They are a decision for you, not a change: DESIGN.md forbids changing the look without approval, so `/` is untouched. The lab 404s in a production build unless `DESIGN_LAB=1` (tested) and is not in the sitemap or navigation. All three pass axe in both languages and fit 320 px.
- **Not done:** `/design-review` and `/qa` as a whole (I looked at the event page and the home at 1440 px and nothing was off, which is a spot check, not a review); a licensed-photo poster; no real user has looked at any of the three directions.

## 41. A page-by-page QA sweep, and the lists that could not be browsed (round 20, 2026-10-03)
The last DESIGN.md item, `/qa`, done as measurement rather than impressions.
- **The sweep.** All 59 smoke routes in both languages (118 loads, 1280 px, signed out) checked in the browser for: a broken image, a request answered 4xx/5xx, text spilling out of its box, text under 11.5 px, no heading, "undefined"/NaN/"[object" in the text, page-level horizontal overflow. One finding: the basis glyph in the earnings bars (◐) was wider than its 8 px box (cosmetic; the box is 12 px and centred now). No console errors from the app (the only ones were the dev server's hot-reload socket, caused by loading pages in iframes).
- **Flows tried by hand:** command palette (Ctrl+K, search, Enter opens the fighter), rail toggle and its saved state, Ask the data (submit, table), matchup lab sliders (the probability moves), watchlist (star, then the home strip), scouting report, records filter, matchmaking, language links. All worked.
- **The real finding, from asking "can I reach everything?":** the fighter list stopped at 48 with the note "showing top 48" and no way to see the rest (966 fighters; 63 in welterweight alone), and the events page showed only the 24 latest results, so 700 older cards could only be found through a fighter's page. Both are fixed: `/boxers` pages 48 at a time (filters and the plain-English query are kept across pages; a bad or out-of-range page number lands on a real page) and `/events?year=YYYY` browses a whole year 24 cards at a time with year chips. Shared `Pager` component and `lib/paging.ts` (tested: every result reachable, fractions, NaN, negatives, empty lists); both are in the smoke run. Arabic written by me, unreviewed.
- **Not done / limits:** the sweep is structural, not aesthetic (no one has judged spacing or colour on all 59 pages); `/people` still shows only the top 50 trainers, judges and referees (they are ranked tables, and everyone is reachable from their fighters); signed-in views and the Arabic palette were not swept; Safari and Firefox are untested.

## 42. Running it for real: backups, sessions and the leaderboard under load (round 21, 2026-10-03)
Three things that do not show in a demo but would hurt the first week of a real deployment.
- **Backups of the two files that cannot be rebuilt** (`npm run backup`, `lib/backup.ts`): the sports database (it holds the live prediction ledger) and the accounts database. Snapshots by `VACUUM INTO` while the app writes (a test proves a write that is only in the WAL is included and a later one is not), each copy integrity-checked as it is written, `verify` to check one later (a missing ledger table or a damaged page fails it), rotation that only ever removes folders with the timestamp name, the accounts copy mode 0600. Run against the real dev database: 968 fighters and 46 ledger rows came back, integrity ok. `docs/deploy.md` has the schedule advice and the restore steps; **a restore has not been rehearsed on a real host.**
- **Sessions** are now visible and revocable: a device label ("Chrome on macOS", nothing else of the user agent), last-used (written at most hourly), end one, or end all others; an old accounts file is upgraded on open; `npm run accounts -- check | purge` for the operator.
- **The leaderboard was a hidden cost.** Measured: 0.2 s at 500 players, **1.9 s of blocking work per request at 5,000** players with 100 picks each. It is now cached per language and rebuilt only when the day, the sports data, any pick, or the set of public players changes; the first version of the test missed two of those (a mutation check found it) and now covers all five.
- **A test harness bug found on the way:** the accounts tests had been using the *same file* for the sports and accounts databases (the temp name collided), which hid any mix-up between the two. They have separate files now; nothing broke, but the isolation is real.
- **Not done / limits:** no automatic off-machine copy (the script cannot know where you keep things); no scheduler in the container; the cache lives in one process (as everything else here does); beyond tens of thousands of players the leaderboard needs a database aggregate; the 8 new Arabic strings are mine and unreviewed.

## 43. Browser security headers and a content security policy (round 22, 2026-10-03)
The app set no security headers at all, and since round 18 it has sign-in cookies. Now every response carries `nosniff`, `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, a referrer policy, a permissions policy, `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy`, HSTS (only over https) and no `X-Powered-By`; every page carries a **nonce-based content security policy** built per request in `proxy.ts` (`lib/security.ts`, `docs/security.md`).
- **Scripts:** nonce plus `strict-dynamic`, never `unsafe-inline` or `unsafe-eval` in production. The one inline script of our own (the rail's pre-paint state) takes the nonce from the layout; JSON-LD is data. **Styles:** this origin plus the nonce, with `style-src-attr 'unsafe-inline'` because charts and posters use `style=""` (an attribute cannot carry a nonce): the one real concession, with `img-src https:` for licensed photos.
- **Proven, not assumed:** on a production build in a browser the site hydrates and works (rail, client navigation, command palette, sign-up, 95 images, 6 fonts) with no violation, and the policy really is enforced (an injected `<style>` and an inline `onerror` were blocked). `npm run smoke` now fails on any page whose policy lacks this response's nonce, whose inline script lacks it, or that has an inline handler, and on any response missing a standing header: 134/134. 7 new tests, nine mutations each caught.
- **Found on the way:** Next's development error overlay writes un-nonced inline styles, which flooded the console with 33 violations the first time; development now allows inline styles (and `unsafe-eval`, websockets), production does not. `npm audit` is clean for production; the 5 high advisories in the full tree are one linter-only chain (`braces` via `eslint-config-next`), documented and not "fixed" by a downgrade.
- **Not done / limits:** Chrome only; no CSP report endpoint; no third-party scanner run; no `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy`; HSTS without `includeSubDomains`/`preload` by choice; any future third-party script, font or embed needs a deliberate policy change (documented).

## 44. Measured performance, and 215 pointless requests (round 23, 2026-10-03)
A round that started by measuring rather than fixing. On a production build, in a browser, on the demo league: TTFB 13-26 ms, first paint 72-140 ms, **layout shift 0**, long-task time 0, HTML 12-47 KB gzipped, about 220 KB of client JavaScript for the whole site. Server time per page 5-17 ms for 22 page and API routes (none an outlier); about 105 pages a second on one core, flat from 10 to 50 concurrent, 0 failures in 12,000 requests, memory settling at about 600 MB (it does not leak; the same run needed only a 160 MB heap, so the rest is uncollected garbage). Nothing there needed fixing; it is written down in `docs/deploy.md` as sizing advice (give the container at least 768 MB; the heap that is really needed grows with the data, not the traffic).
- **The one finding:** the style map made **250 requests, 215 of them `?_rsc=` prefetches** (110 KB, each answered by our own server) because `next/link` prefetches every link that scrolls into view and the map has 515. Every page here is rendered per request and there is no `loading.tsx`, so a prefetch fetches only an empty shell: it bought nothing. `components/L.tsx` now defaults to `prefetch={false}`: the same page makes 35 requests, none of them prefetches, and clicking a link still navigates (checked). A test fails if the default is removed, or if a `loading.tsx` is ever added (then prefetching has something to fetch and the default should be revisited).
- **`npm run loadtest`** (`scripts/loadtest.ts`, `lib/loadtest.ts`) repeats the measurement against any server you own: concurrency, req/s, p50/p95/p99, and `--each` for every page alone. The percentile maths is tested (a mutation of floor to ceil is caught by the seven-sample case).
- **A consequence of round 22, noted:** the new `X-Frame-Options: DENY` stops a page being loaded in an iframe, so the iframe-based sweeps used in earlier rounds (axe, reflow, QA) no longer work against a build with the headers; they navigate the pane page by page instead (slower), or run against a build without them.
- **Not done / limits:** all of this is localhost with no network in between and no throttled CPU (a phone on a slow connection will be several times slower than 140 ms); Chrome only; one dataset size (the 20x run was not repeated); no real-user monitoring; the 600 MB figure is for this dataset and this machine.

## 45. The backfill runbook: resumable, priced before it is spent (round 23, 2026-10-03)

**Why.** The adapter (§38) can map a feed; it could not yet *run* one: a full history is thousands of requests, a failure partway lost everything, and nothing kept the league current. This is the procedure and the tooling for the day a plan with full history is bought. As first written, nothing here ran until the vendor confirmed storage in writing; **by the owner's decision the default is now to store provisionally** (see the update below), and none of it has run against the real API.

**What was built.**
- **`npm run vendor:backfill`**, four modes: `--plan` (fetch the fight list in memory and say what the fighters will cost; writes nothing, needs no confirmation), `--check` (fetch into the cache, validate, print the report, touch nothing), the load (fetch, validate strictly, back up, load in one transaction, recompute ratings) and `--update` (the daily job).
- **A read-through cache** of every successful response (`lib/providers/boxing-data-api.ts`, `cacheDir`): a crashed or interrupted run costs only the requests that never completed; a finished one costs none, so a mapping fix is re-run for free. Only a good answer is kept (a refusal or an error body never is), writes are atomic, and a damaged file is fetched again.
- **Retries** with Retry-After or 1, 2, 4 ... 30 s back-off on a 429, a 500/502/503/504 or a dropped connection; a refusal (403) is never retried; a retry spends the request budget like any request.
- **Safety refusals:** the storage confirmation is checked before anything is created; a database that holds other fighters (the demo league) is refused unless `--into-existing`; a load with any fighter missing is refused (re-run to retry only those); a validator error stops the load; a backup is taken before any load into a database with data (the last 14 kept).
- **The app no longer starts a paid fetch on a page view.** An empty database with `BOXING_PROVIDER=licensed` now refuses to seed and names `vendor:backfill` (`lib/db.ts`). Before, the first request (or the health check) would have started the fetch.
- **The daily update** asks for the fights since the latest card in the database (less 14 days), fetches everything in its window fresh (a cached list page or career record predates today's results), and replaces "no result yet" with the result; a running app notices the change itself (its world rebuilds when another process commits).
- `docs/real-data-runbook.md`: the ordered procedure, the checks to make by hand, the cron line, what each error means, undoing it, and what is stored where.

**Tests** (all new, 3 files): `tests/boxing-data-api.test.ts` grew to 33 (resume, cache integrity, retries and back-off, the plan); `tests/vendor-backfill.test.ts` (3: the foreign-database check, the update window, the plan text); `tests/db-licensed-guard.test.ts` (2); and `tests/vendor-backfill-cli.test.ts` (11) **runs the real command end to end against a local HTTP server standing in for the vendor**, with a real database file: plan, refusal, check, load, reload, the wrong-database guard and backup, a failed fighter and the resume, the daily update picking up a late result, an update on an empty database, and a wrong key. Mutation-checked: an ignored cache, retried refusals, an uncapped wait, free retries, cached error bodies, the foreign-database guard, a late confirmation check, cached lists on update, an allowed incomplete load, and no empty-database guard each fail a test. The end-to-end test found two real flaws while it was being written (an empty database file left behind by a refused run; two backups in the same second colliding).

**Honest limits.** Not run against the real API. The plan prints a time from a guessed 250 ms round trip. The cache is the vendor's data on disk, which the storage terms must allow. A daily update costs one request per fighter in its window. A feed whose history is shallow gives shallow ratings whatever the tooling does.

**Update, same day: storing is on by default (the owner's decision).** The vendor has not answered the question of whether a stored backfill may be kept, and the owner chose to go ahead provisionally rather than wait. So `BOXING_API_STORAGE_CONFIRMED` is no longer a gate: **unset means store, with a warning on every run** ("storing PROVISIONALLY ... if it says no, delete the cache and the database"); `1` records that the vendor agreed in writing and silences the warning; `0` refuses to store (a sample can still be evaluated). Nothing else was loosened: the budget, the wrong-database refusal, the completeness gate, the validator gate, the backup and the empty-database guard are unchanged. The real risk is the vendor's terms, not the code: if it says no, the vendor's data must be deleted, which is why the real league has its own database file and cache directory (`docs/real-data-runbook.md`, "Undoing it"), and why the cache and backups are gitignored.

**Update, same day: what the load can and cannot promise.** Asked to store only data that could be confidently confirmed as correct. The honest answer is that no code can confirm a vendor's facts; it can check whether the feed agrees with itself. The vendor gives each fighter's career record (`stats`), the adapter now keeps it (`vendorRecords()`), and `lib/vendor-verify.ts` compares it with the loaded fights: **complete** (they add up exactly), **partial** (fights missing: the site would show a shorter record than the fighter has, a false statement), **conflict** (more than the vendor's own total: the feed contradicts itself). A load is refused unless 90% of fighters are complete and none conflict (`--min-complete`, `--allow-partial`, `--allow-conflicts`); `--check` prints the same and exits 1 if a load would be refused; the daily update audits the careers in the database afterwards. This makes the free plan unloadable as a league (its window of a few weeks leaves nearly everyone partial), which is correct. The audit also caught a real flaw in the update on its first run: cached fighter records predate a fight, so every fighter who had just fought looked like a contradiction. The update now fetches its window fresh. Tests: `tests/vendor-verify.test.ts` (4) and two end-to-end scenarios in `tests/vendor-backfill-cli.test.ts` (a short career refused and then loaded on a lower bar; a contradiction refused even with `--allow-partial`). Mutation-checked: a disabled gate, an update that reuses the cache, `--allow-partial` allowing conflicts, and a conflict classed as partial each fail a test. What this still cannot do: say a winner, a date or a method is right. Two systems agreeing is evidence, not proof.

## 46. Coverage, and what the gaps hid (round 24, 2026-10-03)
Started from a coverage run (Node's built-in: lib and app/api): 97.08% of lines, 89.49% of branches, and only three files under 80%: `weights.ts` (69%), `analytics.ts` (75%), `ask/tools.ts` (77%). Writing tests for those, with expected values worked out by hand from a table in the test (not copied from the code), found four real bugs; a fifth class of bug came from asking what states the real world passes through that the demo never does.
- **Weight-gap buckets were not mirror images.** `fightNightEdge` cut the signed gap at -8, -4, -1.5, 1.5, 4, 8, so a gap of exactly 8 lb put one fighter in "8+ heavier" and the other in "4-8 lighter": the two sides of one fight landed in rows that should have matched. Now bucketed by size, then side; a test asserts every "lighter" row holds as many fighters as its "heavier" mirror.
- **A no-contest ended a win streak** in the longest-streaks table (win, NC, win = 2), though the fighter's record and current streak both ignore no-contests (= 3). Fixed to match.
- **The home page returned a 500 when nothing was upcoming** (between seasons, or before the first fixtures load), **and the style map when nobody had eight bouts**; `overview` gave NaN for a league with no bouts, and the map divided by zero for one fighter. All fixed; the home page now says "No upcoming fights are scheduled yet." (Arabic written by me, unreviewed).
- **How they were found:** two leagues the real world passes through, an empty one and one finished card with nothing upcoming, run through every aggregate, every per-fighter/bout/card view and every Ask-the-data tool (`tests/empty-league.test.ts`, `tests/sparse-league.test.ts`, with a NaN walker, since JSON hides NaN as null), and through the whole site (`npm run smoke -- --feed sparse|empty`, now in CI: 95 and 68 pages in both languages).
- **Pinned, not changed:** `missCount` includes the weigh-in for the coming fight while the missed-weights list, history and division averages do not. That is intended (it is what the upset-watch "has missed weight" warning is about) and a test now says so.
- **New tests:** `league-stats` (5: weights, edge buckets, misses, overview, finish heat, countries, stance, reach, streaks, upsets, on a hand-made 7-fighter league), `ask-tools` (2: every tool, every argument alone, both languages, over 300 runs), `empty-league` and `sparse-league` (3 each), `style-map` (4). 418 tests. Coverage after: 98.64% of lines, 90.88% of branches; the three files are now at 98% (weights), 99% (analytics) and 98% (ask tools); only `warm.ts` is under 90%. Fifteen mutations were made on purpose; one survived the first draft (upcoming misses listed) and a second test case was added.
- **Not done / limits:** coverage covers `lib` and the API routes; pages and components are covered only by the smoke run (no unit coverage of `.tsx`); line coverage says a line ran, not that its result was checked; the sparse and empty leagues are tiny and artificial, so a league that is large but has some other gap (no weigh-ins at all, no officials, no punches) is covered only by the demo's partial gaps; the Ask-the-data tools are checked for being well formed (a sentence, rectangular tables, site links, no junk), not for giving the *right* ranking, which the demo-league tests only partly pin.

## 47. When something breaks: error pages, an error log, and an Arabic bug found on the way (round 25, 2026-10-03)
Round 24 found pages that returned a 500; this round makes sure a failure is handled and recorded rather than a bare framework screen, and in doing so found a worse bug in something already shipped.
- **Error pages.** `app/[locale]/error.tsx` (inside the layout, so navigation and language stay) and `app/global-error.tsx` (when the root layout fails: its own document, both languages): "Down for the count", Try again, back to the ring, and a **reference number** that is Next's digest of the error. Nothing of the error itself is ever shown (a test fails if either file reads `.message`, `.stack` or logs). Checked on a production build with a deliberately failing page and a failing layout (temporary, removed): the reference on screen matched the log line's digest each time, the page held no trace of the secret-looking text in the error, and axe is 0 in both languages.
- **An error log.** `onRequestError` in `instrumentation.ts` writes one JSON line per server error (`lib/error-log.ts`): time, method, path **without the query string**, route, digest, message cut at 500 characters, six stack lines; never headers, cookies or bodies. Anything can be thrown (strings, objects, a circular object, nothing) and a line is still written. Seven mutations (query logged, headers logged, message or stack uncut, digest dropped, reference always shown, the message shown as the reference) each fail a test.
- **The bug: the Arabic site showed English in every client component added since round 18.** `i18n/client-keys.json` (the list of strings sent to the browser) had not been regenerated for four rounds, and nothing noticed because every check looked at the *dictionary*, which was complete: account forms, sign-in, sessions, the pick'em messages and pager, contribute, review all rendered in English under an Arabic page heading. Found because the new error page, rendered in Arabic on a production build, was in English. Regenerated (`npm run i18n:extract`; +112 shipped strings), and a test now fails if either key file is stale or if any client string's Arabic is not in what `clientDict("ar")` sends; I confirmed Arabic text on the Arabic home, picks, account, contribute and review pages in a browser. **This had been live since round 18** (anything pushed since then on `main` had it).
- **Process lesson, kept:** my Arabic checks on earlier rounds looked at headings (server-rendered) and axe, not at the client-rendered text; a check that renders a client component with the *shipped* dictionary (as the error-page test now does) is what catches this.
- **Not done / limits:** no error reporting service (the log line is for a person or a log shipper to read); a client-side error that happens after hydration is shown but not logged on the server (Next logs only server errors); API routes still return Next's default 500 body, not JSON; the global error page cannot be translated beyond its two fixed languages; the error text is mine in Arabic and unreviewed.

## 48. A career total that trails yesterday's result (round 24, 2026-10-03)

- **What the first real run showed:** `--check` on the free plan reported one CONFLICT, a fighter with a loss in the loaded fights and 17-0-0 in the vendor's record. `npm run vendor:explain -- --name Acosta` (a read-only look at the cache: the vendor's record, the raw winner flags and result of each cached fight, what the loader counts) showed a finished fight on the 27th with consistent flags and a UD, and a fighter record whose `updated_at` was the 29th and still lacked it. The mapping was right; the vendor's total trailed its own result. A wrong winner flag would look the same, which only a primary source can settle.
- **Decision (owner):** in the **daily update's audit only**, a surplus that disappears when the fights of the last 7 days (`VENDOR_LAG_DAYS`) are set aside is reported as `lagging` ("probably lagging; the next day's update shows whether the total caught up"), not as a conflict. A surplus older fights cause, or more than the last week's fights explain, is still a conflict. A first load and `--check` stay strict: `reconcileFeed` has no allowance.
- **Tests:** unit (reconcileDb with and without the allowance: lagging, not fully explained, the window moved past the fight, complete and partial untouched, the feed check strict) and end to end (`--update`: a trailing total is lagging, an old-fight contradiction is a CONFLICT, exit code 0 either way). Two mutations (every fight counts as recent; the allowance switched off) each fail tests.
- **Limit:** a lagging total is an assumption, not a finding. If the total has not caught up after a few days, the audit will keep saying "lagging" for as long as the fight is inside the window and then call it a conflict; read the log.

## 49. A check for English left on the Arabic site (round 26, 2026-10-03)
Round 25's bug (client strings in English on the Arabic site) got through because no check ever looked at what an Arabic page *says*. This adds one: `arabicLeaks` (lib/smoke.ts), run by `npm run smoke` on the HTML of all 67 Arabic pages of the demo league. It flags any Latin word of three letters or more, one block of text at a time (so "968 boxers" among Arabic and digits is caught, which a "run of words" rule missed), in the text, the title, the description and the attributes a screen reader announces. Allowed: the brand and Elo, units and short capitals, other organisations' names (BoxRec, Wikidata, CompuBox ...), the demo provider's own source label, and anything inside an element marked `lang="en"` (removed whole, nested elements of the same name included).
- **What it found** (after filtering the noise from its first, looser draft): the belt name on a bout page (`GBC` link followed by "World Title" raw, and "World Title (vacant)"), and the whole "Last data load" panel on `/data`: the loader's own names for what it counted ("968 boxers · 618 events ..."), the kinds of row it dropped, and its validator messages. Fixed: belts through `t.name()`, counts and kinds through a translated label table (25 new Arabic strings, mine, unreviewed), validator messages and codes marked `lang="en"` (they are English by nature and a screen reader should say them in English), and the wordmark too.
- **A side effect worth having:** marking English-by-nature text with `lang="en"` is what lets a screen reader on the Arabic site pronounce it properly instead of reading Latin letters with Arabic rules.
- **Honest limits:** it reads the HTML the server sends, so it sees client components' first render but not text that appears after the browser loads (account forms once signed in, a fetched list); that class is covered by the key-file test from round 25, not by this. It is built for the demo league (whose names are all transliterated); a real feed's untransliterated names would be flagged, so it is skipped for `--feed` runs and would need a name allowance before it can gate a real deployment. Three-letter English words that are also Arabic-script-adjacent abbreviations are not distinguished from leaks beyond the allow-list. 5 tests, eight mutations caught (one needed an extra case: an English element's own attributes).

## 50. Your place on the board, and what was graded since you looked (round 27, 2026-10-03)
Accounts gave people picks and a leaderboard but no answer to the obvious questions: where am I, and what happened to the fights I picked? `/picks` now has a card for each (signed in only; `components/PickStanding.tsx`, `GET /api/account/standing`).
- **Your place:** "#4 of 31 players", or how many more graded picks are needed to be ranked, with points, points a pick and, where the model called at least three of the same fights, the model's score on those fights. It is the person's own: someone hidden from the public board still sees where their score would put them, and is told only they see it. `rankIn` gives one more than the ranked players strictly ahead (equal points and accuracy share a place, as on the board); a test pins that a person's own place equals their row on the board, and that a hidden person's score takes the place it would on it.
- **The recap:** right and wrong counts and the five newest graded fights since they last looked, with "Got it". Kept by a mark rather than a clock (`users.picks_seen_through`, migrated on open): the date of the latest graded fight shown. The recap is the graded picks after the mark, so each fight is reported once, a result that arrives late still is, and the mark cannot move backwards or take a junk date. The alternative I rejected was "since the last visit time": a result posted after you left on fight day, or a visit the same day, would be missed or repeated.
- **Checked:** type check, lint, 459+ tests (5 new: place semantics, agreement with the board, hidden people, recap lifecycle, the endpoint in English and Arabic including fight names in the language asked for, the migration), eleven mutations on the new code each fail a test; in a browser, signed in with graded picks, English and Arabic (including the dual form "اختياران"), axe 0, no overflow at 320 px, dismissal persists across reloads.
- **Not done / limits:** the card is on `/picks` only (not the home page or the account menu); the recap is a list of results, not a notification (nothing is sent anywhere, there is no email); the model comparison needs at least three shared fights to show; the Arabic is mine and unreviewed; the standing is computed per request from the cached board, which is cheap here but would want its own cache at tens of thousands of players.


## 51. A configuration doctor, and a test that keeps the settings documented (round 28, 2026-10-03)
After the code, the next thing that fails a deployment is the settings around it: a misspelt name that is silently ignored, a `SITE_URL` that is `http://`, a backup that stopped running last week, an `accounts.db` anyone on the machine can read. This round adds a way to ask "will this do what I think it will?" before the first visitor, and fixes a drift between what the code reads and what the files say.
- **`npm run doctor`** (`lib/doctor.ts`, `scripts/doctor.ts`): some fifty distinct findings in two layers, settings only (`envFindings`) and settings plus the files they point to (`fileFindings`, through an injectable `Probe` so every disk state is a unit test, not a staged directory). Each finding is `fail` / `warn` / `info` / `ok` with the fix beside it; exit 1 on any failure, `--strict` for warnings, `--json`, `--production` (the shell you run it from rarely has `NODE_ENV=production`). Never prints a secret: key findings say missing / short / odd, and the log line carries only a `SITE_URL`'s origin (a password pasted into the URL cannot reach a log; tested).
- **Start-up log:** one `{"event":"config"}` JSON line and one `config_problem` line per setting problem, from `instrumentation.ts`. Settings only, no disk, so no start-up cost.
- **Misspelt names** are matched case-insensitively within edit distance 2 of a setting the code reads ("Did you mean SITE_URL?"). My first draft warned about any unknown variable with a familiar prefix and flagged `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` and `AI_AGENT` (other tools' variables) on the first run on this machine; a doctor that cries wolf gets ignored, so it reports near misses only.
- **The drift this found:** `tests/config-docs.test.ts` scans the source for every setting read (`process.env.X`, `process.env["X"]`, `envNum("X")`) and requires it to be in `KNOWN_ENV` (or the short `INTERNAL_ENV` list of test hooks), in `.env.example`, and read by something (no stale entries). Seven settings were read by code and absent from `.env.example`: four public ones now added (`DESIGN_LAB`, `VENDOR_LAG_DAYS`, `WIKIMEDIA_GAP_MS`, `WIKIDATA_GAP_MS`) and three marked internal (`I18N_DIR`, `REVIEW_OUT`, `RINGSIDE_NO_SEED`). (My own audit before writing the test said fourteen; seven of those were already documented. The test, not my grep, is the reliable count.) The test's first run also found a problem the audit had not: `BOXING_API_STORAGE_CONFIRMED` was described in `.env.example` without an `=`, so it could not be uncommented as a line. It also checks that copying `.env.example` turns nothing on (the only live values are an empty key and `BOXING_PROVIDER=demo`) and that the docs name no setting one letter away from a real one.
- **Checked:** type check, lint, 21 new tests (every threshold at its boundary: Node 22.4 vs 22.5, backup 47 vs 49 hours, disk 99 vs 100 vs 499 vs 500 MB, accounts file mode 600/640/644/700; and the CLI end to end on real SQLite files: good, loose permissions, `--strict`, a text file that is not a database, a foreign database). Ten deliberate breaks of the code each failed a test; the one that survived was a guard for platform variables that could never match anything, which I deleted rather than keep.
- **Not done / limits:** the doctor reads the process's environment and the files; it cannot tell whether a key is *valid* (that needs a network call to the vendor, deliberately not made) or whether the volume is really persistent (a container that loses `/data` looks like a fresh install). It does not run on a schedule or alert; wire `npm run doctor -- --production --strict` into the deploy script or a cron if you want that.

## 52. Title reigns, enquiries, and what California really publishes (round 25, 2026-10-03)

- **Asked for:** title reigns from Wikipedia, California purses and gates, FightFax, UK suspensions and Barb audiences. This round did the first three as far as they can go without the owner; the rest is listed below.
- **Title reigns** (`docs/title-reigns.md`): `npm run champions:import` reads the men's WBA, WBC, IBF and WBO lists (CC BY-SA 4.0, credited on the fighter page), stores 2,600 or so reigns in `title_reigns`, and links a reign to a fighter only through a Wikidata ID. The parser was built on the real pages (saved as test fixtures) and tested on all four layouts, and it found things a guess would have missed: citation templates that run over several lines (they split a note into cells), a page that numbers two consecutive reigns "4", rows with no number cell, status labels beside names (`– Super champion`), US-style and partial dates, and typos in the page itself (`No` for Nov, `Ma` for Mar or May), which are refused and counted, never guessed. Four mutations (no year-fill, no multi-line-citation guard, no ambiguity guard, a lone date accepted) were each caught by a test. A fighter's page shows "Title history" with the source page linked; the data page counts how many fighters have a reign.
- **Wikimedia rate-limits the shared address of this cloud container** (429 for minutes at a time), so the live importer should be run from the owner's machine. The fetcher waits out a rate limit (Retry-After first), caches every page, and uses `WIKIMEDIA_CONTACT` as its identity (never a contact baked into the code).
- **California purses are not online** (`docs/csac-purses.md`): CSAC's Events page says event results are no longer posted and points boxing to BoxRec (never ours to use). The route is a public-records request, drafted. The survey's CSAC row is corrected.
- **A gap this found, not yet closed:** the research checker confirms a claim by fetching a web page and finding the quote; a PDF that arrives by email has no page. Until the checker can verify a quote against a local document (by quote and hash), commission documents cannot reach "verified" by code; they stay `unconfirmed` unless a person decides.
- **Enquiries drafted, to be sent by the owner:** `docs/tbrb-enquiry.md` (lineage and rankings history, credit and link) and `docs/fightfax-enquiry.md` (records, suspensions, a licence). Nothing from either is used before a written yes.
- **Not done:** UK suspensions (BBBofC notices) and Barb audiences; women's champion lists; the local-document checker; cross-checking Wikipedia's reigns against `lib/lineage.ts`.

## 53. On this day (round 29, 2026-10-03)
A history page for a database that is mostly history: `/on-this-day` shows the fights decided and the fighters born on a calendar date, from every year the database covers. Today by default; `?d=MM-DD` for any other day (previous and next day links, a "Today" link, and, on a day with nothing, the nearest earlier and later days that have something and how much). It is in the left rail under Fights, ⌘K, the sitemap (the default page only: the other 365 are `noindex` so they cannot compete with each other), the smoke routes and the loadtest paths. Home is unchanged.
- **Rules** (`lib/on-this-day.ts`, written down in its header): a fight counts only if it is completed, has a result, and is dated on or before today (a cancelled bout with a result entered, and a result dated in the future, are both bad data and both left out); a no contest is a result and is shown. A birthday counts only on an exact `birthDate` on or before today; a year-only fighter and an impossible date such as 1990-02-29 are left out. The day of the year is worked out in a leap year, so 29 February is its own day and is never moved to the 28th; stepping a day wraps the 366-day year and passes through 29 February.
- **Which fights to show when a day has dozens:** title fights first, then the fight score (the same 0-100 as Fight of the year), then the ratings going in; shown newest year first; at most three from any one year on the first pass, and spare places filled afterwards. The cap exists because the demo league's first draft showed four fights from one 2026 card at the top of March 6 (80 fights that day): a history page should span the years. The numbers (12 fights, 12 birthdays, 3 a year, a 40-point title bonus) are my judgment, written as constants.
- **A 404 for a date that does not exist** (`?d=02-30`, `?d=banana`, a repeated `d`): a quietly different page would hide a typo in a link.
- **Checked:** type check, lint, 13 new tests on a league worked out by hand (the table is at the top of `tests/on-this-day.test.ts`: results on the day, today's fight in, upcoming/cancelled/future out, birthdays and the three kinds that are skipped, the leap day, the crowded-day cut, the per-year cap and its fill, empty days and wrap-around in both directions, an empty database); 497 tests pass; smoke 144/144 on the full, sparse and empty feeds (including the busiest day, an empty day, the leap day and `?d=02-30`); axe 0 violations and no overflow at 375 px in English and Arabic on four variants; the Arabic page read through. Eighteen deliberate breaks of the code each failed a test. Two survivors on the first pass were informative: a filter that was redundant (a bout with a result is never "upcoming", so I removed it) and a count that my data could not tell apart (every fight was in its own year), which led to the per-year test data.
- **Arabic is mine and unreviewed** (22 new entries, including plural forms for "birthdays" and "fighters born"); it joins the list that needs a native reader (`docs/arabic-review.md`). "{n} من السنوات المختلفة" is a compromise for number-noun agreement.
- **Not done / limits:** it ranks with `scoreFight`, which at about 160,000 bouts builds the whole punch-statistics table once (about a second, shared with the Fight of the year pages) the first time any day is opened after a restart, so the first visitor to this page after a deploy can wait that long; it is not in `warm.ts`. Nothing on the home page links to it (the home look is awaiting your decision). No upcoming-anniversary or "this week" view, no deaths (the database holds no death dates), and a fighter's birthday is a link, not a story: there is no copy beyond names, years and records.

## 54. Evidence that is a file (round 25, 2026-10-03)

- **The gap:** the research checker confirmed a claim by fetching its web page and finding the quote. A purse report that arrives by email after a public-records request has no page, so the best sources (commission documents) could not reach "verified" by code.
- **What was built** (`lib/research/documents.ts`, `docs/research.md`): a person registers a document once (`npm run research -- add-document`): its SHA-256, who issued it, when and how it was received, and whether it is an official record. A claim then says `"document": "name"` instead of a URL, and `check` reads the document's text (a PDF through `pdftotext`) and requires the quote and its numbers, with no network and no contact needed. A document that is unregistered, missing or **changed since registration** makes the claim `unconfirmed`.
- **Who can say "official":** only the person registering it. A claim cannot declare its own evidence official; a transcription (typed from a scan) can never be official, even if the manifest is hand-edited to say so (found by a mutation that survived the first tests: registration refused it, but the checker's own guard had no test). Promotion keeps the document's own basis (`disclosed` only if registered official, else `reported`) and puts its name and hash prefix in the money row's note; no web address is invented.
- **What it proves and does not:** the quote is in the document the owner registered and the document is unchanged. Not that the document is genuine: that rests on the person who got it from the issuer.
- **Tests:** 12 (`tests/research-documents.test.ts`): registering and refusing, PDF text with an injected extractor and with a real generated PDF (skipped where poppler is missing), tamper, the checker's statuses, promotion, and the command end to end with no network. Six mutations (no file hash check, no text hash check, official from the host and not the manifest, official transcriptions registrable, the checker's transcription guard, path traversal) each fail a test.
- **Limits:** the extractor is poppler's `pdftotext` (a system tool; a scan needs a person to transcribe it); the checker trusts the manifest, which is a file a person can edit, so the manifest is committed and reviewed like code.

## 55. The home page: directions A and C combined (round 30, 2026-10-03)
You chose "a combination of A and C" from the three directions in the design lab (§40), so the live home page now has A's hero (the next fight is the page: large poster, the matchup as the `h1`, probability bar, the two model chips, preview and breakdown buttons) and, directly under it, C's block (a large question box that goes to `/ask`, five example questions, and a real answer under them), followed by "Coming up" beside the pick'em and every section that was already there. The layout is written down in DESIGN.md's decisions log.
- **The answer on the home page is a teaser**, not the `/ask` page: `AskResults` gained a `compact` mode (the answer, the first table cut to five rows with "Showing 5 of 10.", and a link to the full answer; no "How this was answered" panel). In the lab C showed the whole thing, and on the real page ten table rows pushed the calendar and the pick'em a screen and a half down, which I saw in a screenshot before changing it. `/ask` is unchanged.
- **Cost of the live answer:** it is the first example question, so it is the same question for every visitor and is cached per language per day by `askData`; with an API key that is at most two model calls per language per day (inside the same daily budget and per-visitor limits as everything else), and without a key it is rules only. A refused or failed model call falls back to the rule-based answer, which is not cached, so a busy day on a spent budget costs lookups, not calls. If the league cannot answer the question (an empty one) the block shows only the box and the examples.
- **What was removed to make room:** the hero's fighter-search box (the header search and ⌘K remain, and `/boxers` still has its own), the separate "Next main event" card with headshots (its two model chips moved into the hero), and the line "Want an answer rather than a list?". Seven now-unused Arabic entries were deleted from `i18n/ar.json`. Between seasons (no fixture) the hero is the old brand line with the notice, no poster, and the page still has one `h1` (the smoke run on the empty and sparse feeds covers it).
- **Two bugs the new smoke/leak checks caught in my own first build, not in the lab:** the venue in the hero eyebrow was the raw English name on the Arabic page (the poster beside it showed it in Arabic; fixed with `t.name`), and the "How this was answered" query list (`record_list(...)`) is English by nature and now says so (`lang="en"`); it was never visible before because `/ar/ask` shows no answer until someone asks. A check was added to the smoke run for the home page itself: exactly one `h1`, an ask section, and a question box that goes to `/ask` (with the `/ar` prefix in Arabic), with a unit test that each of those can fail.
- **Checked:** type check, lint, 514 tests, smoke 144/144 on the full, sparse and empty feeds, axe 0 violations and no overflow at 375 px in both languages (320 px: no overflow), the page read through in screenshots at desktop and phone width in English and Arabic.
- **Not done / limits:** the design lab (`app/[locale]/design`) is still there, development only, and its copy of A and C still has the raw-venue bug; delete it once you have approved the real home. No real user has looked at the combination. The Arabic home was checked at phone width only. The first visitor of the day waits for the example answer to be computed (a few milliseconds from rules, longer if a model is used).

## 56. Rehearsing the first real load, and the 10,000-fight wall (round 31, 2026-10-03)

- **Why:** the first load of a full history is the most expensive step (about 20,000 requests on a paid plan) and had only ever been run on 40 fighters. `npm run vendor:rehearse` runs the real `vendor:backfill` at full size (19,000 fighters, 160,000 fights) against a stand-in vendor (`lib/vendor-mock.ts`, the real answer shapes), then kills a fetch part way and runs it again. All eleven checks pass; the numbers are in `docs/real-data-runbook.md`.
