- **What it found:** the vendor's docs limit page numbers to 10,000 documents, and the adapter walked `page_num` from 1 to `total_pages` with no date windows. Against a stand-in that refuses past the limit the load died with an error; against one that returns empty pages it **silently stopped at the newest 10,000 fights** (the record check would have refused the load, but there would have been no way to load it). Fixed: a list longer than a page number can reach is read in date windows, split in two until each fits. A plan with a short history never sends a date range, so the free plan is read as before. Tested both ways the real API might behave and with `total_pages` true and capped, with the old adapter failing all of them first. What the real API does past the limit is still unknown.
- **Stale data made visible:** `/api/health` now reports `data.updatedAt`, `data.ageHours` and `data.stale` (a licensed feed not updated for two days), and `npm run doctor` warns (`stale-data`). A stale feed never makes health a 503: it is a reason to look at the cron, not to restart the container. The contract test that pinned the health keys was changed on purpose.
- **Mutations:** six (never window, never split a window, no stop at the reachable page, stale after four days, money runs counted as updates, no run treated as not stale) were each caught by a test.
- **Not shown by the rehearsal:** messy data (the stand-in's career records all add up), rate limiting, and the real API's behaviour past the limit. It proves the plumbing holds at size, not that the vendor's data will be clean.

## 57. The first visitor: a warm-up that was never seen by the pages (round 31, 2026-10-03)
Rounds 27-30 added pages and a home-page answer, so I measured what a visitor who arrives right after a deploy waits for, at 20x (159,000 bouts), restarting the server for each of 36 pages. 14 of them cost a first visitor 300 ms or more beyond the usual time, up to 948 ms (upset watch), and the 36 penalties added up to 9.0 s.
- **The bug under it:** `lib/warm.ts` computed the fight-of-the-year scores, the upset record and the trainer fit at start-up, but the upset-watch page then spent 879 ms recomputing them. Next compiles `instrumentation.ts` and the pages as separate bundles, each with its own copy of `lib/memo.ts`, and the memo cache was a module variable, so the pages never saw what the warm-up had computed (the world is shared through `globalThis`, its aggregates were not). It went unnoticed because nothing logged what a request computes; I did not look up when the warm-up was added, so I do not know for how long it has been wasted. The cache is now on `globalThis`; a test loads the module twice (two query strings = two instances, as in Next) and requires the second to see the first's result.
- **The warm-up now covers what the slow pages need** (`WARM_STEPS`, each its own try, timed, a failure is logged and skipped): the all-time lists at the two sizes the all-time page and the fighter pages use, the trainer study, the track record, organisations and corners, analytics, money, weigh-ins, lineages, today on "on this day", and the data coverage table. Each calls the same function with the same arguments as its page, because the cache key includes them (a test lists the keys the slow pages asked for when measured and requires each to be present after warming; the money page's `topEarners(w, 8, year)` and the home page's windowed upset are in it).
- **A second, smaller bug found on the way:** the home page passed a spread copy of the world (`{ ...w, bouts: filtered }`) to `biggestUpsets`, a new object every request, so its cache was never hit (10 ms a request, every request). It now uses the function's `since` argument; a test fails if any `{ ...w }` appears in `lib`, `app` or `components` again.
- **Result** (`npm run coldcheck`, same machine, same 36 pages): 0 of 36 pages with a first visit 300 ms or more slower; slowest first visit 151 ms (was 948); penalties 2.2 s in total (was 9.0). The price is start-up: the server accepts requests after 9.1 s instead of 6.2 s at this size (at the demo size the warm-up is a few milliseconds). Memory is unchanged: 1,655 MB idle against 1,625 MB, and 1,700 MB against 1,750 MB after 14 pages, because nothing is computed twice any more.
- **New tools:** `npm run coldcheck` (restarts a production server per page and prints the first visit against the usual one, flagging 300 ms or more; `--shared` for one long-lived server) and `RINGSIDE_MEMO_LOG=1` (which aggregates a request computed, and for how long). A production server now also logs `world_built` (duration, new key, previous key) whenever it builds the world.
- **Checked:** type check, lint, 532 tests (6 new: the memo cache across bundle copies, the warm-up keys, warming a sparse and an empty league without a failing step, a failing step not stopping the others, the cold-cost arithmetic, no spread worlds), smoke 144/144 on the three feeds. Ten deliberate breaks of the warm-up, the cache and the home page each failed a test; two survivors on the way (a step that scored every year of fights, and a separate best-fights step, both redundant because the all-time lists already do it) were deleted rather than kept.
- **Not done / limits:** the numbers are one machine (an Apple laptop, a Node production server, no network between the client and the server) and one synthetic league; a real feed's shape could move the costs. The remaining 50-60 ms on a first request is the page's code loading and is not warmed. `/matchmaking` and the Arabic versions of per-language aggregates (`upsetWatch:ar`, `fightsToMake:8:ar`) are not warmed (10-35 ms each). Pages that depend on a fighter, event or bout (profiles, bout pages) are warm only for the shared parts, which is why they still show the largest remaining first visits (about 110-150 ms). At the demo size none of this is measurable.

## 58. Reports of a wrong fact, and corrections that survive the daily update (round 32, 2026-10-03)

- **Decisions (owner):** signed-in people only; a private queue (nothing public because someone reported it); a **local corrections layer** (not "fix it at the vendor and wait"); a separate admin-only route for "this is about me".
- **Part 1 (this change, no screens):** `lib/accounts/corrections.ts`, a `reports` table in `accounts.db`, `POST/GET/DELETE /api/report`, the review routes under `/api/review/reports`, the source check reused for reports, and hooks in `ingest` and the database open. Fighter fields that can be corrected: birth date, height, reach, stance, nickname, country; fight fields: result, method, end round; `other` is a report an editor looks into and nothing is applied. Every correction needs a source link and the exact words from the page; an editor who is not the reporter decides; a rejection needs a reason.
- **The hard part was the daily update.** The vendor rewrites its values every day, so a correction written once would be undone by the next update, which runs in a separate process that the running app does not see. So `ingest` applies the corrections itself, before ratings are recomputed, and the database open applies them too (a fresh or restored one). The correction remembers the vendor's value as it was: if the vendor rewrites the same value it is corrected again; if the vendor changes it to something else the sourced value stays in and the correction is flagged `vendor_changed` for an editor (keep, or retire and put the vendor's value back). A corrected result or method recomputes the ratings.
- **A bug the tests found:** a newer correction for a field a person had already corrected took the older correction's own value for a change by the vendor and flagged itself. Retiring or superseding a correction now puts the vendor's value back first.
- **Privacy:** an about-me request is never applied and only an admin sees it (it carries an optional contact); a reporter's own list shows no contact, source check or vendor values; an editor never sees about-me requests. The queue's kind and status are whitelisted because they are part of a query (a mutation that removed the whitelist is caught).
- **Mutations:** nine (ingest not re-applying, reviewing your own report, editors seeing about-me, the reporter seeing reviewer fields, retire not restoring, never flagging a vendor change, the route skipping the ratings, duplicates allowed, kind not whitelisted) were each caught by a test.
- **Owner rule (owner's principle: "where there is a verified owner of data that is where the source lies, otherwise use what we ingest"):** a **fight's** facts belong to the commission or sanctioning body, so a correction to them is accepted only with a source on an official host (`.gov` or `data/research/official-hosts.txt`, shared with the research checker via `lib/research/official-hosts.ts`); a **fighter's** details belong to the fighter, so an account an admin has linked to them (`boxer_owners`, `accounts owner NAME BOXER --url ...`) corrects them at once without a source (recorded `by_owner`, audited `owner_correction`, undoable, listed in the `owner` queue for after-the-fact review), and anyone else needs a source under the fighter's registered official URL (`underUrl`: same origin, whole path segment, so `/ace` is not `/ace-fake`). Anything else can only be marked **noted** (new status): the ingested value stands. Bounded: the owner path applies only to the owner's own boxer and only to applicable fighter fields, never to `other` or to fights. **Mutations:** ten tried, nine caught (accepting without an owner source, any host for a fight, any URL for a fighter, prefix match without a path boundary, origin ignored, owner path without the link, `other` applied for an owner, no audit, not marked by_owner); the tenth (owner path for a fight) survives and is equivalent because the link check already fails for a fight id. A test that first passed for the wrong reason (a duplicate-report refusal) was rewritten on a different fighter.
- **Limits:** where a value is one the adapter filled in (an imputed reach) the vendor's value can drift by itself and flag a correction each time, so some noise is expected there. The record audit in `vendor:backfill` still compares fights with the vendor's career totals, so a corrected result shows as a disagreement with the vendor, which is true. **Part 2** (the report form, the review queue screens, a "corrected from a source" note on the page, Arabic strings) is not built.

## 59. A browser sweep of everything since round 20 (round 32, 2026-10-03)
Rounds 21-31 added pages and changed shared components without a browser pass over the result: tests and smoke say a page renders, not that it can be read or reached. This sweeps every route in both languages at 1280 and 375 px, plus the signed-in pages on a local test account, with axe-core, forced WCAG 1.4.12 text spacing, overflow, clipped text, the *rendered* size of chart text, and overlapping text. `scripts/a11y-sweep.js` is the check (the old iframe method broke when round 22 forbade framing), and docs/accessibility.md says how to run it.
- **What it found** (all fixed; the details are in docs/accessibility.md, round 32): chart text as small as **3 px** on a phone (SVG text scales with its picture; the weigh-in chart's labels were three pixels high, the rating chart's five, the radar's six to nine) against a rule that said no text under 12 px, which only checked CSS classes; seven table scroll boxes a keyboard could not scroll (every table wrapper without links now uses the fix); a chip laid over the fighter's name on every similar-fighters card; two timelines with labels printed on top of each other.
- **How the charts were fixed:** labels that must stay readable are HTML, not SVG (the sparkline's peak and dates, the weigh-in chart's ticks and legend); the radar is drawn at its own size and wraps; the calibration chart and donut text were enlarged. `ScrollRegion` (focusable, named, a group) wraps every table that can scroll sideways; `lib/timeline.ts` labels only the years whose 1 January is inside a strip.
- **Guards added** (tests/a11y.test.ts, tests/timeline.test.ts): no SVG `fontSize` under 12 (the poster excepted), no new `overflow-x-auto` outside `ScrollRegion` and the files where every cell is a link, the markup of a scroll box (focusable, named, a group not a region), and the year-tick arithmetic. Mutation checks: twelve deliberate breaks; eleven failed a test, and the twelfth was a condition that could never matter (the end of the strip), which I removed.
- **Two things I got wrong on the way, recorded because they are the kind of mistake a sweep invites:** my first axe run on the new home page at 1280 px reported five contrast failures that were an entrance animation mid-fade (the script now waits for it); and my first `ScrollRegion` used `role="region"`, which made a second landmark with the same name as its section on an Ask answer (found by the last pass, now a group).
- **Not done / limits:** no screen reader (axe finds a fraction of real problems); the graded-picks recap cannot be reached in a live session and is covered only by round 27's hand-built tests; dynamic pages are sampled (one fighter, event, bout, preview and belt); the overlap check is a heuristic (sibling boxes, inline text ignored) and was tuned on this site's pages; the belt timeline's clipped champion names are left as designed.

## 60. Reports and corrections, part 2: the screens (round 32, 2026-10-03)

- **Built:** `/report` (the form, `?boxer=` or `?bout=`, with the person's own list and Withdraw), `/review/reports` (editors' queue: Corrections with waiting / accepted / not accepted / noted, Look again, Fighters' own changes with Undo, and About me for admins only), links from every fighter profile, fight page and the account page, and the "corrected from a source" / "provided by the fighter" note on the fighter and fight pages. Followed `DESIGN.md` (same cards, chips, red button, gold focus as the team-history forms; no new colours). Arabic strings added (105, machine-written like the rest, not reviewed by a person); `/ar/report` looked at on a 375 px phone, no sideways scroll.
- **The owner rule shows in the screens.** The form says which sources can be used (a commission's page for a fight, the fighter's own page for their details) and a confirmed fighter sees that no source is needed ("Correct it now"). In the queue each open correction says whether its source is published by the owner of that fact; if not, there is no Accept button, only **Note it** or **Reject**, so an editor cannot hit the server's refusal by accident (the server still refuses it: `source_not_owner`).
- **Nothing public from a report alone.** A page shows a note only for an accepted correction or a confirmed fighter's own, never for a waiting, rejected or noted report; retiring one takes the note off. Tests, with five mutations caught (open reports shown, retired shown, `by_owner` ignored, another fighter's fights shown, `myOwned` listing everyone).
- **A bug found on the way (not in the new screens):** the error messages and field labels of the existing account, pick'em and team-history forms were written in a module under `components/` that the string scanner counts as server code, so their Arabic was never sent to the browser and they showed in English on the Arabic site (52 strings). The wording modules now live in `lib/` (`lib/account-text.ts`, `lib/correction-text.ts`), and a test fails if a string in them is not counted as a client string (checked by moving one back).
- **Limits:** the fighter and fight names in the person's own list and in the queue are shown as the data has them (English); the form header and page notes use the translated names. A person-reviewed Arabic pass over the new strings is still to be done (`npm run i18n:review`). Linking an account to a fighter is still an operator command (`accounts owner`); there is no screen for it, deliberately: it is the step where someone's identity is checked.

## 61. The Arabic leak check allows the two statistics terms (round 32, 2026-10-03)

- **Why:** the Data page prints "log-loss · {acc} correct · Brier {brier}" under each model once a fit exists (`data/model-fit.json`, gitignored; the demo checkout in CI has none, so CI never showed it). The Arabic keeps the two Latin terms, which `npm run smoke` flagged as English on `/ar/data` in any checkout that had fitted the model, so the first real fit would have turned CI red. Found when the smoke check failed in a working copy that had a fit file while clean `main` passed.
- **What (owner's choice):** `log-loss` and `Brier` are in `LATIN_OK` in `lib/smoke.ts`, next to Elo and other technical names, with a test that the line passes and that English *around* the terms is still flagged (checked by removing them: the test fails). Smoke on a checkout with a fit file: 148/148.
- **Still open:** whether the Arabic should translate the two terms is a question for a native reviewer (`docs/arabic-review.md` already counts "Brier left in a sentence" as a hard check); if they choose a wording, take the two entries out of `LATIN_OK`.

## 62. Does "Ask the data" understand what people type? A question battery (round 33, 2026-10-03)
The home page now puts the question box at the top, so I measured what the rule-based planner (no API key) makes of questions a fan might actually type, instead of the five examples it was built around. `tests/ask-battery.ts` holds 225 questions in four batches (English and Arabic; typos, slang, fragments and long conversational sentences; and questions that should get *no* answer: the weather, a prompt-injection attempt, `DROP TABLE`), each with the tool, and arguments, a sensible reader would expect. `npm run ask:battery` prints the rate for each batch and every miss.
- **Measured honestly.** Fixing the failures of a batch and then reporting that batch's new score only measures how well I fitted it (I did exactly that with batches 1 and 2, and said so while doing it). The numbers that mean something are the ones on questions the planner had not seen: **the planner as it was on main scored 60% on batch 3** (30 of 50); after the fixes made from batches 1 and 2 it scored **72%** (36 of 50) on those same unseen questions; and **batch 4, written after that and measured before any change for it, scored 71%** (29 of 41). So: roughly 70% on fresh phrasing, up from about 60%. For the record, on the original planner batch 1 was 81% (70 of 86) and batch 2 72% (34 of 47). After the last fixes all four batches pass (225 of 225), which is now only a guard against regressions.
- **What was wrong, and is fixed:** names were not found beside punctuation or a possessive ("Villalba's record", "X vs Y: who is better"); a fighter named in a sentence that also said "last fight" went to the recent-events list; two fighters joined by "or" were read as one; "knockouts in 2025" and "title fights this year" could not be asked at all (the `bouts` tool existed but no rule ever called it; it now has a `stoppage` and a `decision` group, because "knockouts" in fan language is KO and TKO); "best welterweight ever" and "best light heavyweight right now" were searches instead of the greatest list and the ranking; "top ten" (a word) was not a number; "who might get upset", "gate receipts", "biggest paydays", "what's on this weekend", "who's fighting next", "the most recent events" and the Arabic "النزالات الأخيرة" matched nothing; a trainer's name without the word "trainer" matched nothing; and "who is the best football player" was answered with a fighter search (a lone "best" is now a search only if the question mentions fighters).
- **One real correctness bug, not a coverage gap:** "who has the most knockouts in 2024" was answered with the **all-time** list, silently ignoring the year (the lists have no year). That is a wrong answer that looks right. A question with a year that none of the year-aware tools can answer now gets no answer rather than the all-time one. (The generic "I could not match that question" message is not very helpful there; a sentence saying why would be better.)
- **Also fixed:** "fighters with more than 20 wins and a knockout rate over 70%" is a search, not the KO-rate list, and "knockout rate over 70%" is now read as a filter; "most fights" and "longest reach" are sorts (a new `bouts` sort, with its label in Arabic, found because the existing tool sweep failed on "by undefined").
- **Checked:** 567 tests (the new file has 6: the whole battery, that every tool and both languages and the no-answer cases are covered, the year rule, name matching, and the `stoppage`/`decision` counts against the world), types, lint, smoke 144/144 on the three feeds. Fourteen deliberate breaks of the planner and the tool each failed a test; two survivors on the first pass (the tool's new groups and the knockout-rate phrase, neither reached by the planning battery) got their own tests.
- **Not done / limits:** about 30% of fresh phrasing is still not understood without a key (ambiguous superlatives such as "who punches the hardest", questions with two intents, things the data cannot answer, such as "never been knocked out", which is correctly refused); **the AI planner (with a key) was not measured**: there is no key here, and the battery's expectations are about the rules; the expectations are my judgment of what a reader would want, and a few questions were removed or given two acceptable tools because they were genuinely ambiguous; the battery is of demo-league names, and Arabic is 20 of 225.

## 63. The home page is approved: the design lab is deleted, and a refusal now says why (round 34, 2026-10-03)
You approved the combined A and C home page ("as you have at the moment"), so the lab that existed to choose it is gone.
- **Deleted:** `app/[locale]/design` (the three directions, development-only, 404 in production), its `DESIGN_LAB` setting (`.env.example`, the doctor's list of known settings, and the drift test now agrees), the test that guarded the lab (replaced by one that fails if `app/[locale]/design` or `DESIGN_LAB` comes back, and tells the next person to put experiments on a branch), the lab's allowance in the scroll-box test, and one Arabic entry only the lab used ("Next main event"). Verified in a production build: `/design/home` is a 404 with no setting involved. DESIGN.md says the direction is approved and the lab removed; the older sections of PLAN.md and docs/accessibility.md that describe the lab are left as history, marked as such where they would mislead.
- **A follow-through from round 33:** "who has the most knockouts in 2024" correctly gets no answer (the all-time lists have no year), but the message was the generic "I could not match that question". It now says "Those lists cover all of boxing history, not one year… Try “best fight of 2025” or “knockouts in 2025” instead" (and in Arabic, with an example the Arabic planner does understand: «أفضل نزال في عام 2025»). `refusalReason()` in `lib/ask/rules.ts` decides it, `Answer.hint` carries it, the answer page and the API (`hint: "year"`) show it. It appears only when there is no answer: a model that does answer a year question, with an API key, gets no hint (a test with a faked model; the first version of the condition would have shown it anyway, which the test caught).
- **Checked:** types, lint, 572 tests (3 new in the battery's file: the refusal in the answer and the API, `refusalReason` on its own, and the model case), smoke 148/148 on the three feeds, the sentences read from a real server's HTML in both languages. Six deliberate breaks, each failed a test.
- **Not done / limits:** only the year case has a specific refusal; every other refusal is still the generic one, and so is a year question for a fighter-filter search ("fighters who debuted in 2024" is a search and works). The live server you are using on :3470 is on the code from before this round: it still has the lab.

## 64. Unknown facts stay unknown (round 32, 2026-10-03)

- **Why:** the readiness list's most important code gap before real data. Height, reach, birth year, stance and debut year were required numbers, so the Boxing Data API adapter filled the gaps: reach from height, then a division median, then 175 cm; a birth year from an age; "Orthodox" for a missing stance; a debut from the first fight held. On the page each became a fact about a real person that nobody had said. The owner's rule is that only verified data is stored.
- **What:** the five are nullable from the adapter through the database to the page (`Boxer`, `ProviderBoxer`, the world; a stored 0 from an older load also reads as unknown). The adapter no longer imputes (`imputePhysicals` is gone) and counts each unknown in its notes (`birthYearUnknown`, `physicalsUnknown`, `stanceUnknown`, `debutUnknown`). The model's reach and age terms give **no edge** when either fighter's is unknown (an unknown on either side is a zero difference, so a missing reach neither helps nor hurts, and the prediction stays symmetric); the fit does the same. Pages show a dash or leave the line out (`lib/facts.ts`); the matchup lab says "Unknown: not counted" instead of a slider; the radar leaves reach off for both fighters when either's is unknown; written previews, scouting text and the Ask tools leave an unknown out of the sentence (a separate Arabic sentence for each combination); a search for a reach or age never matches an unknown and sorts it last; the reach and stance statistics count only fighters and fights where the fact is known; the style vector treats an unknown as typical. Wikidata enrichment now links a fighter with no birth year by BoxRec ID and fills the year from the match (never over a known one); the photo matcher will not match a name without a birth year to check it against.
- **Checked:** 9 new tests (8 on a demo league with the facts removed for two fighters in three: every aggregate and Ask tool, the model, the written text, the preview, search and sort, the statistics, the fit; 1 for the Wikidata link), 12 mutations of the safeguards each caught; the adapter tests now assert that nothing is guessed. `npm run smoke -- --facts unknown` (new, in CI) renders every kind of page in both languages with the facts missing; 148/148. Looked at a fighter, the compare table and the matchup lab with facts missing.
- **Changes the numbers:** an age-only birth year and an imputed reach no longer feed the model; the demo league is unaffected (it has every fact). §58's note that an imputed reach can drift and flag a correction no longer applies.
- **Open:** the demo league never has a fact missing, so the dash states appear only in tests, the unknown-facts smoke run and a real feed. The Arabic for the four new sentences is machine-written like the rest.

## 65. The vendor credit and a contact for people who are not signed in (round 34, 2026-10-03)

- **Why:** the last two code items on the real-data readiness list. A page about real people needs somewhere to report a mistake that does not require an account, and a licensed feed needs its supplier credited.
- **What:** `SITE_CONTACT` (an email address or an `https://` page, published as written; no default, never written into the code) is shown on the Data page ("Spotted a mistake?") and on the Report page for signed-out visitors. The Data page has a "Data supplier" section when `BOXING_PROVIDER=licensed`: "Boxing Data API", linked, with "Licence terms" once `VENDOR_TERMS_URL` is set (https only), and a line that Ringside's ratings, rankings and predictions are Ringside's own. Neither shows for the demo league. Both are validated before they reach every visitor (`lib/site-info.ts`: plain http, `javascript:`, credentials in an address and malformed emails are refused), and English-only text (the vendor's name, the email) is marked `lang="en"`. The doctor warns when a licensed site has no contact or a bad one, and notes a missing terms link.
- **Checked:** 5 new tests (the contact, the https rule, the credit, and two for the doctor), five mutations caught; looked at the Data page in English and the Report page in Arabic at 375 px on a copy of the demo database set up as a licensed site.
- **Open:** what the vendor requires beyond a credit (specific wording, a logo) is not known until its written answer arrives. The contact must be a role address you are happy to publish; use none of your own.

## 66. A search that forgives a slip, and an index that was rebuilt on every keystroke (round 35, 2026-10-03)
Everything about finding a fighter starts with typing a name, and the search was exact substring matching: every word typed had to appear in a name. Accents, word order and half-typed names were fine; one wrong or missing letter found nothing. I measured it first with a battery of 3,439 queries made from the league's own names (`tests/search-battery.ts`, `npm run search:battery`): letters missing, wrong, swapped, doubled; vowels confused; initials with and without a full stop; a hyphen dropped; nicknames; shouted queries; stray spaces and punctuation; and Arabic misspellings. The fighter meant had to be among the eight results the type-ahead shows.
- **Before, on main (in the top eight):** group A (the mistakes I designed for) 48%, B (related ones) 1%, D (kinds written after the matcher) 14%, Arabic 75%, C (respellings by ear) 0%. Every kind of one-letter slip scored 0%, and "T. Villalba" failed 86% of the time because of the full stop.
- **After:** A 100% (first result 92%), B 99% (95%), D 100% (96%), Arabic 98% (96%), C 98% (94%). Exact search is unchanged: a name typed as written is first 98+% of the time and an exact match is never padded with near ones.
- **How it works** (`lib/fuzzy.ts`, `lib/fighter-search.ts`): when nothing matches exactly, look for names with a word close to each word typed: starting with it, or one slip away (a letter missing, added or wrong, or two neighbours swapped), or one slip from the start of it (for a half-typed word). Three letters or fewer must match exactly (too many words are one slip apart), one or two letters are an initial and must start a word, four to six letters get one slip, seven or more get two. The scan runs over the distinct words of all names (with neighbouring words run together, so "Alqahtani", "Al Qahtani" and "Al-Qahtani" are one name), not over the fighters; the fighter with the fewest slips overall comes first, then the more experienced.
- **Honest accounting.** Group D was written after the first version of the matcher and measured once before it was touched again: **99% in the top eight and 95% first**. That measurement showed punctuation (`!`, `?`, quotes) was not a separator, and group B showed the number of slips allowed followed the typed word's length instead of the name's (two letters missing from a long name); I fixed both, so the final D and B numbers include fixes I made after seeing them. A later check on real queries found my long-name rule too generous for a half-typed word ("ramil abat" also offered Bautista: two slips in four letters); the allowance for the start of a word now counts only what was typed, which removed the false match with no change in the battery.
- **A performance bug, found while testing this and fixed:** on main, at 19,360 fighters, **every English type-ahead request rebuilt the whole search index** (25 ms each, 40 requests = 1 second): the index is kept per names table, and `getNames("en")` returned a new empty object on every call, so the cache never hit. English is now one shared table (and any empty table is treated as the same one), and recent answers are cached: 1.5 ms per request, 17 times faster. Nothing in the old tests could see it.
- **What it costs and where it stops:** at 19,360 fighters an exact search is 0.5 ms, the forgiving fallback about 30 ms (15 ms when nothing is close), the index is built once per world (about 70 ms) and answers are cached; the scan grows with the number of distinct words, so expect a few times that at 100,000 fighters. Random strings far from any name find nothing (`qqqqqqqq`), but about one in five random 5-9 letter strings at this size is within a slip of some name and returns it, which is what "did you mean" does. In the ⌘K palette the guess is shown only when nothing else matched (so a typo in a page name does not list fighters beside it).
- **Checked:** types, lint, 585 tests (13 new: a hand-built league of eight fighters with near-duplicate names such as Villalba and Villalta, the battery with floors just under what was measured, the cache, the shared English table, the palette rule), smoke 148/148 on the three feeds. Seventeen deliberate breaks of the matcher (including the search palette and the shared English table) each failed a test except one, which is only a speed shortcut (the answer is the same without it).
- **Not done / limits:** only fighters are forgiving: people, gyms and events in the palette, the `/boxers?q=` name filter and the rule-based Ask planner's name matching are still exact (the AI planner's fighter lookup is forgiving); the demo league's names are synthetic and fairly regular, so real names (more diacritics, transliterations of Arabic and Cyrillic names, longer compound surnames) could do better or worse; phonetic misspellings beyond two edits ("Thompson" for "Tomsen") are not covered; and the matcher does not learn from what people click.

## 67. Mining Wikidata for what no feed will give (round 35, 2026-10-03)

- **Measured first, on live Wikidata from this container (2026-10-03, one request each, identified, three seconds apart):** 19,639 boxers; an Arabic label for **6,510** (4,337 of them with a BoxRec ID, so the ones a feed is most likely to carry); an English Wikipedia article for 12,524; a gender for 18,895; Olympic participation for 5,526 (but a ranking on only 394); 581 sports-team statements; 310 boxers with a boxing relative; **a nickname for only 438; a weight class for only 36; a held position for only 118**. So Arabic names and article links are worth taking; weight class and champion positions are not (the Wikipedia lists already cover belts, in full); nicknames are a small bonus. The staged run on the owner's machine matched: 19,639 listed, 17,198 with a birth year, 10,110 with a BoxRec ID; the champions run read 2,640 reigns (WBA 1,079, WBC 664, IBF 495, WBO 402), 1,741 Wikidata IDs, 4 unreadable rows left out, 2 unknown division headings kept under the page's name.
- **What was built:** the second (extras) query now also reads the Arabic label, a nickname and the English article title, and stages them (`ar_label`, `nickname`, `enwiki`, `labels_at`). Boxers staged before this are filled by `npm run wikidata:import -- --extras-only` (it now also selects rows with no `labels_at`), without refetching biographies. `--enrich` applies them **only to fighters already linked to a Wikidata entity**, and only to blanks: an Arabic name becomes the translation of the fighter's name with source `wikidata`, not reviewed, and never replaces an existing translation (a person's or a machine's); a nickname is set only where the fighter has none; the article title (`boxers.wikipedia_title`) only where there is none, and the profile links to it ("Wikipedia" under Identifiers; no text is copied, so CC BY-SA attribution does not arise).
- **What is refused:** an Arabic label that is not Arabic script alone (Latin letters, digits of either kind, markup, over 80 characters); a nickname that is not one a person could set (the same rule as a nickname correction) or when Wikidata gives several different ones; an article title with brackets, braces, pipes, angle brackets, `#` or `?`. The cells are checked again when applied.
- **Checked:** 7 new tests plus a live run of the new query on 40 boxers (valid query; 40 of 40 with an Arabic label and an article, 6 with a nickname, honours intact, 195 rows for 40 boxers: no row explosion) and 8 mutations each caught (Latin in an Arabic name, Arabic digits, overwriting a translation, overwriting a nickname, picking the first of several nicknames, ignoring `labels_at`, not marking labels as read, unsafe article titles).
- **Not mined, and why:** boxing events (about 1,859 dated, 998 with a place) have no bouts or results on Wikidata, so they would be empty cards; weight class (36) and held positions (118) are too thin; Olympic results (394 with a ranking) and families (310) are small and can wait. All are one more query away if wanted.
- **Open:** the Arabic labels are Wikidata contributors' work, not a native reviewer's: they show as unreviewed names like the rest and go through the review sheet the same way. Fighters Wikidata has no Arabic label for (about two in three) still use the existing fallback, which is still the owner's decision to make.

## 68. Forgiving search for trainers, gyms, venues and the name filter (round 36, 2026-10-03)
Round 35 made fighter search forgive a slip but left the other things people type: a trainer, judge, gym, promotion, venue or city in ⌘K found nothing with a letter wrong, and so did a misspelt name in the `/boxers?q=` filter. This extends the same matcher to them.
- **Measured first:** `entityCases` in `tests/search-battery.ts` makes 1,308 queries from the league's own people, gyms and promotions, venues and cities (a letter missing, wrong, doubled or swapped; a vowel confused; a hyphen dropped; and the name as written), scored through the real palette function (`globalSearch`): the thing meant, or any event at that venue, has to be among the hits. **On main: 0% for every kind of slip** (the as-written controls were 100%). **Now: 100% for the designed slips (876 queries) and 100% for the related ones (432)**; the weakest cells are a letter missing from a four-letter word in a city or venue (86%), which stays exact on purpose. As in round 35 the two groups were both written before the fix; they were not tuned separately, but I did see both before measuring, so this is a check that the engine generalises across kinds of name, not a held-out number.
- **How:** the near-match engine moved out of the fighter search into `lib/fuzzy.ts` (`buildWordIndex`, `nearTexts`) and the fighter search now uses it; the fighter numbers did not move (checked by re-running the round-35 battery before building on it). In ⌘K, when nothing matched exactly, guesses now come from every kind: fighters, then trainers/judges/referees, then events (by name, city or venue; a called-off event is never guessed), then gyms and promotions, each with its fewest-slips first, capped per group. The index is built once per world and per names table (45 ms at 12,270 events, 4,920 people, 1,296 organisations), and an empty table is one table. A page match or any exact match still means no guessing at all.
- **The name filter:** `/boxers?q=Tomás Vilalba` (a name and nothing else, spelt a little differently from any fighter's) used to show "Nobody matches that". It now shows the nearest spellings under "No fighter is spelt exactly “Tomás Vilalba”. These are the closest names." (Arabic added; machine translation, unreviewed). Only when the whole question is a name: a question with filters in it is not turned into a name search. A route in the smoke run now requests exactly this on a real production server and requires the fighter to be on the page, English only (the query is English the Arabic page rightly echoes; the leak check said so), on the full, sparse and empty feeds.
- **Cost (19,360 fighters, 12,270 events):** palette lookups with exact names are unchanged (about 18 ms; that scan is older and could be made much cheaper), a palette search with a slip across all four kinds about 66 ms (fighters about 30, the rest about 35), the one-off index 45 ms.
- **Checked:** types, lint, 603 tests (the near-spelling palette on a hand-built league with a called-off event and look-alike names such as Marcos and Marcus Quintero, the entity battery, the shared index, the smoke route), smoke 149/149 on the three feeds. Ten deliberate breaks of the palette's guesses each failed a test; three survivors on the first pass (a cancelled event guessed, the order of guesses, the shared index) got a case in the hand-built league each.
- **Not done / limits:** the rule-based Ask planner's name matching is still exact (a typo in a name there gets the generic refusal; the AI planner's fighter lookup is forgiving), and the palette's exact path still normalises every person and event on every keystroke (about 18 ms at this size) when it could reuse the index.


## 69. The vendor confirmed storage; the three enquiry drafts tightened (round 35, 2026-10-03)

- **The vendor's answer is in.** The owner reports that the Boxing Data API's operator confirmed that its data may be stored (2026-10-03). The readiness list and runbook no longer call storage provisional: set `BOXING_API_STORAGE_CONFIRMED=1` and keep the vendor's message where it can be found. Two things to check in that message before relying on it, since the paid month is for a one-time backfill: that the data may be **kept after dropping to a cheaper plan**, and that the plan bought has the **full history** and how far back it goes. The code is unchanged; `=0` still refuses to store.
- **Drafts shortened** (TBRB, FightFax, BBBofC): the ask in the first lines, what we would and would not do stated once, four to six questions instead of five to seven, and one signature block. FightFax now leads with a **verification-only** licence (the records come from the licensed feed; the need is to check each career record against the official one), with display as an optional second use. Still drafts: they need the sender's name and role, and nothing from any of the three bodies is used until a reply says yes in writing.

## 70. A privacy page that is checked against the code, and two privacy defects it found (round 37, 2026-10-04)
Accounts have existed since round 18 with no statement of what is kept, who can see it or how to remove it, and the question "what does deleting my account actually do" had never been tested. Writing the page meant reading the code for every claim, and two of them were false.
- **Defects found and fixed (each test written to fail first):** (1) *Deleting an account left the person's username in the activity log* (as actor, target and inside the detail text) *and left the contact they typed on any report they filed.* Deletion now clears the contact, replaces the name in the log with "a deleted account" (whole words only: a user called "ned" does not damage "planned"), and logs only that an account was deleted. (2) *The data export omitted* reports, sessions, linked fighters and the person's activity. `lib/accounts/export.ts` now builds it from a list of exported tables plus a list of excused ones (`NOT_EXPORTED`: password hash, session tokens, other people's rows), and a test fails if a table with a person's id is in neither, so a new table forces a decision about export and the page.
- **The page** (`/privacy`, both languages; linked from the footer and the account panel, in the sitemap and the ⌘K palette) has seven sections: reading without an account, creating one, who can see what, your controls, what deleting removes and what stays, what is sent to other services, and who is responsible (`SITE_CONTACT`). Its numbers and lists are read from code (`lib/privacy.ts`: browser storage keys, held fields, the places text is sent to the model provider, picture hosts; `RESET_MINUTES`, `DEFAULT_BACKUPS_KEPT`, session days from the modules that use them) instead of being retyped.
- **Claims are tested against the code (`tests/privacy.test.ts`, 13 tests):** the browser's own storage holds exactly the keys the page lists; only the sign-in routes set a cookie, and it is HttpOnly, SameSite=Lax; nothing that runs or styles the page loads from another site and no tracker appears in the code or the CSP; the accounts database holds exactly the tables and columns the page declares; model-provider calls come from exactly the listed places; the page contains no hand-typed copy of a number the code owns; the page is linked, in the sitemap and palette, and has Arabic. `securityProblems` (used by the smoke run) checks the matching headers on a real server.
- **Arabic:** 52 new machine-translated entries, unreviewed. The smoke English-left-on-Arabic check flagged the cookie name, the device-label example and `https` on the first run; the first two are wrapped as English islands, `https` is an allowed term (with a test).
- **Checked:** types, lint, 623 tests (622 pass, 1 skipped as before), smoke 151/151 on the three feeds, an axe/overflow/spacing sweep of `/privacy` at 375 and 1280 px in English and Arabic (clean). 25 deliberate breaks across two passes: 24 each failed a test; the 25th (an escape of `_` in a LIKE) was dead code, because the filter that follows is exact, so it was removed rather than tested.
- **Not done / limits:** this is a description of the software, not legal advice; retention periods, the legal basis and the controller's name are the operator's to decide (the page says so and shows `SITE_CONTACT`). Backups keep a deleted person's rows until they rotate out (14 kept by default). Reports, contributions and approved edits stay after deletion, without the author. The Arabic text needs a native reader.

## 71. Ask the data reads a name with a slip (round 38, 2026-10-04)
Round 36 left one limit: the rule-based planner (no API key) found a fighter or trainer only when the name was spelt exactly, so "tell me about Marcus Brightwel" got no answer, and sometimes a wrong one that looked right ("what was Ellis Pehnale's last fight" was read as the latest results; "is Ellis Penhle a southpaw" as a list of southpaws). The tools themselves already forgive a slip once they are handed a name; the planner never found the name.
- **Measured first:** `tests/ask-names-battery.ts` makes 743 questions from the league's own names (`npm run ask:names`): fighters, pairs and trainers with a letter missing, added, wrong or swapped, a vowel confused, a slip in each name, accents or capitals left off, spelt by ear, and Arabic names with their slips; plus 40 questions that use names that are nobody's. The tool called and the name found are both checked, on the result the tool returns. **On main:** A (designed kinds) 123/326 = 38%, B (related kinds) 7/217 = 3%, C 44/160 = 28%, and every case with a slip failed; the controls (name written correctly, lower case, accents off) and the nobody's-name group were fine. **Now:** A 326/326, B 216/217, C 157/160, nobody's 40/40.
- **How honest that is:** A and B were written before the change but I saw their failures while building it (the hyphen handling came from them). C was written after A and B passed and measured once before I touched the matcher again: **93%** (149/160), the one kind it missed completely being a space typed inside a name, which I then added; so 98% is no longer an unseen number. There is no unseen group left; the next kind of slip someone finds should go in a new one.
- **How:** `lib/ask/rules.ts`. Names written in full still claim their words first (fighters, and a trainer's full name is protected from a fighter a middle initial away: "What difference does Niklas G. Kessler make" is about the trainer, and "Wyatt G. Stroud trainer" about the fighter; both were wrong on the first version of this and found by running the old and new planner over the question battery on a 19,360-fighter league). Then in the words left a name is found when each of its words (two at least) is, in order and side by side, a question word or within the slips `lib/fuzzy.ts` allows (none up to three letters, so initials and short words are exact; one up to six; two beyond), at most two slips in the whole name. The fewest slips wins; when two different people tie ("Marcas Quintero" between Marcos and Marcus) neither is named, because a wrong answer that looks right is worse than none. A hyphen can be a space, absent, or have the slip next to it; two neighbouring words are tried run together ("Ell ery"); in Arabic the attached و of "and" is allowed. The same matching serves trainers and Arabic names.
- **Cost (19,360 fighters):** the word index is built once per league and names table (about 50 ms), and a new question costs about 1 ms more than before (4.7 ms against 3.6 ms on average over the 225 battery questions; words looked up are remembered).
- **False positives:** the 225-question battery gives byte-identical plans on the 19,360-fighter league before and after; 15,120 questions made of a real first name and an ordinary word ("tell me about Marcus rankings", "Rakan fights") named nobody, before and after.
- **Checked:** types, lint, 637 tests (636 pass, 1 skipped as before: 11 hand-built-league tests of what must and must not be forgiven, 3 for the battery's thresholds), build, smoke 151/151, 111/111, 82/82 and 151/151, and a real server asked "who is Lukas Hartmnan" and "Lukas Hartmnan vs Tomas Vilalba" over HTTP. 17 deliberate breaks of the matching each failed a test; two survived at first and were dealt with (the three-letter exact rule had no test; a check that the merged words were separated only by spaces was dead code, because the question's punctuation is already gone, and was removed).
- **Not done / limits:** names are whole names, as before: a surname alone or an initial and surname ("L. Hartmann") is not read as a fighter (ambiguous, and the search box handles those); a three-letter word, or a name with only three-letter words, must be spelt exactly; a name in the question with a middle initial left out or added is not guessed at; with an API key the model plans, and its name lookup was already forgiving. The Arabic cases use the demo league's machine-translated names.

## 72. Two more question batteries for Ask the data, and the wrong answers they found (round 39, 2026-10-04)
Round 33 measured the rule-based planner (no API key) at about 70% on fresh phrasing. This round wrote two more batches, both before changing anything for them, and went looking for answers that look right and are wrong, not only for questions with no answer.
- **Batch 5** (73 questions: filters the way fans say them, the same lists in other words, the other arguments of the tools, Arabic, and things that are not boxing) scored **74%** (54 of 73) on the planner as it was. (My first run said 67%: five of the "failures" were my expectations naming the wrong argument, `division` where the fighters tool says `weightClass`; I corrected the expectations, not the planner, and say so because it moved the number.) Fixing its 19 misses took it to 100%, which makes it a regression guard, not a measure.
- **Batch 6** (55 questions, written after batch 5 was fitted, asking for the same things in different words) scored **78%** (43 of 55) before any change for it; after the fixes, 100%, and the same caveat. So the honest estimate for fresh phrasing is now about 75-80%, up from about 70%. All six batches (353 questions, 54 of them Arabic) pass.
- **Wrong answers that looked right, now fixed:** "Brazilian southpaws" listed **all 177 southpaws**: the planner knew a country only if a fighter in the data was from it, and the tool then dropped a country it did not know. A country not in the data is now an answer ("No fighters in the data are from Brazil", in Arabic too); there is a hand-made list of about 70 nationalities and countries for the words that are not in the data. "How many people live in Mexico" listed Mexican fighters and "who won the game last night" the latest events: a country alone is no longer a search (it needs a word about boxing or another filter) and another sport's name ends the question. "Left handed boxers" and "25 or more wins" were read without the stance and the number.
- **Four dead Arabic patterns:** the question is folded before it is read (ى becomes ي, ة becomes ه, ئ becomes ي), so a pattern written with the unfolded letter can never match. Found because "نتائج الفعاليات" matched nothing: the pattern for it, and three in `lib/ai.ts` (right-handed `يمنى`, "the strongest" `الاقوى`, "range of the arms" `مدى`), had never worked. All four fixed and each has a test; the rest of `lib/` was searched for the same mistake and has none.
- **Phrasing now understood** (all rules, each with a test or a battery question): quickest/fastest *ever* stoppage, "most one-sided/surprising result", "won the most world title fights", "longest unbeaten run", "beaten the most top-rated opponents", "highest rated boxer ever", "most successful defences", "most dominant reign", "best *bout* of 2022", favourites who "look shaky", are "in danger" or "at risk", "top paid boxers", "biggest live gate", "who headlines next"; in Arabic: the best three/five in a division, who holds a belt, most title defences (دافع as well as دفاع), how many knockouts in a year, the latest results, expected upsets, "highest salaries", "never lost".
- **Checked:** types, lint, 642 tests (641 pass, 1 skipped as before), `ask:names` unchanged (326/326, 216/217, 157/160, 40/40), build, smoke 151/111/82/151, i18n check (one new Arabic string, machine-translated), and a real server asked five of the new questions over HTTP. 37 deliberate breaks of the new rules each failed a test; four survived on the first pass (two alternatives of the quality-wins rule, the "shaky favourites" group, and the Arabic latest-results words, each hidden by another rule that matched the same battery question) and got a test of their own.
- **Not done / limits:** about one question in five of fresh phrasing is still not understood without a key; the AI planner (with a key) is still not measured (no key here); the nationality list is hand-made and will miss some; "Georgia" is read as the country; the expectations are my judgment of what a reader wants; the battery uses the demo league's fictional names; and Arabic is 54 of 353.


## 73. The backfill meets the plan's own hourly limit (round 36, 2026-10-03)

- **What happened:** the first full `vendor:backfill -- --plan` worked (44,255 fights, 11,021 events, 35,222 fighters; 496 list requests; the date-window paging read a list of 477 pages in 12 windows, its first test against the real API). The `--check` that followed died after 11 seconds: the gateway (RapidAPI's, `x-rapidapi-proxy-response: true`) answered 429 with "You have exceeded the rate limit per hour for your plan, MEGA, by the API provider", no `Retry-After`, no quota headers. The plan *is* Mega; the limit is the API provider's own, per hour, and the vendor's public pricing page gives none (it lists Mega as 500,000 a month, unlimited history and fighters).
- **Two defects in our code it exposed:** the adapter gave up after four retries spread over about eleven seconds, and the message said "retry after unknown s" and left out the vendor's words; worse, a 429 on a *fighter* fetch was logged as "fighter skipped" and the loop went on, so an hourly limit in the middle of 35,000 fighters would have skipped thousands, each a refused request.
- **What changed:** `--per-hour N` spaces requests evenly under a known limit (the slower of that and `--gap-ms` wins); `--patience-min M` (default 90) waits a rate-limit refusal out in steps of 1, 2, 5, then 10 minutes (or the `Retry-After` given) and retries the same request without using up the ordinary retries; a refusal that says a quota is used up (monthly, daily) is never waited for and ends the run at once with the vendor's words and where to look; any 429 now ends the run instead of being skipped; the message carries the vendor's own text. `--plan --per-hour N` prices a paced run in hours. The stand-in vendor (`lib/vendor-mock.ts`) can now refuse as the real one did (`hourlyLimit`, `monthlyQuota`, a movable clock).
- **Checked:** 7 unit tests on a fake clock (the vendor's words and the advice; waits finish the run with every fight once and no extra request served; the waits grow 1, 2, 5, 10, 10; a quota is never waited for; patience running out says how long it waited and the next run only does the rest from the cache; `--per-hour` is never refused where the same run unpaced is; the plan estimate) and one test of the real command end to end (the flags, the hourly refusal, the quota).
- **A test of mine hung for six minutes** because `--per-hour 2` makes the plan's own list requests wait half an hour each; the flag does what it says, the test was wrong. It uses a sane rate now.
- **The limits, now known (owner attached the RapidAPI plan card):** Mega is 500,000 requests a month (hard), **500 requests an hour**, and 10,240 MB of bandwidth a month, then $0.001 per MB. About 36,000 requests are needed, so the month is no constraint and the hour is: at `--per-hour 450` the 35,222 fighters take about 78 hours (resumable; `BOXING_API_PER_HOUR=450` does the same as the flag, the flag wins). The adapter now counts the bytes it downloads (`bytes()`; cached answers count nothing) and the fetch summary prints "N MB downloaded", so the bandwidth is read off the first run instead of assumed. A follow-up to the vendor (not sent) asks only whether the hourly limit can be lifted for a day or two.
- **Open:** three days is long for a first look at real data. Fetching the most recently active fighters first and loading only fights whose fighters were fetched would give a coherent league sooner, with the career-record check deciding how complete it is; not built, because it changes what the loaded league means and wants the owner's say.

## 74. Taking the real league in stages (round 37, 2026-10-03)

- **Why:** at the plan's 500 requests an hour the 35,222 fighters take about three days (§73). The owner chose to fetch the most recently active fighters first and load only the fights between two fetched fighters, with the career-record check saying how complete it is.
- **What changed:** `--fighters N` fetches the N fighters with the most recent (or coming) fight, newest first (`lib/vendor-selection.ts`, `rankByRecency`), and loads only the fights between two of them; the rest are counted as `boutsOutsideSelection`, not as a failed fetch (that note still stops the command). A later run with a bigger N, or none, fetches only the rest from the cache. `--plan --fighters N` prices the chosen fighters and, from the fight list alone (no fighter request), prints for a few sizes the fights that would load, the share of fighters with every listed fight loaded, and how many sit in groups chosen whole. `--fighters`/`--complete-only` are refused with `--update`.
- **A finding that changes what "coherent" can mean:** a record is only certain to be right if the opponent's fights are loaded too, and so on along the chain. The first measure (every fight loaded) is what the obvious design gives, but it leaves the opponents at the edge with short records (a fighter shown as 1-0 who is 19-0-1). The strict measure, a **core** (`coherentCore`): the fighters whose records equal the vendor's totals and whose opponents' do, repeated until nothing short is left, is exactly right but is a *connected-group* property, so it is far smaller than the first measure until most of the league is fetched. `--complete-only` loads that core: no record on the site is short or invented; the league is smaller. Both numbers are in the plan, so the owner can see them before spending the hours.
- **Checked:** unit tests for the ranking (newest first, coming fights first, a long career placed by its newest fight, ties keep the list order), the preview and the core (a short record spreads along its whole chain; a conflict or a missing record puts the fighter and his opponents out; cancelled or undecided fights hold no one in or out); adapter tests on the stand-in vendor (which fighters, in what order, counted not failed, every fight loaded or counted, a bigger N costs only the rest, no limit loads everything); the real command (plan, check, refusal until allowed, flag validation, a `--complete-only` load in which **every record in the database equals the vendor's career total**). Each safeguard was broken in turn and a test caught it (oldest first, outside counted as failure, no spreading, core not applied, core not loaded, limit ignored).
- **Open:** whether the core is big enough to be worth publishing is an empirical question for the real data: the owner's first `--plan --fighters N` and `--check --fighters N` answer it. If the core is tiny at N=5,000, the choices are `--allow-partial` (short records on the site for the edge) or waiting for the full fetch; storing the vendor's career totals beside the loaded record, so a page could say "record shown is partial", is the other way and is not built.

## 75. The corners and officials leaderboards can be read to the end, and searched (round 40, 2026-10-04)
`/people` showed the first 60 trainers and the first 50 managers, judges and referees and stopped: no next page, no search. On the demo league that hid 90 of 150 trainers, 20 of 70 managers and 14 of 64 judges (all 40 referees fit); on the 19,360-fighter league, 2,940 of 3,000 trainers, 1,350 of 1,400 managers, 270 of 320 judges and 150 of 200 referees. They were reachable only through a fighter's team history or ⌘K.
- **Now:** every tab pages 50 at a time (`?page=`, the same previous/next links as the fighter and event lists; a bad or out-of-range number lands on a real page), and has a name filter (`?q=`) that keeps the tab and, for trainers, the sort. Each row keeps its place in the whole ranking, so page 2 starts at 51 and a filtered list still says who is 137th. The filter finds a name by any of its words in any order, ignoring accents and capitals, in English and (with the table) in Arabic. When no name has what was typed it offers the nearest spellings, as the fighter filter does ("No name is spelt exactly “Tomas Vilalba”. These are the closest names."), fewest slips first, and says "No one in this list has that name" when nothing is near. The logic is `lib/people-list.ts` (`filterByName`, `pageRows`), not in the page, so it is tested without a server; the scoring-disputes cards on the judges tab show on the unfiltered first page only.
- **Cost:** about 1 ms to filter a list on the demo league and about 4 ms on the 19,360-fighter one (measured for judges and referees, exact and near-spelling; the trainer list of 3,000 was not timed separately).
- **Checked:** types, lint, 647 tests (646 pass, 1 skipped as before; 5 new: the empty filter, matching and ranks, Arabic, near spellings and strangers, paging with 120 rows), smoke 156/113/84/156 (five new routes: the trainer at rank 51 must be on page 2, a judge spelt with a letter missing must be found, a page number past the end), `i18n:check` (nine new Arabic strings, machine-translated). A browser sweep (axe, overflow, text spacing, clipping) of six pages in English and Arabic at 1280 and 375 px found one problem the first time: my search form was a second landmark with no name next to the header's (axe `landmark-unique`); it now has one, and all twelve pages are clean. 11 deliberate breaks of `lib/people-list.ts` each failed a test (one of my own new tests was wrong first, in how the nearness of a spelling is counted, and was corrected before the breaks were run). The page's wiring (the pager's links, the disputes cards) is covered by the smoke routes and the sweep only, not by mutation.
- **Not done / limits:** the lists still have their thresholds (a trainer needs 4 fights on record, a manager 4), so people below them are in no list (⌘K and the fighter's page still find them); the filter is by name only, not country or gym; only the trainer tab has a sort; the managers, judges and referees tables have no rank column (their order is the ranking).

## 76. Pictures beyond headshots: belts, organisation logos, venue photos (round 41, 2026-10-03)

- **Asked:** a resolver for organisation logos, fighter belts, venue photos and the like, with the six major promotions' logos as the example.
- **What a live look at Wikidata and Commons showed before anything was built:** WBA, WBC, IBF and WBO each have a belt photo, free-licensed (CC BY-SA); none of the six promotions has a logo on Wikidata, because a promotion's logo is a trademark and Commons does not hold non-free files. So belts and venue photos can be filled; promotion logos cannot be, by any route that does not mean copying a trademark. Wikidata's query service was refusing requests at the time, so the check used the plain API one item at a time.
- **What changed:** a table `entity_media` (kind, ref, licence, credit, thumbnail address; only `matched` rows are shown) and `orgs.wikidata_id`; `lib/media/entities.ts` (belts for the four bodies with a label guard on the Wikidata item, organisation linking on an exact name + boxing + a single candidate, logos from P154 with SVG allowed, venue photos from P18 for venues already matched); the licence gate of the headshot resolver is shared (`imageFor`, now with options; a "trademarked" restriction stays on the credit); `npm run media:resolve -- --entities`; a credited picture on the title pages, the organisation pages and under the event poster; the coverage page counts them; the privacy page's list of outside hosts includes where they are served from; Arabic for the new strings; `docs/media.md`.
- **Checked:** 9 tests on the real values (belt licences, the Top Rank namesake that is a record label) and a mocked Commons; each rule was broken in turn and a test caught it (label guard, boxing check, ambiguity check, near-miss match, SVG for photos, the trademark note, the 45-day re-check, unmatched shown, hosts undeclared, link not kept).
- **Open, for the owner:** (1) the promoters' logos: ask each promoter for permission from their press kit, or leave the name in text; (2) the feed's event poster address (`poster_image_url`) is promoter artwork that the adapter already keeps and the page loads from the vendor's host, while the readiness note says not to hotlink vendor images: ask the vendor, or drop it; (3) the organisations table is empty in a real-data league until promoters and gyms come from some source, so organisation logos wait for that; belts and venue photos do not.

## 77. The gyms and promotions can be read to the end, and searched (round 41, 2026-10-04)
The same gap as `/people` (§75): `/orgs` showed the top 36 gyms and the top 36 promotions with a "top 36 of N" label and no way to see the rest. On the demo league that hid 28 of 64 gyms (all 12 promotions fit); at 1,300 organisations it showed 72 cards.
- **Now:** Promotions and Gyms are two tabs (`/orgs?kind=gym`; promotions by default, as before), each paged 36 cards at a time with the same previous/next links and a name filter, which is the one built for `/people` (`lib/people-list.ts`, now used with its own page size; the search box is a shared component, `components/ListFinder.tsx`, which the people page uses too). Cards keep their place in the whole ranking, a misspelt name gets the nearest spellings, and Arabic names are found. The four sanctioning bodies stay above the tabs.
- **How it stays cheap:** `orgsIndex` (stats for the top 36 only) became `orgsRanking`, a cheap count-based ranking of every gym and promotion, and the page computes the expensive stats (`orgStable`) for the cards of the page shown only, as before: 36 at most. The warm-up and the scale test were moved to it (the scale test now checks that the ranking has every gym and promotion in the same order as computing stats for all of them first), and the scale benchmark times the ranking plus one page of stats. I did not re-time it at 20x.
- **Checked:** types, lint, 676 tests (675 pass, 1 skipped as before; one new test of the page-size option, the warm-up and scale tests moved to the new function), smoke 161/115/86/161 (five new routes: the gym ranked 37th is on page 2, a promotion spelt with a letter missing is found, a page past the end), a browser sweep (axe, overflow, text spacing, clipping) of six pages in English and Arabic at 1280 and 375 px, all clean. Two Arabic strings added (machine-translated) and two removed (the "top 36 of N" labels). I did not run a mutation pass on this round: the new logic is `lib/people-list.ts` (mutation-tested in §75), and the page wiring is covered by the smoke routes and the sweep only.
- **Not done / limits:** gyms and promotions are filtered by name only (not country or city); the link from a gym's or promotion's page back to the list is unchanged; the tab, not both lists, is what a bookmark remembers.

## 78. Every division's ranking can be read to the end (round 42, 2026-10-04)
`/rankings/[division]` showed the top 15 and stopped, and a fighter's own page gave a rank only if it was in the top 200. The demo league never shows it (no division has more than 14 qualifying men), which is why it went unnoticed; a real league does. Measured on the 20x league (19,360 fighters): **every men's division has 206 to 221 ranked fighters, so 93% of them (about 200 of 215) were on no ranking page at all**, and about 15 per division had no rank on their own page (the 217th of 217 got `null`).
- **Now:** the page shows 25 a time with the previous/next links, and a name filter when the division has more than a page (nearest spellings when nothing is spelt that way; Arabic names found; `?sex=female` and the page are kept in every link). Each fighter keeps the place held in the whole division (page 2 starts at 26, and a filtered list still says who is 137th), and the movement arrows and 90-day rating change are worked out for the rows shown. The "top rated" card above the table is always the division's number one. A page past the end lands on the last one.
- **How:** `lib/rankings.ts` keeps one memoised ranking per division and sex (`divisionPool`: the eligible fighters best first, each one's place, and each one's place three months ago); `rankDivision` gained an `offset`, and `rankedBoxers` and `rankRow` let the page filter the whole list and build rows for one page. `rankOf` reads the place from that map, so it has **no 200 cap**: every ranked fighter has a rank, and an unranked one (inactive, fewer than five fights, a losing record, nothing in 24 months) still has none. The rankings used to be recomputed on every call (0.4 ms for the top 15 at 20x; it was cheap, not free); now they are computed once per world and warmed with the other aggregates (`division rankings` in `lib/warm.ts`, and the warm test checks all 34 keys).
- **Checked:** types, lint, 681 tests (680 pass, 1 skipped as before; 4 new in `tests/rankings-paging.test.ts` on 260 hand-made fighters: who is ranked, pages that continue the numbers and add up to the whole division, ranks past 200, movement including a new entry), smoke 165/119/90/165 (two new routes), a real server on the 20x league answering every new URL in 10 to 75 ms, and a browser sweep (axe, overflow, text spacing, clipping) of six pages in English and Arabic at 1280 and 375 px, all clean. 14 deliberate breaks of `lib/rankings.ts` each failed a test; one survived at first (a new entry reported as "no movement" instead of "new") and got a test of its own. One Arabic string added (machine-translated). The scale benchmark's division line now times a deep page.
- **Not done / limits:** the overview `/rankings` still shows the top 5 of each division (a summary, linking to the full page); `/all-time/[list]` is still top 25 by design; the matchmaking and preview pages still use the top 200 of a division for their own lookups (not changed); pound-for-pound is still a short list; no real feed has been paged through, only the generated league.


## 81. A partial load no longer shows a wrong record (round 42, 2026-10-04)

- **Why:** the staged backfill (§74) and a plan with a short history both leave some fighters with fewer fights loaded than they have had. The page used to add up the fights it held, so a 19-0-1 fighter whose only loaded fight was a win was shown as 1-0: a false record, which is why the load gate refused partial leagues unless overridden.
- **What changed:** the adapter keeps each fighter's career record as the vendor states it (`careerRecord`), the ingest stores it (`boxers.vendor_wins/losses/draws`, kept when a later run has none), and one function, `careerView` in `lib/world.ts`, decides what a page shows: the vendor's total when the loaded fights give fewer wins, losses or draws and none more; otherwise the loaded record (they agree, there is no total, or the loaded fights exceed the total: the vendor's totals trail its results, and a page does not pick a side). `recordStr` uses it, so every card, ranking, preview, search result, share image and AI sentence follows. The fighter page labels it: "the record is the career total from the data supplier; Ringside holds N of those M fights, so the fight list, knockouts, rating and rates are built from those N only", and counts the fights "in all". The coverage page counts fighters with a stated total and fighters held in part. The gate's wording now says what a page really does with a partial fighter.
- **Not changed, on purpose:** the rating, knockout count, rates and the fight list are still built from the fights held, so a partial fighter's Elo rests on fewer fights than the record suggests; the page says so, and the real remedy is the rest of the fetch. A conflict (more loaded than the total) still shows the loaded record and is for the check to report.
- **Checked:** unit tests of the rule (every direction, including a mix of fewer and more), an end-to-end test (stand-in vendor, a 40-fighter selection of 120, through the adapter, the database and the world: every record shown equals the vendor's total, with both partial and complete fighters present), a fighter with no total, each safeguard broken once and caught; `npm run smoke -- --feed partial` renders every kind of page on a league of that shape.

## 79. The smoke run can crawl, scale up and check the real league (round 43, 2026-10-04)
Rounds 40 to 42 each found a page that was fine on the demo league and wrong on a bigger one (a list cut off at 50, 36 or 15). The smoke run only looked at one page of each kind on a league of 968 fighters, so this round gave it three ways to look harder, and then used them.
- **`npm run smoke -- --crawl N`** adds up to N of every kind of page with a parameter (fighters, bouts, events, people, organisations, previews, belts), plus a head to head for sampled bouts, every division for both sexes, every all-time list and every fight-of-the-year; the same pages for the same league and seed (`crawlRoutes` in `lib/smoke.ts`), the extreme fighters first (most and fewest fights, oldest, one with facts missing). **`--scale N`** runs it on a league N times the demo's size (20 is 19,360 fighters and 159,296 bouts; it loads in 16 s), and lists every page that takes a second or more. **`--database FILE`** checks a league you already have, through a *copy* of the file that is deleted afterwards (the original is never opened for writing; I checked its checksum before and after on `~/ringside-real/real.db`). The leak check for English on the Arabic pages now also runs on the empty league.
- **What it found:** on the generated leagues, nothing broke: **10,461 renders (5,235 pages in both languages, every fighter, with two thirds of fighters' facts missing) and 4,355 at 20x, no failure and no page over a second** (the slowest at scale was 234 ms, after the start-up warm-up). The two things it did find were small and real, and both are in an empty-state: the Arabic `/money` page, when no figures are loaded, printed the English file path in the middle of the sentence (found on the copy of `~/ringside-real/real.db`, which turned out to be an empty league; it is now marked as English text, as the privacy page does), and the Arabic Data page printed the provider's name (`file`, `demo`, `licensed`) as plain English and a command without the English marker (both marked now). Those are exactly what the empty league's Arabic pages show, so CI's `smoke -- --feed empty` now guards them.
- **Checked:** types, lint, 682 tests (681 pass, 1 skipped as before; one new test for the crawl list: same pages every time, another seed other pages, every kind of dynamic page, the extremes always in, no page twice, everyone when everyone is asked for), build, smoke 165/119/90/165 on the four feeds, 1 Arabic string changed (the money sentence, with the path now in a marker). 10 deliberate breaks of `crawlRoutes` each failed a test except one that cannot (a sample the size of the list takes everything either way).

- **Not done / limits:** nothing at the real league's real size has been crawled (the copy of `real.db` has 61 pages' worth); a crawl looks for errors, empty text, slow pages and English on Arabic pages, not for wrong numbers; the 20x league has generated names, so its Arabic pages are not checked for English words; `--scale` needs a lot of memory to generate (it was run with a 12 GB limit).


## 80. An automated accessibility sweep of every kind of page (2026-10-04)
- **Method:** the route list of `smokeRoutes` (79 pages on the demo league, 59 on `--feed partial`), each in English and Arabic at 390 and 1280 px, on a production server seeded the way `scripts/smoke.ts` does it (clock 2026-10-03): 310 and 236 page loads. axe-core 4.13 (installed in a scratch directory, not in `package.json`) with the wcag2a, wcag2aa, wcag21a, wcag21aa and best-practice tags, run 1.5 s after load so entrance animations had finished. There is no theme switch (dark only), so there is no light run. A second pass opened the phone menu drawer and the search palette (with results, with no match, and empty) on three pages in both languages.
- **Found (violations):** `color-contrast` on `/events/{cancelled card}`: the whole bout card was dimmed with `opacity-60`, so the red "Cancelled" chip measured 2.72:1 (9 chips, 2 widths). Then, once the sweep reached the palette: `aria-required-children` and `listitem` on the search palette's "Nothing matches that." and "Searching…" messages, which were `<li>`s directly inside a `role="listbox"`. On the partial league: `aria-prohibited-attr`, a `div aria-label="Last five results"` with no role, on `/previews/{id}`, empty when a fighter has no results (4 pages).
- **Fixed:** the event page dims only the headshots of a cancelled bout, not the text (the chip and the rest are at full contrast, and "Cancelled" is still written); the palette's two messages are paragraphs outside the listbox (the polite status line already announces the same words); the form row is a `role="group"` and is not drawn when it has no results; the division ranking table is not drawn when it has no rows (a labelled table with headers and no cells). No string was added or changed, so the i18n key files are unchanged. After: **0 violations on all 325 + 251 page loads.**
- **Left, and why:** axe's "incomplete" results are all text over the card gradients, the side rail's current item and the red logo tile ("could not determine the background"; the contrast of those tokens is already enforced by `tests/a11y.test.ts`), plus `link-in-text-block` on gradient cards; not checked by hand. One `th-has-data-cells` "needs review" remains for an empty leaderboard table (`/people` on a league with no trainers): the page shows headers with no rows and no sentence; it needs a new string and a decision on wording, so it is for the owner. Not tested: a screen reader, signed-in pages, pages beyond the sampled route list.

## 85. The page checker, hardened after a security review (round 43, 2026-10-04)
