- **What was reviewed:** the code that takes input from outside or calls outside services: the correction-report API and its source-quote checker, the picture and Wikidata resolvers, the vendor adapter. Authorisation, CSRF, rate limits, SQL, markup and link injection, and the handling of the API key came out sound (each checked in the code, not assumed).
- **Five real problems in the quote checker (`lib/research/fetcher.ts`, `netguard.ts`), all closed:** (1) each robots.txt rule became a regular expression, so a reporter's own robots.txt (`Disallow: /*a*a*a*a*a*a*a*a*a*a*a*c`) and a long path made one editor click stall the whole server for minutes; rules are now matched by a linear scan, checked equal to the old behaviour on 6,000 random rules, and a robots.txt is believed only up to 200 rules a group, 200 characters a rule. (2) robots.txt was fetched with redirects followed and no host check, a blind request to internal addresses (cloud metadata, localhost) from a public host's redirect; it is now fetched as the page is: same site only, at most 3 hops, each hop through the host check, anything else read as "stay away". (3) a page or robots.txt was read whole before being cut to 2 MB; bodies are now read up to the cap and the stream cancelled. (4) a Crawl-delay of two million seconds hung the request; it is clamped to 10 s. (5) IPv4 inside IPv6 (`::ffff:7f00:1`, 6to4, the old site-local range) was judged public; the address is now parsed and judged as the IPv4 it carries (not reachable today, hardening). Also: two vendor error texts are now scrubbed of the API key like the rest.
- **Not closed, on purpose:** DNS rebinding. The host is looked up once to check it and again to connect, so an attacker's DNS could answer public first and internal second; the page text is only used to test whether a quote is on the page, so the leak is a yes/no shown to editors, and only for addresses a reporter typed. Closing it means connecting to the checked address (pinning it in the HTTP agent), a larger change; noted in `netguard.ts`.
- **Not reviewed:** deployment (whether the reverse proxy strips `X-Forwarded-For`, which the per-client AI limit trusts, though the daily budget does not), the sports-database ingest validators, the Arabic review tooling.
- **Checked:** 9 tests (the equivalence corpus, the slow rule, the caps, the redirect cases including that the internal address is never requested and that robots.txt is asked for with manual redirects, the body cap with a stream that is cancelled, the Crawl-delay ceiling, IPv6 in every spelling, the key scrub), each fix removed in turn and a test caught it.

## 86. The long lists on an organisation's and a person's page can be read to the end (round 44, 2026-10-04)
After `/people`, `/orgs` and the division rankings (§75, §77, §78), the same cut-off lived one level down. A promotion's page showed its 12 latest events, a sanctioning body's its 15 latest title fights, a judge's or referee's page the 12 latest bouts, and the fighters table stopped at 50 or 40 with "Showing the 50 most recent of N" and no next page. Unlike the lists at scale, these show on the demo league: the busiest promotion has 84 events (72 hidden), the busiest body 293 title fights (278 hidden), and the busiest judge or referee officiated 389 bouts (377 hidden).
- **Now:** every one of those lists pages, each with its **own query key** so a page with several keeps the others where they are (`lib/section-page.ts`, `sectionHref`): `/orgs/[slug]` has `fighters` (50 a page), `events` (12) and, for a body, `fights` (15); `/people/[slug]` has `stable` and `clients` (40), `judged` and `refereed` (12). The previous/next links are the ones the other lists use. The gym or promotion's "current roster" says "Top 12 of N by rating; everyone is in the table below". `TenureTable` takes an optional pager (without one it behaves as before).
- **Found by the browser sweep, not by a test:** two lists on one page gave two navigation landmarks both named "Pages" (axe `landmark-unique`). `Pager` now takes a name, and every list's pager has its own ("Pages of fighters", "Pages of events", "Pages of title fights", "Pages of clients", "Pages of bouts scored", "Pages of bouts refereed").
- **Checked:** types, lint, 689 tests (688 pass, 1 skipped as before; 4 new for `sectionHref`: page 1 has no key, the other lists keep their page, a repeated or empty key, escaping), smoke 177/121/90/177/128 on the five feeds (12 new routes: the busiest promotion's second page of events, which must show the 13th event; a page past the end; the busiest body's second page of title fights; the busiest judge's and referee's second pages; a trainer's stable), and a browser sweep (axe, overflow, text spacing, clipping) of seven pages in English and Arabic at 1280 and 375 px, all clean after the landmark fix. 5 deliberate breaks of `sectionHref` each failed a test. Seven Arabic strings added (machine-translated). The page wiring (which list gets which key and size) is covered by the smoke routes and the sweep, not by a unit test.
- **Not done / limits:** the trainer's "Fighters over time" chart still shows the latest 16 (it says "latest 16 of N"); a fighter's own fight history and the event and bout pages were not audited for the same cut-off; the lists have no name filter (only paging); the sanctioning body's "current titleholders" is one card per division, all of them.

- **Not done / limits:** **correction (round 45): `~/ringside-real/real.db` is an empty database (0 fighters, 0 bouts, no ingest run), not a small real league: the vendor cache beside it (1,597 fetched files) has not been loaded into it. The "real league copy" run therefore exercised only the pages an empty league shows, and the 122 renders say nothing about real fighters;** the money-page leak was found there because an empty league is exactly where that sentence shows. Nothing real has been crawled yet; a crawl looks for errors, empty text, slow pages and English on Arabic pages, not for wrong numbers; the 20x league has generated names, so its Arabic pages are not checked for English words; `--scale` needs a lot of memory to generate (it was run with a 12 GB limit).

## 87. The palette's exact match no longer folds every name on every keystroke (round 45, 2026-10-04)
The limit written down in §68: ⌘K's exact path folded the text of every person, organisation and event on every keystroke (`normalize` on each, then a substring test), about 18 ms at 19,360 fighters that nobody had needed to cut yet, and it is the first thing a visitor does on every page.
- **Measured on the 20x league** (4,920 people, 12,270 events, 1,296 organisations; queries made of names that exist: a surname, a fighter's full name, an event's first word, an organisation's name and its first four letters, "rank", "las"): **4.4 to 18.2 ms per keystroke before, 0.4 to 1.3 ms now** (a surname 4.9 to 0.5 ms; a fighter's full name 17.5 to 1.3 ms; an organisation 17.8 to 0.7 ms).
- **How:** `exactOf` in `lib/search.ts` folds each person, organisation and event once (the English and, with a table, the Arabic spelling; events newest first, called-off ones left out) per world and per table of translated names, the same arrangement as the near-spelling index beside it (an empty table is one table). The match itself is unchanged: every word typed must be in the text, text that starts with what was typed ranks first, then ties by name, and the five newest matching events.
- **What it does not change:** a query that matches nothing exactly still goes to the near-spelling guess, which costs about 8 to 17 ms at this size (the profile put almost all of it in `editDistance` and `nearTexts` over the whole vocabulary); that path has its own limit and is untouched. A typed query that is a typo is the only one that pays it.
- **Checked:** types, lint, 688 tests (687 pass, 1 skipped as before; 3 new in `tests/search-exact.test.ts`: the indexed match against a plain version that folds everything per query, over 340 queries made from the league's names (accents, capitals, spaces, partial words, strangers), then over Arabic names of people, organisations and events; and that the index is built once per world and table), build, smoke 165/119/90/165/128. 10 deliberate breaks of the index each failed a test (the first run of the Arabic comparison let one through, because its queries never reached an organisation's Arabic name; they do now). The benchmark gained a palette line. No page changed, so there was no browser sweep.
- **Not done / limits:** one machine, one generated league, Node's single thread with nothing else running; the first keystroke after a restart builds the index (about 10 ms) and is not warmed with the other aggregates; the fighter part of the palette was already fast and is not touched.

## 88. Names the way a real league has them (round 46, 2026-10-04)
The generated league has plain two-word names, so nothing in the tests had ever typed a name the way a real feed spells one. I made 22 invented names in real shapes (letters that do not fold by accent such as Ł, Ø, Đ, ı and ß; an apostrophe; a suffix; a middle name; a particle such as van der, dos, De La or bin; a hyphen) and typed each the way a fan would (accents off, apostrophe dropped, suffix spelt out or left off, middle name or particle left out, particles run together). **The first run had 26 failures** (a failure of the palette's search and of the Ask planner on the same typing counted separately). A few were artefacts of my own script, such as a variant made by deleting an accented letter outright ("Sal lvarez"), and are not in the test; the realistic ones, listed in `tests/real-names.test.ts`, failed in the palette's search (letters like Đ, a suffix as "Junior" or "3rd", "vanderberg") and, more often, in the Ask planner (anything but the full name).
- **Search (⌘K, the fighter list):** `normalize` (`lib/fighter-search.ts`) now folds the letters that accent-stripping does not touch (ł, đ, ð, ø, ı, æ, œ, ß, þ become l, d, d, o, i, ae, oe, ss, th) and the written and typed forms of a suffix meet (junior = jr, senior = sr, 2nd = ii, 3rd = iii, 4th = iv; whole words only), so "lukasz rozanski" finds Łukasz Różański, "Le Van Duc" Lê Văn Đức, "Roy Jones Junior" Roy Jones Jr. and "Eddie Hearn 3rd" Eddie Hearn III. The word index (`lib/fuzzy.ts`) joins three neighbouring words as well as two, so "vanderberg" finds van der Berg. Both help the fighter search, the forgiving guesses and every place that folds a name.
- **Ask:** the planner reads a name the other ways it is typed (`nameVariants` in `lib/ask/rules.ts`): without the suffix ("Roy Jones" for Roy Jones Jr.), with the middle names left out ("Maria Garcia" for Maria Elena Garcia), without the particles, with the particles run onto the surname ("Sven vanderberg"), and any of these without the apostrophe ("Conor OBrien"). **A variant is used only when it points to one fighter**: if two fighters shorten to the same name, or the shortened name is another fighter's full name, it belongs to the full name or to nobody ("Jose Castillo" with a Jose Luis and a Jose Antonio Castillo gets no answer rather than a guess). This lifts a limit written in §71 (a middle initial left out was not guessed at); "who is Yazan Al-Ghamdi" now finds Yazan H. Al-Ghamdi.
- **At 20x (19,360 fighters):** the 225-question battery gives the same plans as before, 15,120 first-name-and-ordinary-word decoys name nobody, as before; the planner's indexes take 160 ms to build once per league (92 ms before). The name battery (`ask:names`) and the search battery are unchanged.
- **Checked:** types, lint, 708 tests (707 pass, 1 skipped as before; 7 new in `tests/real-names.test.ts`: the search on 18 typed variants, the planner on those and five more, ambiguity, ordinary words like "junior welterweights" not read as a suffix, `normalize` letter by letter, `nameVariants`, and a fake league for "a variant that is another fighter's full name"), build, smoke 177/121/90/177/128. 19 deliberate breaks each failed a test, except that a variant missing from only the exact index, or only the near index, is covered by the other (both removed together fail), so those two are equivalent breaks.
- **Not done / limits:** the names are invented in real shapes, not real fighters' (the real league is not loaded); a surname alone, a first name alone and a surname-first order are still not names (ambiguous); a nickname is not a name here (single-word nicknames such as "Canelo" risk matching ordinary words, and the demo has no nickname unique to one fighter, so there was nothing to measure); the Vietnamese order (family name first) and a respelling such as Djordje for Đorđe are not handled; Arabic names get no variants.

## 89. Asking about a group no longer answers for everybody (round 47, 2026-10-04)
Round 33 found that "most knockouts in 2024" was answered with the all-time list and fixed the year. The same wrong answer, a list with the qualifier quietly dropped, turned out to be the rule for every other qualifier. Written as questions and run on the planner as it was (batch 7, 37 questions, `npm run ask:battery -- --batch 7`): **12 of 37 right (32%); 25 answered for everybody or for something else.** "Most knockouts among southpaws", "most wins among Mexican fighters", "highest ko rate among active fighters" and "knockout leaders in Japan" gave the all-time lists; "most knockouts in the 2010s", "longest win streak in the last five years" and "most title defenses this decade" the all-time lists; "fastest knockout before 2015" the fastest knockouts *of 2015*; "top 5 southpaws" the pound-for-pound top five; "most knockouts in Europe" the all-time list.
- **Now:** a record list (`record_list`) takes only a sex and a weight division. Asked about any other group (a stance, a country, active or retired, undefeated, an age) it is either a **fighter search that honours the group, sorted by what the list counts** (wins, knockouts, knockout rate: "most knockouts among southpaws" is southpaws sorted by knockouts; a knockout rate asks for at least 15 wins, as the list does, unless the reader gave a minimum) or, for the lists a search cannot reproduce (win streaks, title defences and wins, reigns, upsets, fastest knockouts), **no answer, with a reason**. A **stretch of time** ("since 2018", "in the 2010s", "the 80s", "the nineties", "this decade", "the last five years", "recently") gets no answer either, and is no longer read as the year it mentions. A **region** (Europe, Asia, South America, "African") is not a country the data has: no answer. "Top 5 southpaws" is a fighter search, not a division ranking. An unanswered question says why: the answer page and the API now carry a `hint` of `year`, `span` or `group`, each with its sentence, in Arabic too.
- **Measured on a batch written after that one was fitted** (batch 8, 31 questions in other words: lefties, Ukrainians, British, Filipino, "among fighters over 35", "the nineties", "in Asia", "in the UK"): **main 8 of 31 (26%); the fixes made from batch 7 alone 28 of 31 (90%)**; the three misses ("the nineties", "the 80s", a region with no list in the question) were then fixed, so batch 8 is a regression guard now. All eight batteries (421 questions) pass, and on the 20,000-fighter league **all 353 older questions get byte-identical plans before and after**.
- **Three more wrong filters found on the way, fixed:** "old" matched inside words, so "who holds the heavyweight belt" set a minimum age of 35 (whole words only now); "technical knockouts" set the style "Technician" (now only "technical" on its own); "Mexicans" and "Germans" were not nationalities (a plural is now); "most technical knockouts" is the knockouts list.
- **Checked:** types, lint, 712 tests (711 pass, 1 skipped as before; 4 new in `tests/ask-battery.test.ts`: groups, time and region, the reasons and the API hint, the filter words), build, smoke 177/121/90/177/128, and the three messages read from a real server's HTML. 19 deliberate breaks of the new rules each failed a test (a twentieth, a region check inside the list branch, was dead behind the general region check and was removed).
- **Not done / limits:** a style as a group ("most wins among counter-punchers") is not recognised as one (the Arabic word for knockout sets a style and would have wrongly refused every Arabic knockouts question), so it is answered as before; the regions are a fixed list, not every place that is not a country; "fighters who debuted since 2020" is a search and keeps its year; the AI planner (with a key) is still not measured; the expectations are my judgment of what a reader wants.

## 90. A question about one fact of one fighter is answered with that fact (round 48, 2026-10-04)
Asking the rule-based planner (no API key) about a named fighter gave the same one-line profile whatever was asked: "how tall is X", "what is X's reach", "how old is X", "is X a southpaw", "where is X from", "who trains X", "when did X last fight", "when does X fight next" and "how many fights has X had" all answered "X is a 34-year-old Light Flyweight from Philippines: 30-0-0, rated 1742." Worse, **"what is X's knockout rate" answered with the knockout-rate list for everybody** ("Highest knockout rate: Maximilian Hartmann leads with 81%"), because the list's wording was matched before the name was looked at: a wrong answer that looks right.
- **Now:** the `fighter` tool takes an optional `about` (height, reach, age, stance, country, division, trainer, gym, last fight, next fight, record, knockouts, rating, belts), and the answer is the sentence for that fact ("Ramil Abad is 157 cm tall.", "Ramil Abad's next fight is against … on Nov 15, 2026.", "Ramil Abad has 9 knockouts in 30 wins (30%)."), with the full profile and the recent-fights table still below it. **Where the data does not give the fact the answer says so** ("The data does not give a height for Pablo Quintana.", "The data has no current head trainer for …", "No upcoming fight is scheduled for …", "… holds no current belt.") and nothing is filled in. The next fight is the earliest bout that is still on (a bout that fell off the card, or on a called-off one, is not counted), the last fight the latest completed one, and the gym and trainer the current ones, not an old stint. The planner (`FACT_ASKED` in `lib/ask/rules.ts`) reads the fact from the wording, English and Arabic, most specific first ("how many knockouts" is knockouts, not the record), only when one fighter is named and the question has no superlative (a superlative is a list or a comparison; "most recent" is not one). A trainer named in a question that says "trainer" is still the trainers tool's. With a key the model can pass `about` too: it is declared with the tool.
- **Measured:** batch 9 (44 questions in other words than the patterns were written from: "what age is", "when was X born", "is X left-handed", "who coaches X", "what's X rated", "is X fighting soon", "who did X fight last", "how many losses has X had") scored **39 of 44 (89%)** before the patterns were touched again (main: none of them is answered with a fact, 0 of 44); the five misses (the "most" in "most recent", "coaches", "fighting soon", "fight last", "last box") were fixed. On the 20,000-fighter league the 421 older questions give **the same plans before and after, ignoring `about`**, which 16 of them now carry.
- **Checked:** types, lint, 717 tests (716 pass, 1 skipped as before; 4 new in `tests/ask-facts.test.ts` (each of 14 facts in two or three wordings against the league's own numbers, the fighter's own knockout rate against the list, a superlative beside a fact word, the same in Arabic, the declared argument) and 1 in `tests/ask-facts-unknown.test.ts` (nine unknowns and, on a record made by hand, the exact sentences for a record of 1-1-1 with a knockout, the earliest of two cards ahead beside a called-off one, the current gym and trainer beside gone ones, and the last fight)), 24 new Arabic strings (machine-translated), build, smoke 177/121/90/177/128. 25 deliberate breaks of the patterns and the sentences each failed a test (the first run let five through: a superlative guard, the cancelled and earliest rules, a knockout percentage and a fight count, a gym stint; each now has a case, and a cancelled-bout check was dead behind `upcoming` and was removed).
- **Not done / limits:** one fact per question (a question with two, such as height and reach, gets the first the patterns find); no fact for the style or the streak, and "has X ever lost" is the profile; a trainer, judge or referee is not asked about this way; the Arabic wording of each fact is mine and unreviewed; the AI planner is still not measured.

## 91. Two fighters and a fact between them, and four more facts about one (round 49, 2026-10-04)
Round 48 answered one fact about one fighter. Probing the next kinds of question about fighters (18 of them, on the planner as it was) found more answers that look right and are not: **"who is taller, A or B", "who has the longer reach", "who is older", "who has more knockouts" and "who has more wins" were all answered with the head-to-head prediction** ("Toss-up: A 53%. Expect a stoppage. They have never met."), which answers "who would win"; **"how many title fights has A won" was answered with the league's 660 title fights**, A forgotten (the bouts tool has no fighter); "how many times has A been knocked down" with A's record; and "is A retired", "is A undefeated", "has A ever been knocked out", "who is A's manager" with the generic profile.
- **Two fighters and one fact:** named two fighters and asked something both have a value for, the answer is their two answers side by side, in the order they were named ("Mateo Vidal is 175 cm tall. Diego Ramos is 175 cm tall."): two `fighter` calls with the same `about`. It covers every fact except the next and last fight (two names and "when does A fight B" is about a bout between them), and a question that is about how they met ("against", "beat", "beaten", "met", "would win", "who wins", "each other", "ever fought") is still a head to head, as are "A vs B", "compare A and B" and "who would win" with no fact in them.
- **Four more facts (18 in all):** `title_fights` ("A has won 3 of 5 title fights." or "has not fought for a title on record"), `stopped` ("has been stopped 2 times (losses in all: 4)" or "has never been knocked out or stopped on record"), `status` (active or retired, with a record that is unbeaten or has losses: "is active and unbeaten (30-0-0)", "is active and has lost 1 time (1-1-1)", "is retired; the record is …") and `manager` (the current one, not an earlier one; "The data has no current manager for …"). "Most title fights won" is the title-wins list. "How many times has A been knocked down" is no answer (the data counts knockdowns per fight, not per fighter) and no longer the record.
- **Measured:** batch 10 (40 questions, 41 with the knockdowns case added last) scored **5 of 40 (13%)** on the planner as it was and 31 of 40 after the facts and the comparison branch, 40 of 40 after the comparative words (taller, older, more wins) were added; one expectation of mine was wrong (the order two fighters are named in) and I corrected the expectation, not the planner. **Batch 11, written after that and measured once before anything was changed for it: 23 of 30 (77%)**; its misses ("compare the ages of", "more experienced", "longer arms", "won a world title", "is A perfect", "has A lost a fight", "agent") were then added. All eleven batteries (536 questions) pass, and the 465 older questions give identical plans on the 20,000-fighter league.
- **Checked:** types, lint, 719 tests (718 pass, 1 skipped as before; 2 new in `tests/ask-facts.test.ts` (the new facts against each fighter's own bouts, and seven two-fighter comparisons with the order and the head-to-head cases) and the hand-made-record test extended: a never-stopped fighter and a stopped one, active/unbeaten/retired, a manager now and not before, a title bout that is still ahead, "which gym, A or B"), 10 Arabic strings (machine-translated, with the six plural forms where a count is spoken), build, smoke 177/121/90/177/128. 27 deliberate breaks each failed a test (the first run let two through: an upcoming title bout counted, and a comparison allowed for every fact including the last fight; each now has a case).
- **Not done / limits:** the new facts and the comparison are recognised from English wording only (an Arabic question about a manager, a title record or "is he retired" gets the profile); one fact per question; two fighters compared on a fact the data lacks for one of them says so for that one; no comparison of height and reach together; "has A fought B" stays a head to head; the AI planner is still not measured.

## 92. One backfill at a time per key, and what the first real run taught (round 44, 2026-10-04)

- **What happened:** the first real `--plan --fighters 5000` ran for hours with rate-limit waits of up to ten minutes, because an older copy of the backfill, begun before the rate-limit fix and forgotten in another terminal, was spending the same 500-an-hour allowance (and, being the old code, retrying a refusal in seconds and skipping the fighter). Stopped, the plan finished at its designed pace. The real feed: 44,265 fights, 11,024 events, 35,235 fighters, read in 12 date windows.
- **What changed:** a lock per API key on the machine (`acquireBackfillLock`, a file named by a hash of the key, holding the process id): a second live run refuses to start and says which process holds the key and the command it is running; a lock whose process is gone, or that cannot be read, is taken over; a run never blocks itself; it is given back at exit and on Ctrl-C. The runbook has a section on the lessons (the allowance belongs to the key, the key in the shell, `pbpaste`, one command at a time, the second checkout, the plan with `--cache-dir`).
- **Not covered:** another machine (the lock is local); a run killed with SIGKILL leaves a stale lock, which the next run takes over.
- **Checked:** 3 tests on the lock (the rule, staleness and unreadable files and self, the real command refused while another holds the key and not stopped by a stale lock); each rule broken in turn and a test caught it; the full-size rehearsal still passes with the lock.


## 93. What a league loaded in part does to the rankings (round 45, 2026-10-04)

- **Measured, on a league shaped like the real feed** (35,235 fighters, 44,265 fights, the 5,000 most recent taken through the real adapter): the fights are spread thinly, 3,239 fighters with one loaded fight, 1,435 with two, 4 with five or more. A ranking needs five loaded fights (pound-for-pound eight), so **0 fighters were ranked in any division and 0 in pound-for-pound**, and the highest-rated fighters were ones with three or four loaded fights. The rankings pages showed a blank card in every division and "Not enough qualifying fighters" under all of them, with no reason given.
- **Why the rule stays:** a rating is worked out from the fights Ringside holds, so the fights held are the honest measure of how much it can be trusted; letting a fighter in on the supplier's career total while his rating rests on one fight would put thinly-rated fighters at the top of a ranking.
- **What changed:** the rankings page and each division page say why, when most careers are partial (`rankingDepth`, in `lib/rankings.ts`: the share of fighters with a supplier total larger than the fights held, above a half): "These rankings count only the fights Ringside holds. Most fighters here have only their most recent fights on record so far, so few reach the five fights a ranking needs; more qualify as the history is added." An empty division says "No fighter in this division has the five fights on record that a ranking needs yet." `careerView` moved to its own file (`lib/career.ts`) so the rankings can use it without a circular import; `lib/world.ts` re-exports it. `npm run smoke -- --feed partial` now checks both pages show their note.
- **Regression tests for the four accessibility fixes of §80** (the palette listbox holds only options, no bare div carries an aria-label, a cancelled bout dims only the headshots, an empty division draws no table). Each was broken in turn and a test caught it. They are source-level, like the rest of `tests/a11y.test.ts`; they do not replace an axe run.
- **Open, for the owner:** whether to change what the rankings need while the league is partial (for example three loaded fights while most careers are partial, shown as provisional), or to hold the ranking pages back until more of the league is loaded. The real answer comes from the first load: `select bouts per fighter` on the real database shows how many fighters will ever have five fights in this feed (the whole feed averages 2.5 fights a fighter).

## 94. A place in a ranking, a measure to sort by, and a count (round 50, 2026-10-04)
Three more kinds of question that were answered with something that looks right and is not. On the planner as it was (batch 12, 36 questions): **8 of 35 right (23%)**. "Who is ranked number two at welterweight" and "who is ranked third in the middleweight division" were answered with **the number one** ("Welterweight rankings: Marcus Larkin is number one"); "who is the second best lightweight" with the highest rated; **"who is the tallest heavyweight" with the highest rated heavyweight, "who is the oldest active boxer" with the highest rated fighter of 35 and over, "the youngest heavyweight" with the highest rated fighter of 26 and under**; "who is the youngest champion", "the average age of a champion", "which gym has the most champions" and "who trains the most champions" with the list of every champion; and "how many fighters are there", "how many fights have there been", "how many knockouts were there in total" and "how many events in 2024" with no answer at all.
- **A place in a ranking:** `rankings` takes a `position` (2 to 25) and the answer is the fighter in that place ("Welterweight rankings: X is number 2 (…, rated …)"), by number ("number two", "number 5"), ordinal ("third", "3rd", "second place") or "sits fifth", in a division or pound for pound ("in the world"). The number one is the plain ranking, and a place nobody holds ("who is number 25 at flyweight", with seven ranked) is "Nobody is ranked there." An ordinal that is not about a ranking ("the second welterweight fight") is not a place. (`normalize` had folded "3rd" into "iii", from round 46; the place is read from the question as typed.)
- **A measure to sort by:** the fighter search sorts by height (`height`, tallest first; `shortest`) and by age (`age` is oldest first, `youngest` youngest first), and "tallest", "shortest", "youngest" and "oldest" are the order, not a filter at 35 or 26 (so "young heavyweights" is still a group of fighters 26 and under, and "oldest" no longer sets a minimum age; "shortest reach" is not "shortest"). The `age` sort had been youngest first, which nothing used; the model's plan can use all of them.
- **Champions in order of age:** `champions` takes `by: youngest | oldest` ("The youngest champion is X, 24, who holds …", champions with no known age left out and said so). The average age of anything, and which gym, trainer, manager or promoter has the most champions, are no answer: no tool aggregates them. (Round 33's battery had accepted the champions list for "youngest champion"; it was not an answer to the question.)
- **Counts:** "how many fighters / boxers" is the fighter search with its filters and its count ("966 fighters match."), "how many fights / knockouts / decisions / draws / title fights (or bouts)" the fights count with the same filters, "how many events (in 2024)" the new count of completed cards (`events` takes `year` and `when: all`: "There were N completed cards in 2024; the latest was …"). A count asked about a named fighter is that fighter's.
- **Measured on a batch written after that one was fitted** (batch 13, 28 questions: "second place", "number 3", "sits fifth", "the tallest woman boxer", "youngest unbeaten fighter", "how many shows have there been", "how many bouts ended by knockout"): **24 of 28 (86%)** before anything was changed for it; the misses ("second place", "sits fifth", "title bouts", an average height) were fixed. All thirteen batteries (600 questions) pass; on the 20,000-fighter league **530 of the 536 older questions give identical plans**, and the six that differ are the intended ones (oldest active ×3 now sorted by age, the youngest and the oldest champion, the tallest heavyweight).
- **Checked:** types, lint, 726 tests (725 pass, 1 skipped as before; 4 new in `tests/ask-places.test.ts` against the league's own numbers: the fighter in each place, the first row of each sort, every count, the youngest and oldest champion; one earlier test changed because "oldest" is no longer a minimum age), 13 Arabic strings (with the plural forms of the card counts), build, smoke 177/121/90/177/128. 28 deliberate breaks each failed a test (the first runs let some through that were dead code, now removed or given a case).
- **Not done / limits:** the heaviest is not here (no weights in the data); a place is read for rankings only (not "the third highest KO rate"); two measures in one question ("tallest and youngest") get the last; the counts are of what the fighter search and the fights tool count (fighters with at least one fight; completed fights); the Arabic wording of places, sorts and counts is not recognised (the new sentences are translated, the questions are English); the AI planner is still not measured.

## 110. A dead link offers the fighter you probably meant (round 50, 2026-10-04)
`lib/not-found.ts`, `app/[locale]/not-found.tsx`, `proxy.ts`, `tests/not-found.test.ts`
- A fighter's address that misses (an old slug, a mistyped one, a name changed at the source) used to end at "Not on the card" and a link home. The page now turns `/boxers/noura-al-ghamd` back into "noura al ghamd", asks the name search (which already forgives a slip, a missing letter, a different order) and offers up to four fighters who have fought, plus a search box carrying the guess. Any other dead address keeps the plain page with the search box.
- **The path reaches the page through the proxy**, which sets an `x-pathname` request header the way it sets the nonce: it is overwritten on every request, never taken from the visitor. The page shows names from the database only; the slug is never echoed, and what reaches the search is letters, digits and spaces, 60 characters at most (a raw slash makes it another address).
- Still a real 404 with `noindex`. The smoke run now requests a mistyped fighter link and fails unless the page offers the fighter (English; the Arabic page offers the name in Arabic).
- Checked: unit tests with four mutations (any address accepted, brackets let through, no length limit, locale prefix not stripped), each of which fails a test; the smoke run on the demo (179/179), partial (132/132) and empty (90/90) leagues; the page read in English at 1440 px and Arabic at 390 px.

## 95. The numbers in a plain-English fighter search (round 51, 2026-10-04)
The rule-based search (no API key) read a sentence for a few numbers and ignored the rest, and some of what it read it read as the wrong thing. On the search as it was (`npm run search:nl`, `tests/search-nl-battery.ts`, answers checked against the league's own fighters, not against a filter list): the eight comparisons it was built for were right (8 of 8), the other 44 kinds of sentence were **0 of 44**. The worst were wrong in a way that looks like an answer: "fighters with more than 10 losses" was **the 27 undefeated fighters** (the "0 losses" inside "10 losses"); "heavyweights with a losing record" was every heavyweight (44, not 22); "fighters with 10 or more knockouts and no losses" was 60 (not 1); "fewer than 10 wins" was *at least* 10; "under 15 wins" was "at least 15 wins, aged 15 and under"; "between 20 and 30 wins" was "30 or more"; "champions from Mexico" was the list of champions of everywhere; "retired champions" the same list. Most of the rest fell through to a name search and found no one.
- **One quantity parser** (`lib/search-quantities.ts`): every number is a minimum, a maximum or both on the measure it counts, and the comparison word keeps its direction. Measures: wins, losses, knockouts, fights (bouts, "fought N times"), age, height (cm, feet and inches), reach. Words: at least / no fewer than / N or more / N+ / plus; more than / over / above; at most / no more than / up to / N or fewer; fewer than / under / below / younger / shorter; exactly; between A and B; A to B. "Lost 5 times" counts losses, "won 20 times" wins, a bare "times" fights. "None of something" in words ("no losses", "never lost", "winless", "never won by knockout", "no knockouts") is a maximum of 0.
- **Strict means strict:** "over 35" is 36 and up (as "over 33" already was), **"under 25" is 24 and down** (it had been 25), "over 6 feet" is 183 cm and up (6 ft is 182.88), "after 2015" is 2016 on and "before 2005" 2004 and back (both had included the year; "since 2015" still does). "At least" and "at most" keep their number. A bare number is still a minimum ("20 wins" is 20 or more; the chip says so), except an age ("30 years old", "aged 30").
- **Not a number of anything:** "top 5 knockout artists" (a style; it had been read as 5 knockouts), "80% knockout rate", "above 60 %", "over 200 pounds", "over 70", "top 10 knockouts". Two limits on one measure keep the stricter.
- **New filters** (`Filters`, `applyFilters`, `sanitizeFilters` for the model's own JSON, the AI prompt, the `fighters` tool's arguments, chips): `maxWins`, `maxKOs`, `maxLosses`, `minBouts`, `maxBouts`, `maxReach`, `minHeight`, `maxHeight`, `record` (winning: more wins than losses; losing: the reverse) and `champion` (`current`: holds a live belt, the same ones the champions page lists, dormant belts not counted; `former`: held one and no longer does; `ever`: has held one). "Retired champions" and "were champions" are `ever`; "former / ex / one-time champions" `former`. A fact the data does not have (a height, a reach) never satisfies a limit on it, and a belt or team filter given no world to look in matches nobody, not everybody (the team filters had matched everybody).
- **In Ask:** "champions from Mexico", "champions over 35", "former champions" are a fighter search with the belt filter, not the champions list (which has no such cut); plain "who are the champions" still is. A record list asked about any of the new cuts ("most wins among fighters with fewer than 5 losses", "among former champions", "among fighters taller than 185 cm") is the fighter search sorted the same way, or no answer, never the list for everybody (round 47's rule); "who defended the most times" is still the defences list: "champion" names that list, it does not narrow it.
- **Measured on a second batch written after the first was fitted** (group C, 43 sentences: "lost more than 5 times", "won at least 20 times", "30 and over", "ages 22 to 26", "taller than 6 ft 2", "reach between 180 and 190", "turned pro since 2020", "women with between 5 and 10 wins"): **38 of 43 (88%)** before anything was changed for it; the five misses (lost/won N times, "N cm reach or less", two "between" ranges) were fixed. All three groups now 8/8, 44/44, 43/43.
- **Checked:** types, lint, 742 tests (741 pass, 1 skipped as before; 10 new in `tests/search-nl.test.ts`: the battery against the league, a guard that no battery truth is empty or everyone, every reading pinned, the rounding of feet, repeated limits, styles and rates, champions against the lines of live belts, the planner's routes, the model's filters, the chips in both languages, unknown heights; three older expectations changed because over/under are now strict), 12 Arabic strings, build, smoke 177/177. **Old-versus-new plans over 2,164 questions** (the whole Ask battery, the new sentences, and ten subjects × thirty cuts × five openings): 1,273 plans changed, none that had an answer lost it, and none carries a number in the question that is missing from the arguments; the changes are the qualifiers now applied. **39 deliberate mutations** (direction and rounding of each comparison, intersection of limits, suffix words, styles and ranks, feet, champion sets, unknown facts, routing): 17 survived the first tests, each got a test, and all are killed now but one that cannot differ (the order the comparison words are tried in: only one is ever set).
- **Found on the way:** "best|top" matched inside "stopped", so "boxers who were never stopped" was a request for the best (everyone, by rating); both are whole words now.
- **Not done / limits:** Arabic numbers in a search are still read only for wins, knockouts, reach and "undefeated" (the new cuts are English; an Arabic sentence with them finds no one rather than the wrong people); "never been knocked out" (a chin, not a count of knockouts won) is not a filter yet, and is deliberately not read as "no knockouts"; weight in pounds or kilograms, "in the last year" and "N rounds" are not read; a bare number with no word after it is not guessed.

## 111. Career highlights on a fighter's page (round 51, 2026-10-04)
`lib/highlights.ts`, `app/[locale]/boxers/[slug]/page.tsx`, `tests/highlights.test.ts`
- Under the hero: the **best win** (the opponent with the highest rating going in, linked to the fight), the **biggest upset** (a win over someone rated at least 100 points higher going in; absent when there is none) and the **longest winning run** (three or more; ended by a loss or a draw, "still going" if it has not ended). They come from the fights held and the pre-fight ratings the world already replays; a no-contest, a cancelled and an upcoming fight are not in a run.
- **Honest on a partial league:** the section is titled "the best of the fights we hold", and for a career held in part a line says it is worked out from the fights Ringside holds, not the whole career. A fighter with none of the three has no section.
- Checked: unit tests with five mutations (the upset threshold, best-win direction, the record filter, the streak minimum and no-contest rule, the corner the fighter was in), each of which fails a test; smoke on the demo (177/177) and partial (130/130) leagues; the section read in English at 1440 px and Arabic at 390 px.

## 96. The numbers in an Arabic fighter search, and a ranking question with a cut (round 52, 2026-10-04)
Round 51 taught the English search to read numbers; the Arabic one still read almost none. On the parser as it was (groups D, 38 sentences, in `tests/search-nl-battery.ts`, `npm run search:nl`, answers checked against the league's own fighters): **5 of 38 right (13%)**. "ملاكمون لديهم أكثر من 10 هزائم" and "ملاكمين بدون هزائم" found no one (the whole sentence became a name search); "أكثر من 20 فوزا" was read as 20 or more; "ثقيلو الوزن لديهم أكثر من 20 فوزا" lost the division and was **the list of the most wins for everybody**; "أبطال سابقون" (former champions) was the list of live champions, "أبطال من المكسيك" every Mexican; Arabic-Indic digits ("٥", "١٠") were read by no rule at all.
- **Arabic quantity parser** (`lib/search-quantities-ar.ts`, on the folded question; same measures and rules as English): wins (فوز, انتصارات), losses (هزائم, خسائر), knockouts (ضربات قاضية), fights (نزالات), age (سنة, عاما), height (سم, أطول من / أقصر من), reach (امتداد الذراعين, مدى); أكثر من / فوق / أكبر من; أقل من / تحت / دون / أصغر من; على الأقل / لا يقل عن; على الأكثر / لا يزيد عن (also as a verb on the count: "لا يزيد عدد هزائمهم عن 5", "يزيد عمرهم عن 40"); بالضبط; بين 15 و25; من 20 إلى 30; "… أو أكثر / فأكثر / أو أقل / فأقل" after the count; none in words (بدون / بلا / ليس لديهم هزائم, لم يفوزوا أبدا, بدون ضربات قاضية, "لم يفز بالضربة القاضية" is not "never won"); "فوز واحد" is exactly one; سجل رابح / خاسر. Over and under are strict (أكثر من 20 is 21 and up, تحت 25 is 24 and down); a bare count is a minimum, an age is exactly that age. Number words one to ten and the tens before a count ("أكثر من عشرين فوزا") and Arabic-Indic digits become digits first (`westernize`, also applied to the whole question in the planner, so "أفضل ٥ ملاكمين" is five); a number word that counts nothing ("أفضل ثلاثة ملاكمين") is left alone. The older loose Arabic rules for wins, knockouts and reach are gone (they read the digit after a decimal point, and ignored "more than").
- **Champions in Arabic:** `champion` is current (أبطال, بطل), former (أبطال سابقون, بطل سابق) or ever (أبطال معتزلون, كانوا أبطالا, "سبق لهم حمل حزام"); a champion who is also something else is a fighter search, as in English.
- **A division said the way people say it** is found instead of silently dropped (`DIVISION_SAYINGS_AR`: ثقيلو / ثقيلي الوزن, متوسطو الوزن, خفيفو الوزن, نصف الثقيل, فوق المتوسط, ويلتر, …; the longest phrase wins, so "نصف الثقيل" is not "الثقيل").
- **Lists:** "أكثر من 20 فوزا" is no longer taken for "who has the most wins" (the Arabic list patterns skip "more than N"); "من لديه أكثر فوزا" still is.
- **A ranking question with a cut is a search** (both languages): "top 5 welterweights with more than 10 wins", "أفضل 5 ملاكمون ويلتر لديهم بين 10 و20 فوزا", "who is ranked number two among welterweights over 30", "the best welterweights over 30 right now" were the division's ranking with the cut left out (the cuts on wins and knockouts had never been counted as one). Division and sex are the ranking's own scope; anything else makes the answer the fighter search with the same sort. A plain "top 5 welterweights" is still the ranking.
- **Measured on a second Arabic batch written after the first was fitted** (group E, 35 sentences: لا يقل / لا يزيد, "ليس لديهم", فأكثر / فأقل, دون, "من 30 إلى 35 سنة", طولهم, عمرهم, number words, Arabic-Indic digits, combinations): **32 of 35 (91%)** before anything was changed for it; the three misses (the verb forms on a count) were fixed. All five groups now 8/8, 44/44, 43/43, 38/38, 35/35.
- **Checked:** types, lint, 746 tests (745 pass, 1 skipped as before; 4 new in `tests/search-nl-ar.test.ts`: every reading pinned, the division sayings, champions, the planner's routes; the battery's Arabic groups run in `tests/search-nl.test.ts`), no new strings (the chips are round 51's), build, smoke 177/177. **Old-versus-new plans over 2,349 questions** (the Ask battery and the English sentences, ten Arabic subjects × twenty-eight cuts × five openings, English top/best questions with cuts): 1,242 changed, none that had an answer lost it, no number in a question is missing from its plan's arguments, no English plan changed except the ranking-with-a-cut ones above. **40 deliberate mutations:** 16 survived the first tests; the tests added killed 11, four were code that could not matter (comparison words spelled with ى, which the folding never leaves; a duplicate age range; a unit in a lookahead) and were removed, and one cannot differ (every match carries a comparison group, so its fallback is never used).
- **Not done / limits:** Arabic years of debut ("احترف في 2015"), feet and inches, and "never knocked out" are not read; the Arabic number words are one to ten and the tens (not "إحدى عشرة"); a ranking-with-a-cut question asking for a place ("number two among welterweights over 30") gets the filtered list, not just that place; Arabic-Indic digits are read in the fighter search and the planner but not in the other pages' search boxes; machine Arabic wording of the new sentences has had no native review.

## 113. "Boxing, explained" for newcomers (round 53, 2026-10-04)
`app/[locale]/learn/page.tsx`
- A page for the person who does not yet know what a split decision or a unified champion is, in the words the site itself uses: how a fight is won (KO, TKO, corner retirement, disqualification, decision, technical decision, no contest), how the judges score (the 10-point must system, then unanimous, split, majority, draw), how to read a record (and what it means when Ringside shows the supplier's career total), the weight classes, belts and champions (four bodies, unified, undisputed, interim, super), and where Ringside's Elo-style ratings come from. Each section links to the page where the thing can be seen. In the navigation (Discover, "Boxing explained"), the sitemap and the smoke run; a table of contents of anchors at the top.
- Numbers inside the prose (`25-3-1`, `10–10`) go through placeholders so the Arabic page isolates them left to right (the smoke run's backwards-record check caught the first version).
- Arabic written for all of it, using the site's fixed boxing terms (`i18n/glossary.json`) and the existing division names; not yet read by a native speaker, and a reviewer should read this page first because it is prose, not labels.
- Checked: smoke on the demo (187/187) and empty (100/100) leagues in both languages; the page read in English at 1440 px.

## 97. A fighter's record and form in a plain-English search: stopped, draws, streaks, last fight (round 53, 2026-10-04)
The search could cut by counts (round 51) but not by what a record says besides them, and three of those questions were answered with the wrong thing. On the search as it was (groups F, 38 sentences, `tests/search-nl-battery.ts`, `npm run search:nl`, answers checked against the league's own fighters): **0 of 38 right (0%)**. "Boxers who lost by knockout at least 3 times" was **every boxer with 3 or more fights** (945), "a 4 fight winning streak" was 4 or more fights (934), "unbeaten in their last 5" was "undefeated" (27, not the 78 who are), "fighters who lost their last fight" was the list of recent events; the rest ("never been knocked out", "with a draw", "fought in the last 6 months") fell to a name search and found no one.
- **New filters** (`Filters`, `applyFilters`, `sanitizeFilters`, the AI prompt, the `fighters` tool's arguments, chips): `minStopped` / `maxStopped` (times lost by knockout, technical knockout or corner stoppage: the record's own `koLosses`), `minDraws` / `maxDraws`, `minWinStreak` / `minLossStreak` (the current run, so nobody on a winning streak is on a losing one), `unbeatenIn` (no loss in the last N fights that count in the record, and at least N of them: asks the world, so with none it matches nobody), `lastFightAfter` / `lastFightBefore` (YYYY-MM-DD; a fighter who has not fought has no last fight and satisfies neither), and `maxKoRate` is now read ("ko rate under 30%"), needing at least one win (a rate has a record to rest on).
- **`lib/search-record.ts`:** "never been knocked out / stopped / KO'd", "stopped more than twice", "knocked out twice", "lost by knockout at least 3 times", "5 or more KO losses", "went the distance every time" (no stoppage either way); the active voice is a different fact ("stopped 10 opponents" is wins by knockout, as before); "with a draw", "no draws", "never fought a draw", "exactly 2 draws"; "on a winning / losing streak" (two or more), "of at least 5", "a 4 fight winning streak" (digits or words), "won their last 3", "lost their last two", "lost their last fight"; "unbeaten / undefeated in their last 5", "haven't lost in their last 4", "no losses in their last 5 fights"; "fought in the last 6 months / past year / 2 weeks", "this year", "last year", "has not fought in over a year" (before the day a year ago) / "at least 18 months" (on it), "inactive for 2 years", "last fought in 2024 / before 2023 / since 2024", "haven't fought since 2023". A span counts back from the league's own date (`heuristicParse` takes it; the search box and Ask pass `w.today`; without one a relative span is left in the sentence and no date is invented).
- **A superlative is not a filter:** "the longest winning streak" is still the streak list, "who has drawn the most" and "fighters with the most draws" have no list to answer them and get no answer (they had been "fighters with a draw" sorted by rating, which looks like an answer).
- **In Ask:** a record or form question is a fighter search (with "champions", "top 5", "how many" and the others it combines with); "who lost their last fight" no longer goes to the events list; a record list cannot be cut by it ("most wins among fighters who have never been knocked out" is a search sorted by wins); a question with an events word ("upcoming fights of fighters on a streak") has no tool with both and gets no answer; **how the last fight ended** ("lost their last fight by knockout", "against a champion") is not a cut either tool has, and gets no answer instead of everyone who lost. Round 33's battery had expected "never been knocked out" to be unanswerable; it is answerable now and the expectation is changed.
- **Measured on a second batch written after the first was fitted** (group G, 36 sentences: "KO'd", "exactly once", "at most 1 KO loss", "lost their last 3 fights", "a three fight losing streak", "riding a winning streak", "haven't lost in their last 4", "within the last 3 months", "haven't been in a fight for more than 2 years", "last fought before 2023", "women who have never been knocked out"): **29 of 36 (81%)** before anything was changed for it; the misses (the singular "KO loss", number words in "three fight", "haven't lost in", "unbeaten over", "been in a fight for", "last fought before") were fixed. All seven groups now 8/8, 44/44, 43/43, 38/38, 35/35, 38/38, 36/36.
- **Checked:** types, lint, 751 tests (750 pass, 1 skipped as before; 5 new in `tests/search-record.test.ts`: every reading pinned, the dates, the filters on the league's own fighters and on a fighter with no last fight or no wins, the planner's routes and refusals, the model's filters and the chips in both languages; one older expectation changed), 11 Arabic strings (chips only; the Arabic sentences for these cuts are not read), build, smoke 177/177. **Old-versus-new plans over 2,064 questions** (the Ask battery, all the sentences, ten subjects × twenty-five cuts × five openings): 1,050 changed, none that had an answer lost it; the changes are fighter searches that now carry the cut (including ones that had been the events, champions, rankings or fights-count answer with the cut left out). The differential ran before the "how the last fight ended" rule, which only moves "lost their last fight by …" questions from a search for everyone who lost to no answer. **44 deliberate mutations** (each phrase and its voice, the shift of each unit, strict and inclusive dates, the current run, the world and the unknown last fight, routes and refusals): 9 survived the first tests; one was code that could not matter (an alternative the one before already covers; removed), one was a mutation I wrote as no change, six got tests, and one cannot differ in a plan (the group keys for these facts are shadowed by the record-question rule; they still decide the refusal reason).
- **Not done / limits:** Arabic sentences for these facts are not read (an Arabic question with them finds no one, not the wrong people); no filter for the method of the last fight, a title fight, or "unbeaten in N fights" as a count of fights since the last loss; a streak of draws; "last fought" by month; a bare "recently" (no span) and "active" in the sense of fighting lately are not read (round 49's `status`, the flag the data holds, still decides "active").

## 94. The pipeline while the real league loads (round 46, 2026-10-04)
Found by opening the built site on the demo league in a browser at 1440 and 390 px and reading the pages as a fan would, not by reading code. The site already has the big pieces (fighter pages with record, ratings, earnings, weigh-ins and style twins; rankings; head to head and common opponents on Compare; previews; picks; Ask the data), so the next work is the facts a fan looks for first, and keeping every page right when the league is real and partly loaded.
1. **Done (this round):** a long name beside a match badge, and a long division label, wrap instead of being cut to "Lourdes ..."; the fight record keeps date and method on one line on a phone.
2. **Fighter page, the first-glance facts:** last five fights as a form strip, days since the last fight, the next fight with its date and opponent, and for each past fight the opponent's record and rating going in (from `rating_history`), so "beat an unbeaten fighter" is visible without opening the bout.
3. **The fighters list at 5,000 and more (done in §104):** today it filters by division and sex and takes a plain-language query. Add sort (rating, most recent fight, knockout rate), country, active or retired, stance, and keep the paging; every control a plain link, so it works without a script and each filter is a URL that can be shared.
4. **Country pages:** the best fighters from a country, its champions, its next fights, its venues. A page fans search for ("best Mexican boxers") that the data already answers.


5. **The first real look:** when the 5,000-fighter load lands, the same screenshot pass on the real league, ten fighters checked by hand, the rankings decision (how many real fighters have five fights) made on numbers.
6. **Speed at real size:** time a cold fighter page, the fighters list and a ranking on the 5,000- and then 35,000-fighter league, and fix what is slow before it is public.
Each item is its own pull request and is merged only when the owner says so.

## 112. Share cards for division rankings, belts and matchups, and the smoke run checks every card (round 52, 2026-10-04)
`app/[locale]/rankings/[division]/opengraph-image.tsx`, `app/[locale]/titles/[slug]/opengraph-image.tsx`, `app/api/og/compare/route.tsx`, `lib/seo.ts`, `lib/smoke.ts`
- Only fighters, events, bouts and previews had a share image; a link to a division's ranking, a belt's lineage or a matchup showed the plain site card. Each now has its own, in the site's look: the division's top-rated fighter with record and rating; the belt with its division, current champion (not when dormant), reigns and title defences; the matchup with both names, both records and the model's win probabilities.
- **A matchup is a query (`/compare?a=&b=`), and a card file cannot read a query**, so its card is an image route, `/api/og/compare?a=<slug>&b=<slug>&lang=`, and the matchup page's metadata points at it (`image` in `pageMetadata`) with the two names in the title. Only two different fighters that exist are drawn (slugs are checked against a pattern and the league); anything else is a plain 404, so the route cannot put chosen words on a Ringside card.
- The smoke run now requests one card of each kind (fighter, bout, event, division, belt, matchup) in English and Arabic and fails unless a real PNG comes back (`kind: "png"`); before this nothing checked that a card rendered at all, and the Arabic card loads its own font.
- **Not done:** a Share button, and country cards (the country page's metadata can take `image` the same way).

## 114. The picture a page waits on is fetched first (round 54, 2026-10-04)
`components/Portrait.tsx`, `components/Poster.tsx`, `app/[locale]/boxers/[slug]/page.tsx`, `app/[locale]/page.tsx`, `lib/smoke.ts`
- Every fighter portrait and poster was lazy-loaded, including the one the page is waiting to paint: a lazy hero image is fetched late, which is the slowest way to show the page and the thing Core Web Vitals measures (the largest picture). A `priority` option now fetches the fighter page's own portrait (the real photo or the generated art) and the home page's next-fight poster at once and first (`loading="eager"`, `fetchpriority="high"`); every other portrait and poster stays lazy, and real photos and posters now also decode off the main thread.
- The smoke run fails the fighter page unless its portrait carries `fetchpriority="high"`, so the fix cannot quietly undo itself.
- **What this does not do:** it was not measured with a stopwatch; the demo league has generated portraits only, so the real Commons photos (a thumbnail of one fixed width for every size) remain unmeasured. Measure largest-contentful-paint on the real league when it is loaded, and consider width variants (Commons serves any width) if the 64 px cards fetch more pixels than they show.

## 98. A fighter's record and form in an Arabic search, and the year they turned pro (round 54, 2026-10-04)
Round 53 gave the English search the facts a record holds besides its counts; the Arabic one still read none of them, and some of what it read it read as something else. On the parser as it was (groups H, 35 sentences, `tests/search-nl-battery.ts`, `npm run search:nl`, answers checked against the league's own fighters): **2 of 35 right (6%)**. "ملاكمون في سلسلة انتصارات من 5 نزالات" was **every boxer with 5 or more fights** (916), "فازوا في آخر 3 نزالات" the same for 3, "لم يخسروا في آخر 5 نزالات" was "undefeated, with 5 or more fights" (15 fighters, not the 78 who are unbeaten in their last five); the rest ("لم يتعرضوا للضربة القاضية أبدا", "لديهم تعادل", "خاضوا نزالا في آخر 6 أشهر", "احترفوا في 2015", "نسبة ضرباتهم القاضية أقل من 30%") fell to a name search and found no one.
- **`lib/search-record-ar.ts`** (on the folded question, before the quantity parser, which would otherwise take "لم يخسروا" for "undefeated"): never stopped ("لم يتعرضوا للضربة القاضية أبدا", "لم يخسروا بالضربة القاضية", "بدون هزائم بالضربة القاضية" is not "بدون هزائم"), how many times ("خسروا بالضربة القاضية 3 مرات على الأقل", "أكثر من مرتين", "5 هزائم بالضربة القاضية"); draws (بدون تعادلات, لم يتعادلوا, لديهم تعادل, "تعادل واحد" is exactly one, "أكثر من تعادلين"); streaks ("سلسلة انتصارات / هزائم" with or without "من 5 نزالات"; "فازوا / خسروا في آخر 3 نزالات", "آخر نزالين", "نزالهم / نزالهن الأخير"); "لم يخسروا في آخر 5 نزالات" and "غير مهزومين في آخر 3" as an unbeaten run; when they last fought (في آخر 6 أشهر / سنتين / ثلاثة أشهر, هذا العام, العام الماضي, "لم يخوضوا نزالا منذ أكثر من سنة / 18 شهرا على الأقل / 2023", "لا نشاط لهم منذ سنتين", "آخر نزال لهم في 2024 / قبل 2023 / منذ 2025"), counted back from the league's own date; the year they turned pro (احترفوا في / بعد / قبل / منذ 2015, "بدأوا مسيرتهم الاحترافية في 2018"; after and before are strict, since is not); a knockout rate ("نسبة ضرباتهم القاضية أقل من 30% / أكثر من 70%"). Number words and Arabic-Indic digits go through `westernize` as in round 52, which now also knows days, weeks and months.
- **Not guessed:** "أوقفوا" (and "لم يوقفوا"): without its vowel marks it is both "stopped (others)" and "was stopped", and a wrong guess is a wrong answer, so it is left as it is (no answer), while "تعرضوا للضربة القاضية" and "خسروا بالضربة القاضية" are clear. **How the last fight ended** ("خسروا نزالهم الأخير بالضربة القاضية", "… أمام بطل") is not a cut either tool has: no answer, as in English, instead of everyone who lost (the Arabic parser had read it as a plain loss on the last fight). "أطول سلسلة انتصارات" is still the streak list, not a filter. A question with an Arabic events word (القادمة, نتائج, فعاليات, بطاقة, جدول) and a cut gets no answer, as in English.
- **Measured on a second batch written after the first was fitted** (group I, 33 sentences: "لم يخسر أي منهم", "بدون هزائم بالضربة", "انتصروا في آخر 4", "في آخر ثلاثة نزالات", "خسرن نزالهن الأخير", "في آخر سنتين", "العام الماضي", "منذ 18 شهرا على الأقل", "بدأوا مسيرتهم الاحترافية", "نسبة الضربات القاضية لديهم على الأقل 60%", combinations): **30 of 33 (91%)** before anything was changed for it; the three misses ("لم يخسر أي منهم بالضربة القاضية" read as plain undefeated, "شهرا" with "على الأقل" after it, "لديهم" between the rate and its number) were fixed. All nine groups now 8/8, 44/44, 43/43, 38/38, 35/35, 38/38, 36/36, 35/35, 33/33.
- **Checked:** types, lint, 754 tests (753 pass, 1 skipped as before; 3 new in `tests/search-record-ar.test.ts`: every reading pinned, what is not guessed, the planner's routes; the battery's Arabic groups run in `tests/search-nl.test.ts`), no new strings (chips are round 53's), build, smoke 177/177. **Old-versus-new plans over 2,175 questions** (the Ask battery, all the sentences, ten Arabic subjects × twenty-six cuts × five openings): 1,093 changed, no English plan among them; the only questions that had an answer and lost it are the 46 "lost their last fight by knockout" ones, which had been "everyone who lost" and are now no answer; the rest are searches that now carry the cut (including champions and rankings answers that had left it out). **44 deliberate mutations** (each phrase and its voice, the dual, the unit shifts, strict and inclusive years, the not-a-filter guards, the routes): 11 survived the first tests (one a lookahead that no input reaches, removed); each of the others got a pin and all are killed.
- **Not done / limits:** the Arabic number words are still one to ten and the tens; "أوقفوا" stays unread (a vowel-marked "أُوقفوا" is folded too); no filter for how the last fight ended; the machine Arabic of the chips and of these sentences has had no native review.

## 115. A Share button, and a share card for every country (round 55, 2026-10-04)
`components/ShareButton.tsx`, `app/[locale]/countries/[slug]/opengraph-image.tsx`, `lib/smoke.ts`
- **Share** on a fighter's page (beside Watch), an event's page and a country's page: the phone's own share sheet where the browser has one, otherwise the address goes to the clipboard and the button says "Link copied" (or "Couldn't copy the link"); cancelling the sheet is not an error. It shares the address as it is on screen, so each page's own share card (§105, §112) is what the recipient sees. Read in a browser with the sheet absent: one button, the click copied the page's address, no page errors.
- A country now has a share card (name, best-rated fighter, fighters, active, champions), and the smoke run requests it in both languages with the other cards.
