## 116. The sanctioning bodies' official lists (round 56, 2026-10-04)
`lib/providers/boxing-data-api.ts` (`mapRanking`, `rankingBody`, `fetchOfficialRankings`), `lib/official.ts`, `components/OfficialList.tsx`, `app/[locale]/rankings/[division]/page.tsx`, `tests/official-rankings.test.ts`, `tests/official-index.test.ts`, `docs/boxing-data-api-rankings-enquiry.md`
- **Found by reading the supplier's docs** (https://boxing-data.com/docs): `GET /v2/rankings/` returns the IBF, WBA, WBC and WBO official standings, men's only and current only (no history), one page per division (17 pages, four bodies each), each with its champions (belt type full, regular or interim, or vacant) and ranked contenders, and the body's own `updated_at` ("sourced from BoxingScene"). The earlier research note that said "current and historical" is not supported by these docs.
- **Data:** the adapter fetches the 17 pages once per run (cached, so a load after a `--check` asks for nothing; `--update` refreshes), maps each page to a `ProviderOfficialRanking`, and never fails a run for it (a plan without the endpoint has none; the stored lists stay). `FeedData.officialRankings` is a whole snapshot; `ingest` replaces the `official_rankings` table with it (and leaves it alone when a provider has none). A fighter on a list is linked when we hold them, from this batch or an earlier load, and otherwise keeps the name given; a vacant belt or place has neither.
- **Pages:** a division's ranking page gets WBA / WBC / IBF / WBO tabs beside "Ringside rating" (men's, where the supplier has lists): the body's champions (belt type, vacant), its contenders (a linked fighter shows our record and rating, an unlinked one the name alone) and a line that says whose standings these are, as of when, and that they are not Ringside's ranking. A fighter's page gets a gold badge for each list they are on ("#3 WBC", "WBC champion", "WBC interim champion"). Arabic written (the four bodies' names follow the site's existing "المجلس العالمي للملاكمة (GBC)" style); not read by a native speaker.
- **Open question for the supplier, drafted for the owner to send:** may these lists be stored and shown, with what credit, and does BoxingScene's permission cover it (`docs/boxing-data-api-rankings-enquiry.md`, which also asks about `scores`, the fighter totals and a temporary rate-limit raise). **Until it is answered the page credits "Boxing Data API from BoxingScene" and the site is not public.**
- **Written from the docs, not from a response:** the first real run is the test of the mapping. Read `rankingsSkipped` and `rankingsUnavailable` in the run's approximated-or-skipped list, and the lists on a division page, against the bodies' own sites.
- Checked: mapping tests and eight mutation checks (the body test, the rank filter, the women's filter, duplicates, a refused plan, whole-snapshot replacement, no wipe on an empty snapshot, the belt type), each of which fails a test; ingest end to end; the file provider; request counts in the backfill CLI tests (the rankings are one more cached page); smoke on the demo (211/211) and partial (164/164) leagues, both languages; the list page and a fighter's badges read at 1440 px.

## 100. A record in an Arabic sentence is no longer shown backwards (round 55, 2026-10-04)
Found by opening the new search in the browser in Arabic, not by reading code. The chip for "last fought since" showed the date as it is stored, `2026-07-04`, which a right-to-left page draws as `04-07-2026`; looking for the same slip elsewhere turned up a worse one: **an Arabic Ask answer "الأول بحسب التصنيف هو ياروسلاف زينتشينكو (23-4-1)" showed the fighter's record as `1-4-23`**, a fighter with 23 wins shown with 1. The cause is how the browser orders text, not the data: after an Arabic letter the digits count as Arabic numbers, and the hyphens between them no longer hold them together, so the right-to-left order flips the record. A record on its own in a cell, or after only symbols, reads correctly (the browser was asked: the order of every digit was measured), which is why the rankings table and the fighter cards had always looked right.
- **Measured in the browser, on the pages as they were:** records shown backwards in the home page's underdog line, the fighter page's next-fight card and its scouting report, the division page's champion line (`🇺🇸 الولايات المتحدة · 20-4-1`), every row of the upset-watch page (46), the Ask answer, and every fighter line on an organisation page (`وزن فوق الديك · 16-5-0` drawn as `0-5-16`: ten to twenty rows a page, found only by the new page check below, because the digits were separate text nodes and the browser-side check had read them one at a time).
- **A value put into a translated sentence is isolated** (`isolateNumeric` in `lib/i18n/t.ts`, for `t`, `t.n` and `t.rich`, Arabic only): a variable that is all digits and separators with a hyphen, slash or colon between digits ("23-4-1", "2026-07-04") is wrapped in a left-to-right isolate, so it reads as written wherever the sentence puts it, in the page and in the meta tags. A name, a sentence, a count with a thousands separator, English: untouched. This fixes every sentence that carries a record through a variable (the underdog lines, the scouting reports, the Ask answers, the descriptions) at once.
- **Three places that build the line in markup** ("(record)" after an opponent, "country · record · KOs", "division · record" on the organisation page) now wrap the record in `<bdi dir="ltr">`.
- **The "last fought" chips** write the date in the visitor's language (`4 يوليو 2026`, `Jul 4, 2026`) instead of the stored form.
- **A check that keeps it fixed:** `flippedRecords` in `lib/smoke.ts` reads each Arabic page's HTML a block of text at a time (an element that starts a new line ends a block) and fails the smoke run when an Arabic letter, a space or a symbol and then a record come in one block with nothing isolating it (`<bdi>`, `dir="ltr"`, `ltr-fixed`, text marked English, or an isolate character). Two boxes of a flex row with nothing between them in the text are not one run and are not flagged; a range ("1–48") reads correctly in either order and is not a record. It is part of every smoke run on the Arabic pages (all five feeds and the 859-route crawl). On the pages as they were it flagged the places the browser had shown (the home page, a fighter page, a division page, the upset-watch page, the Ask answer) and, on the build with the sentence fix only, the organisation pages the browser-side check had missed; on the fixed build it flags none.
- **Checked:** types, lint, 758 tests (757 pass, 1 skipped as before; 4 new in `tests/flipped-records.test.ts`: the sentence isolation in all three forms, what is and is not isolated, the page check on each shape; one chip test extended to Arabic), no new strings, build, smoke 177/177 on all five feeds and the crawl 859/859, and the browser again on the rebuilt pages (the answer, the organisation page and the chips read in the right order). **18 deliberate mutations** of the isolation and the page check: 7 survived the first tests; five got tests, and two cannot differ (a second language that does not exist; a space or nothing where an isolate was removed).
- **Not done / limits:** the check finds records (two hyphens between digits); a score with one hyphen ("5-3") or a time is not looked for, and the dates elsewhere on the site were already written out in words; it reads the HTML, so a record drawn inside an SVG or an image is not covered (the poster draws its record through the same isolating sentence); what the browser does to digits next to Arabic letters in a place nobody has opened is found by the check only if it is one of these shapes.

## 99. The first-glance facts on a fighter's page (round 47, 2026-10-04)
`lib/glance.ts`, `app/[locale]/boxers/[slug]/page.tsx`, `components/ui.tsx` (`BoutLine`), `tests/glance.test.ts`
- **Form and recency.** Under the four numbers: the last five results as the same W / L / D pills the fight record uses, newest first, and "Last fought 3 years ago" (days under two months, months under two years, years after, in whole calendar months; nothing for a date in the future). A no-contest, a cancelled and an upcoming fight are not in the strip, as they are not in the record.
- **The opponent going in.** Each past fight in the record now has a second line under the opponent: "then 14-0-0, rated 1650", their Elo-style rating before the fight (the pre-fight rating the world already replays) and their record before it. **The record is stated only when the opponent's whole career is held** (`careerView(...).source === "loaded"`): for a fighter whose early fights are not in the data the count would be short and wrong, so the line shows the rating alone; and a partial career is never called a debut ("then on debut, rated 1500" only when the whole career is held and there is no earlier fight).
- **A bug the Arabic page showed.** An Arabic sentence put a record beside Arabic words, and the browser's bidirectional rules displayed "22-7-0" as "0-7-22" (a number that follows an Arabic letter takes the right-to-left direction, and a hyphen does not join it). The record is now wrapped in `<bdi dir="ltr">` inside the sentence (`t.rich`, `<r>` tag); read on the Arabic page at 390 px. Any other sentence that sets a record or a score next to Arabic words needs the same.
- Arabic written for the new sentences, with the plural forms Arabic needs (zero, one, two, few, many, other); not yet read by a native speaker (see docs/arabic-review.md). Every sentence is one key, not pieces ("Last fought {n} months ago", not "Last fought" + a duration).
- Checked: the unit tests (the record gate, a debut, a cancelled and a no-contest fight, the unit of "ago" at its boundaries, a future date), `npm run smoke` on the demo league (177/177) and on a partial real-shaped league (130/130), and the page read in English at 1440 px and in Arabic at 390 px.

## 117. The judges' scores and the supplier's career knockouts (round 57, 2026-10-04)
`lib/providers/boxing-data-api.ts` (`cleanScores`, `careerTotals`), `lib/career.ts` (`koView`), `components/ui.tsx`, `app/[locale]/boxers/[slug]/page.tsx`, `app/[locale]/bouts/[id]/page.tsx`, `tests/scores-and-totals.test.ts`
- **Two fields the supplier gives that the load ignored** (found reading its docs): a decision's `scores` (`["116-109","117-108","116-109"]`) and a fighter's `stats.ko_wins` / `stopped`.
- **Scores:** kept only for a fight that went to the cards (a decision or a draw: a stoppage with scores attached is a feed slip), as well-formed "116-109" strings, three at most, a dash read as a hyphen. Stored on the bout (`vendor_scores`) and shown on the bout page as "Judges' scores" **in the supplier's order and never assigned to a fighter or a judge**: the docs name neither, and the convention (which number is whose) is not stated, so the page says it does not know. Where real judge cards exist (a richer source) they show instead.
- **Career knockouts:** `ko_wins` and `stopped` are kept only if each is a whole number no larger than the wins or losses it is part of. For a career held in part (the record shown is the supplier's total) the fighter page and card now show the **supplier's knockouts** (and the knockout share of the supplier's wins) beside the supplier's record, instead of a count from the few fights held ("19-0-1 · 1 KO"), and the note under the record says the knockouts are the supplier's too. For a complete career nothing changes. "Times stopped" is a new line in the profile, shown for a complete career (counted) or when the supplier states it, never from a part of a career.
- Checked: unit tests and **six mutation checks** (the knockouts-never-exceed-wins rule, the score format, cards-only, supplier-only-when-partial, the rate over the supplier's wins, an unknown stopped count not shown as zero), each of which fails a test; ingest and the world end to end on a partial load; smoke on the demo (211/211) and partial (162/162) leagues in both languages, including a bout with scores and no judges; the fighter page and the bout section read at 1440 px. Arabic written, not read by a native speaker.
- **Written from the docs, not from a response:** read a few real fighters' `ko_wins` and `stopped` against their records, and a real decision's `scores` against the result, after the first real run. If the supplier's order turns out to be winner first, the page could say so; that is for the supplier to confirm (`docs/boxing-data-api-rankings-enquiry.md` asks).

## 101. Arabic limits are written in words, not with ≥ and ≤ (round 56, 2026-10-04)
Found in the browser at 390 px on the Arabic search: the chip "سلسلة انتصارات ≥ 2" is drawn with the sign mirrored, because in a right-to-left line `≥` and `≤` are mirrored characters, so it shows the arrow of "at most" beside the number and reads either way to someone reading right to left. Twenty-one Arabic chip strings (age, reach, height, fights, draws, losses, wins, knockouts, KO rate, stopped, streaks, debut) said their limit that way. They now say it in words ("العمر: 36 فأكثر", "امتداد الذراعين: 191 سم فأكثر", "الانتصارات: 5 فأقل", "الظهور الاحترافي الأول: 2015 فما بعد"); the English chips keep the signs. Only `i18n/ar.json` changed; a test (`tests/ar-no-inequality-signs.test.ts`) fails if any Arabic string contains `≥` or `≤` again (it fails on the file as it was). Checked in the browser on the rebuilt page, types, lint, 763 tests (762 pass, 1 skipped as before), build, smoke 177/177. The round-55 checks at 390 px found no overflow and records and dates in the right order. Not done: the Arabic wording has had no native review; "حتى {year}" for the upper debut limit was already in words.

## 104. The fighters list can be sorted and filtered (round 48, 2026-10-04)
`lib/fighter-list.ts`, `app/[locale]/boxers/page.tsx`, `tests/fighter-list.test.ts`
- **Four controls under the division chips:** country, stance, active or retired, and the order (rating, latest fight, most wins, knockout rate). It is a plain GET form (works without a script, `Apply` and `Clear filters`), so every view is a link that can be shared, and it carries the search, division and sex already chosen. The country and stance lists offer only values that exist in the league.
- **A value the page does not know is ignored**, not turned into an empty page (`?sort=nonsense`); a country nobody is from is an honest empty list.
- **Honest on a partial league.** "Most wins" uses the career the page shows (the supplier's total for a career held in part), so a fighter with 30 wins on the card is not ranked as the 2 fights we hold. "Knockout rate" (knockouts as a share of wins) ranks only fighters with at least 5 wins **and** a whole career held; the rest come last, and a line under the controls says so. The first version asked for 5 fights, and the screenshot showed 4 knockouts in 4 wins above 15 in 17; the guard is on wins because the rate is over wins.
- A search keeps its own relevance order unless an order is asked for. Ties fall to the higher rating, then the id, so a page never reshuffles on reload.
- Checked: unit tests with five mutations (drop the win minimum, the whole-career test, the supplier's wins, the never-fought-last rule, the unknown-sort fallback), each of which fails a test; four new smoke routes in both languages (demo league 185/185, partial league 138/138); the form submitted in a browser with scripts off; the page read in English at 1440 px and Arabic at 390 px.
- Not done: the browser's empty `country=&stance=` pairs stay in the URL after Apply (harmless; the page treats an empty value as none).

## 102. "The longest winning streak among welterweights who have never been stopped" (round 57, 2026-10-04)
Found by asking the live Ask page, in English, a question that mixes a list and a cut from round 53. It answered "10 fighters match. The first, by rating, is Bohdan Kovalenko (11-0-0)": a search sorted by rating, with "the longest winning streak" dropped, and a fighter with a 0-2 record in the table. Two rules sent it there: the record-question rule (round 53) answered any question that carried a stopped / draws / streak / last-fight cut before the record lists had a look, and the older rule that reads "fighters who …" with "unbeaten" as a search took "longest unbeaten run among fighters who …" for a search of the unbeaten. Now a question that names a record list (longest streak, most knockouts, …) goes to the list code: a list the fighter search can sort by (wins, knockouts, KO rate) becomes that search with the cut, and any other ("the longest winning streak among …") is no answer, as for every other cut a list does not have. "Who is on the longest winning streak" is still the streak list. Two assertions added to `tests/search-record.test.ts` (both fail without their rule: two deliberate mutations, both killed); the Ask battery stays 100%. Types, lint, 767 tests (1 skipped as before), build, smoke. Not done: a streak list that could be cut (a longest streak among welterweights) would need the list tool to take a division or a group; today a division works only where the list already takes one.

## 103. Who beat whom, and who trained the most champions (round 58, 2026-10-04)
Found by asking the live Ask page twenty ordinary questions and reading the answers. Three were wrong in a way that looks right: "who has beaten Marcus Larkin" (and "who beat …", "who did … lose to", "who knocked out …") was **Marcus Larkin's profile**; "who trained the most champions" was the **list of live champions** (the refusal for "which gym, trainer, manager or promoter has the most champions" knew "trains" but not "trained"); and "who has fought the most times" had no answer although the fighter search sorts by fights. Now: a question about who beat, defeated, stopped or knocked out someone, or whom someone lost to, is no answer (no tool lists a fighter's opponents by result), except where a record list is about it ("who has beaten the highest rated fighters"); the refusal for the people behind the most champions knows trained, coached, managed and promoted; "fought the most" sorts the fighters by fights. 3 tests in `tests/ask-opponents.test.ts` (one of my own first expectations was wrong: "who has beaten the most champions" had been the champions list, which is not an answer either, and is now none). Five deliberate mutations, all killed; the Ask battery stays 100%. Types, lint, 771 tests (770 pass, 1 skipped as before), build, smoke 185/185. Of the other seventeen questions, those that were right stay right (rankings, the most knockouts in a division, counts, youngest undefeated, draws on record). Not done: "who fights on October 12", "which gym has the most fighters", "who won the last main event" and "the average age of champions" still have no answer (no tool for them); a question about a named fighter's opponents (who they beat) could use `bouts` filtered to wins, which that tool cannot yet do.


## 106. A page for every country (round 49, 2026-10-04)
`lib/countries.ts`, `app/[locale]/countries/page.tsx`, `app/[locale]/countries/[slug]/page.tsx`, `tests/countries.test.ts`
- **`/countries`** lists every country with a fighter who has fought, the one with the most fighters first; **`/countries/mexico`** shows its fighters, active and retired, its champions, its coming fights and the events held there. The fighter's page links to its country, the page links to the fighters list filtered to the country (§104), and every country is in the sitemap and the navigation (Discover, "Countries").
- **Champions** are the live belts (`belts(w)`, current and not dormant) whose holder is from the country, so the section agrees with the Titles page. **Coming fights** are fights not yet fought, not cancelled, with a fighter from the country on either side, soonest first. **Events held here** are held (not upcoming, not cancelled) cards whose country is the country.
- **A country is the name the feed gives.** Two spellings that make the same address ("Côte d'Ivoire", "Cote d'Ivoire") are one country under the commonest spelling; nothing else is merged. A feed that spells one place two ways ("USA", "United States") will show it twice until that is fixed at the source: **check the real feed's country values when the 5,000 load lands.**
- A fighter with no fights, or no country, is not counted. An unknown country is a real 404 (`/countries/atlantis`), not an empty page. The per-fighter address is worked out once per world, since a page scans every fight.
- Checked: unit tests on a hand-made world with six mutations (active-first order, held-not-upcoming events, cancelled fights, cancelled events, the no-fights rule, most-fighters-first), each of which fails a test; `npm run smoke` 193/193 on the demo league and 146/146 on a partial league, both languages; the page read in English at 1440 px and Arabic at 390 px. Arabic written, not yet read by a native speaker.

## 107. Arabic questions read as their English twins: nationalities, youngest, who beat whom (round 59, 2026-10-04)
Found the way round 58 found its bugs, in Arabic: twenty ordinary questions put to the live Ask page and the answers read. Wrong in a way that looks right: "أفضل 5 ملاكمين سعوديين" (the best five Saudi boxers) was **the best five of all 966** (the nationality was not a country, so it was dropped; the same for every "مكسيكيون", "يابانيات", "ألمان"); "ملاكمون يمينيون" lost "right-handed" (only the singular was known); "من هو أصغر ملاكم لم يهزم" (the youngest unbeaten) was the **best-rated** of the unbeaten, with "youngest" dropped; "من هزم ماركوس لاركين" (who beat him) was **his profile**; "من يدرب أكثر الأبطال" (who trains the most champions) was the **list of champions**; "متى نزال ماركوس لاركين القادم" (when is his next fight) and "متى قاتل … آخر مرة" (when did he last fight) were his profile, and the second also set "knockout artist" because "قاتل" ("fought") is also "killer"; "من فاز بآخر نزال رئيسي" (who won the last main event) was **"fighters on a winning streak of at least one"** (429), from round 54's pattern for the plural.
- A country is also found by its nationality adjective in Arabic (stem and ي / يه / يين / يون / يات: سعودي, سعوديين, يابانيات; plus the irregular plurals ألمان, أتراك, أمريكان), for the nine countries of the league and twenty others. "يمينيون" is orthodox. "أصغر ملاكم", "الأصغر سنا", "أكبر ملاكم سنا", "الأكبر سنا" are the order to sort in (youngest, oldest), while "أكبر من 35 سنة" is still a limit. "قاتل" is a knockout artist only as "ضربة / لكمة قاتلة".
- "Who beat, stopped or knocked out someone" (من هزم, اوقف, اسقط, تغلب على, خسر أمام) is no answer, as in English; "who trains, runs or promotes the most champions" is no answer; "when is his next fight" and "when did he last fight" are those facts; round 54's "فاز في آخر 3 نزالات" is read only as a plural statement about fighters, not after "من" (who).
- Checked: types, lint, 775 tests (774 pass, 1 skipped as before; 3 new in `tests/ask-arabic-questions.test.ts`, using a hand-written Arabic name for one fighter, since the planner needs the names table to claim a name), the Ask battery 100%, build, smoke, and the same questions put to the rebuilt server (all answered or refused as listed above). 11 deliberate mutations, all killed. Not done: the other countries' nationalities (a country outside the list is still dropped with the sentence otherwise understood; the list is easy to extend); "أكبر ملاكم" without "سنا" is not read as the oldest; names in Arabic still need the names table.

## 108. Groupings no tool makes, and "trained by" (round 60, 2026-10-04)
Twenty more ordinary English questions put to the live Ask page. Wrong in a way that looks right: "which venue hosted the most fights" was **the fighters sorted by number of fights**; "which country has the most champions" was the **list of champions**; "how many fights ended in the first round" was **all 7,496 fights**; "how many title fights were scheduled for 12 rounds" was **all title fights** (the rounds dropped, though the tool takes a minimum); "which trainer has the best win rate" looked for **a trainer called "has the best win rate"**; and "how many fighters trained by Saud Al-Dosari" was **Saud Al-Dosari's profile** (he is a fighter and a trainer in the data), not the fighters he trains.
- No answer for a grouping no tool makes: by venue or city (unless it is about gates, purses or earnings, which the money tool answers), by country ("which country has the most …"), by the round a fight ended in. Scheduled rounds are the tool's minimum: "12 rounds" and "at least 10 rounds" are answered (`minRounds`), any other count ("exactly 8") is no answer. "Trained, coached, managed, promoted by X" and "out of the X gym" are the team filter even when X is also a fighter, and are read before the fighter's profile. A sentence is not a trainer's name ("has the best win rate", "who gets the best out of…"): the trainers list, as before.
- Checked: types, lint, 784 tests (783 pass, 1 skipped as before; 3 new in `tests/ask-groupings.test.ts`, using a trainer, a manager and a promoter taken from the league), the Ask battery 100%, build, smoke. A first version also refused every question about "the best trainers", which the battery caught (they are the trainers list) and which was removed. 10 deliberate mutations, all killed (three needed a better test first). Of the other fourteen questions, those that were right stay right; "who holds the middleweight title", "who is ranked second at heavyweight", "list all champions from Japan", "the fastest knockout", "who fought on the last card" and the rest are unchanged. Not done: "who will win Morishita vs Hartmann" (surnames only) and "how long has X been champion" (answered as "holds no current belt") still have no better answer.

## 109. Six more facts about one fighter: pro debut, nickname, style, promoter, streak, decisions (round 61, 2026-10-04)
Twenty questions about one named fighter put to the live Ask page. Six kinds were answered with the whole profile (so the fact was not given) or, worse, with a list that dropped the fighter: "what is X's longest win streak" was **the longest win streak of everybody** ("Ramil Abad leads with 30"); "how many decisions has X won" was **all 4,195 fights that went to the scorecards**; "when did X turn pro", "what is X's nickname", "what is X's style" and "who is X's promoter" were the profile. The `fighter` tool now has six more facts (24 in all): `debut` ("X turned pro in 2015", or that the data does not give it), `nickname` (or that there is none on record, never made up), `style` (the archetype), `promoter` (the current promoter stint), `streak` (the current winning or losing run, or none after a draw, and the longest winning run, worked out from his own fights) and `decisions` (wins and losses by decision, a draw not counted as either). "Longest" no longer sends a question about a named fighter's streak to the list; with no name it is still the list. The stance sentence said "fights from a Orthodox stance"; it is now "X's stance is Orthodox" (and the Arabic with it). 12 Arabic sentences. Checked: types, lint, 788 tests (787 pass, 1 skipped as before; 5 new in `tests/ask-facts-more.test.ts`, each against the league's own fights, and two older expectations changed: the count of facts and the stance wording), the Ask battery 100%, build, smoke; 13 deliberate mutations, all killed (four needed another test: the other ways of asking, and a draw). Not done: "how many rounds has X fought" and "how much has X earned" are still the profile (no round count or per-fighter earnings in the fact set); a decision draw is not counted anywhere.

## 119. Speed at 19,000 fighters: the organisation cards, and a start-up that reaches the pages (round 59, 2026-10-04)
Measured with `npm run coldcheck` on a 19,360-fighter, 159,296-bout league (bench scale 20), each page in a fresh production server. Two pages were slow: `/orgs` on every visit (448 ms) and the home page on its first (738 ms, 693 ms over its usual).
- `/orgs`: each card asked for the organisation's full stable (rating change of every tenure, title wins, best fighter) to print three numbers. A promoter has about 2,500 tenures at this size, so 60 ms a card and 0.7 s for a page of twelve. `orgCard` (`lib/team.ts`) works out only fighters, current fighters and the combined record, once per organisation. Now 22 ms; `tests/orgs-card.test.ts` checks it equals `orgStable`'s figures for every organisation, and two mutations (counting everyone as current; ignoring the tenure dates) fail it.
- Home first visit: the live answer under the question box ("Who has the most knockouts among women?") was worked out by the first visitor (0.4 s). A new warm-up step, `home answer`, runs the rule-based plan in both languages at start-up (no model call).
- The start-up work had been missing the pages for a second reason: the fighter-name indexes for search and Ask were kept in module-level `WeakMap`s, and Next bundles the start-up hook and the pages separately, so the pages built them again (the same trap §31 fixed for `memo`). They now live on `globalThis` (`sharedWeakMap` in `lib/memo.ts`), and so does the empty name table they are keyed by. `tests/shared-index.test.ts` checks the registry (a second module copy cannot be made faithfully under tsx).
- Result: home first visit 738 → 273 ms, no page flagged by coldcheck; `/orgs` 448 → 22 ms. Start-up is about 0.6 s longer (the cost moved there).
- Checked: types, 818 tests (the memo-cache test fails in this sandbox only, as before), smoke.

## 118. One country, however the feed spells it (round 58, 2026-10-04)
Country pages were built from the feed's country text as written, so a real feed that says "USA" for some fighters and "United States" for others would have shown two United States pages (the risk noted in §106).
- `canonicalCountry` (`lib/format.ts`) gives the one English name for any spelling the platform can identify ("USA", "U.S.", "United States of America", "Great Britain", "Côte d'Ivoire" without accents), and `countrySlug` is the address of that name, so every spelling is one page and a fighter's link always lands on it.
- Nothing is guessed: England, Scotland, Wales and unknown names are kept as given, and Northern Ireland stays its own page rather than being folded into the UK.
- The page name is now the proper English name, not the commonest spelling in the feed.
- Checked: types, 815 tests pass (the memo-cache test fails in this sandbox only, as before; 2 changed/new in `tests/countries.test.ts`), smoke 211/211. Mutations: no folding fails 2 tests, folding Northern Ireland fails 1.

## 120. A Share button on the matchup page (round 60, 2026-10-04)
The matchup lab already had its own share card (§112) and per-matchup page metadata, but no way to send the matchup from the page itself. The result card now has the same Share button as fighters, events and countries (`ShareButton`: the phone's share sheet, else the link to the clipboard), titled "{a} vs {b}" in the reader's language. It shares the address as it is, so the two fighters travel with the link.
- No new strings (the title and button reuse existing keys); `i18n:check` has 0 missing.
- Checked: types, smoke (the matchup and crawled head-to-head pages render with the button).

## 121. Typing a misspelt name: 230 ms to 50 ms at 19,000 fighters (round 61, 2026-10-04)
Measured on the 19,360-fighter league: a search that finds nothing exactly looks for near spellings, and each new misspelling cost 200 to 260 ms (the same word typed again is kept, so only the first try paid). 96 of every 108 ms of that scan was `prefixDistance`, which runs once per word of the vocabulary (37,000) and built three edit-distance tables on three sliced copies of the word for each.
- `prefixDistance` (`lib/fuzzy.ts`) now fills one table against the first n+1 letters of the word and reads the distance to each prefix length from its last row: no slicing, no allocation (three reused buffers). A near spelling now takes 35 to 70 ms.
- `tests/fuzzy-prefix.test.ts` keeps the original implementation as the reference and checks the new one gives the same answer on 60,000 random pairs (small alphabet, so slips, swaps and shared starts are common, 2,000+ of them within the allowance). Mutations: no swap rule, and a prefix range one letter short, each fail it.
- The first search typed after a start-up built the index (every name, nickname and alias normalised: 138 ms). `lib/warm.ts` now builds it in both languages at start-up (`fighter search` step), as the other pages' aggregates are.
- Checked: types, search, warm-up and fuzzy tests, full suite and smoke (see the PR).

## 122. Common opponents on the matchup page (round 62, 2026-10-04)
"Both fought X" is how a fan compares two fighters who have never met, and it was only on the matchmaking page, where it kept the last fight against each opponent only.
- `commonOpponents` (`lib/common-opponents.ts`): the opponents both fought, best-rated first, with every fight each had against them in date order (a rematch is both fights). A no-contest, a cancelled fight, a fight not yet fought and a fight between the two are not results. The list is cut at eight and says how many more there are. When either career is held in part (§43), it says so: an opponent missing from the list may be a fight Ringside does not hold.
- The matchup page (`/compare`) shows it under the head to head, as stacked rows (opponent, then each fighter's results) so it needs no sideways scrolling at 390 px; checked in English and Arabic on screen.
- Two new strings with Arabic (the partial-career note and the "{n} more opponents in common" plural); the rest reuse existing ones.
- Tests: `tests/common-opponents.test.ts` (rematches, exclusions, the cut, the partial flag) with three mutations (a no-contest counted, only the last fight kept, the partial flag off) each failing it.
- Checked: types, i18n (0 missing), the a11y and Arabic tests, smoke, full suite (see the PR).

## 123. "By the numbers" on a fighter's page (round 63, 2026-10-04)
The counting facts a fan quotes about a fighter were not on the page: how many rounds he has boxed, how often he goes the distance, how quickly his wins come, where he has fought.
- `numbersOf` (`lib/by-the-numbers.ts`), a pure function of the fight list: rounds boxed (a stoppage counts the round it ended in, a decision or draw its scheduled rounds), fights that went the distance (a decision or draw; a technical decision ended early, so it does not count), wins by stoppage inside three rounds, countries fought in, the most-fought venue, the busiest year and the longest layoff. A no-contest, a cancelled and an upcoming fight are not counted.
- Nothing is estimated: a figure that needs a fact the data does not give (the round a fight ended in, the scheduled rounds of a fight that went the distance) is `null` and the tile is left out. A venue, a year or a layoff is shown only when it says something (twice or more; half a year or more).
- The panel shows only for a career held whole (`careerView` source "loaded") with at least five fights: counted from part of a career these would be wrong in a way that reads as right (§43).
- Eleven new strings with Arabic (worded gender-neutrally, the way the rest of the page is); the layoff reuses the titles page's duration words. For a native speaker to review.
- Checked on screen at 390 and 1440 px in English and Arabic.
- Tests: `tests/by-the-numbers.test.ts` (the counts; what is not counted; unknown is null not a guess; the thresholds; an unknown event), four mutations (a technical decision as the distance, a loss as a quick win, a missing round guessed as 0, no layoff floor) each failing it.

## 127. Division names a real feed writes, and the fights they used to drop (round 64, 2026-10-04)
Two ways a real feed could lose real fights, found by reading how division names are matched.
- A fight whose division the feed names as something Ringside has no division for ("Catchweight", "Open weight") was rejected by the validator, so both fighters lost a real result from their record, form and rating. `placeBouts` (`lib/providers/boxing-data-api.ts`) now gives such a fight the division of its fighters (the heavier, when the two differ) and counts it (`boutDivisionFromFighters`, shown by the backfill with the other notes). Only a fight where neither fighter has a division stays unplaced.
- `normalizeDivision` (`lib/divisions.ts`) read "Over 200 lbs" as cruiserweight (it saw 200); that is the heavyweight limit and now reads as heavyweight (a plain "200 lbs" is still cruiserweight). A "Women's" or "Female" prefix and a limit in brackets ("Super Light (140)", "Heavyweight (200+)") are ignored, where before each made the division unrecognised and dropped the fighter.
- Bridgerweight and Super Heavyweight are Heavyweight for a fighter or a fight (`divisionOf`, in the adapter only): "over 200 lb", and not a division the site lists. `normalizeDivision` itself still leaves Bridgerweight unknown, because the champions importer must keep its title lineage apart from Heavyweight's.
- Tests: `tests/division-aliases.test.ts`, four mutations (the over-200 rule, the heavier division, the women's prefix, the Bridgerweight mapping) each failing it. Runbook table in `docs/real-data-readiness.md`.

## 128. A real feed's odd outcomes no longer drop real fights (round 65, 2026-10-04)
The validator rejects a fight with a winner and no method, and one whose method says there was no winner; both fighters then lose a real result from their record, form and rating. Reading `mapFight`, three shapes of real feed would have done that silently.
- An outcome word the feed's own list does not show but a real feed has ("DQ", "RTD", "Corner Retirement", "Technical Decision", "No Contest", "Draw") left the fight with a winner and no method. `mapFight` now reads such a word through the same spelling table the importers use (`normalizeMethod`) and counts it (`outcomeMapped`). The words it always read (UD, MD, SD, KO, TKO, PTS) are read exactly as before.
- A winner with an outcome nobody can read (an unknown word, none, or one that says "draw" or "no contest"), is not dropped and not guessed: it stays in both fighters' history as "no result yet", no winner named, counted as `outcomeUnreadable`. A result that needs a winner and has none (not only a knockout now: a disqualification, a retirement) is the same, counted as `stoppageWithoutWinner`.
- Both fighters marked as the winner is a feed slip: neither is picked (that would invent a result), and it is not read as a draw (`bothMarkedWinner`). A result round of 0 or below is read as unknown, not as a fight the validator rejects (`roundUnreadable`).
- Not changed, on purpose: a fight scheduled for more than 15 rounds (a historical fight) is still rejected by the validator and shows as a reported error, because other pages assume at most 15; that needs a decision, not a quiet change.
- Tests: `tests/outcome-mapping.test.ts` (eight tests; the last runs every awkward fight through the real validator and expects no error), five mutations (word mapping, winner demotion, both-marked, round floor, winner-needed demotion) each failing it. Runbook table in `docs/real-data-readiness.md`.

## 129. A hostile league: nothing throws, nothing is lost uncounted, and two real gaps it found (round 66, 2026-10-04)
A league of awkward values (`lib/hostile-feed.ts`: markup and SQL in names, a 300-letter name, absurd heights, contradictory results, impossible dates, a fighter with no name, a repeated fight id) is read through the real adapter, validator, ingest and world, and rendered page by page.
- `tests/hostile-feed.test.ts` (five tests): nothing throws; every fight fed in is a repeat, skipped, dropped for a missing fighter or division, or a bout of the feed (each counted by the adapter's notes), and every bout of the feed is kept by the validator or dropped with an error that names it; the world has no NaN, markup and SQL are stored as text, slugs are unique and plain; search, every ranking, countries, careers and a fighter's counting facts do not throw on it.
- `npm run smoke -- --feed hostile --crawl 60`: 326 pages in both languages pass, and the run fails if a name's markup is on any page unescaped (it is on the fighter page, as `&lt;script&gt;`). Not added to CI (the unit tests are); `docs/deploy.md` says when to run it.
- Gap 1, found by it: an event exists in the adapter only because a fight said so, so a fight dropped for a fighter outside the selection (that is, most small cards in a partial first load) or an unplaceable division left an event with no fights, whose page is a 404 yet which the sitemap, the search and the country and organisation pages would list. The adapter now leaves out the events of the fights it drops and counts them (`eventsWithoutFights`); the validator does the same, with an `event_emptied` warning, for a card whose every fight it rejected (a card the feed announced with no fights, and one with money records, are left alone).
- Gap 2: a 300-letter name made a 300-character address. `slugify` now caps at 100 characters, cut at a word where one is near the end; no real name is near it, so no existing address changes (`tests/slug.test.ts`). A name with no Latin letters still makes the slug `x` (then `x-2`...): unique and working, but not readable; transliteration needs a library and real names are Latin, so it is left.
- Not changed: fights scheduled for more than 15 rounds are still rejected (see §128).
- Mutations: each of the new rules removed in turn fails a test (the slug cap, the uncounted skip, the event-emptied rule and its two exemptions, the adapter's event filter).

## 131. This machine's names, written the odd ways (round 76, 2026-10-04)

- Found in a read of the day's diffs: the first-filter `privateHost` in `lib/research/fetcher.ts` missed `localhost.` (trailing dot) and anything under `.localhost`. The visitor-facing path was never exposed (its DNS `hostCheck` refuses private addresses); the CLI research tool had no second check.
- Fix: strip trailing dots and lower-case before matching; refuse `.localhost`, `.local`, `.internal` and the private IPv4/IPv6 forms.
- Tests: two in `tests/fetcher-hardening.test.ts` (refused names; a public name with a trailing dot, or one merely containing "localhost", still allowed). Mutation: reverting the fix fails the first.

## 132. The first-fetch checklist (round 77, 2026-10-04)

- `docs/real-data-runbook.md`: what to paste back from the first real fetch, and a table of every note count (meaning, when it matters). Docs only.

## 135. The Arabic review queue (round 67, 2026-10-04)
The state of the Arabic, read with the repo's own `i18n:review status` and `qa`: 2,235 strings and 1,848 names, none reviewed by a person; the mechanical checks list only notes (gendered English to confirm, glossary terms, a few numbers and Latin words), no failures. `docs/arabic-review-queue.md` lists the 239 strings written on 2026-10-04 (Ask the data answers, Boxing explained, the fighter and country pages, the official lists, the matchup page, the share button) grouped by page with the English beside the Arabic, so the reviewer starts with what a reader meets first; `docs/arabic-review.md` points to it. The complete offline sheet is `npm run i18n:review -- export` (`review/`, deliberately not committed). Docs only; no behaviour changes.

## 134. The hostile smoke runs in CI (round 78, 2026-10-05)

- `.github/workflows/ci.yml`: `npm run smoke -- --feed hostile --crawl 60` joins the sparse, empty, unknown-facts and partial runs (about a minute more). It renders 354 pages in both languages and fails if the markup from a name is ever on a page unescaped.
- Found on the way: once the outcome mapping (§128) changed the hostile league's ratings, the 300-letter name became the top-rated fighter, and the "mistyped link offers the fighter they meant" check could not match it from a 99-letter address. The check now expects a name only for names of 60 letters or fewer (nobody mistypes a 300-letter name; the route is still checked to be a real 404).
- `docs/deploy.md` says CI runs it. PLAN had two sections numbered 133 (another session's "Rehearsing the load-day paths" and the Arabic queue's): the Arabic queue is now 135.

## 136. Fights scheduled for more than 15 rounds are kept (round 79, 2026-10-05)

- Decided by the owner (the open question in §128 and §129). `lib/validate.ts`: a bout may have 1 to `MAX_SCHEDULED_ROUNDS` (50) scheduled rounds, so a historical 20-, 25- or 42-round fight stays in both fighters' records instead of being dropped. A hundred rounds is still a data error: dropped and named (`bad_round`). Ending after the scheduled distance is still rejected.
- The one place that assumed the old limit: the knockout-by-round histogram and heatmap on the analytics page used twelve fixed cells and indexed them by the round of the stoppage, so a stoppage in round 13 or later (even in a 15-round fight, already allowed) wrote past the end and put NaN in the chart. Rounds after 12 now fold into the last column, labelled "12+" (`FINISH_ROUNDS`, `finishBin` in `lib/analytics.ts`), so nothing is dropped and no cell is NaN. No other page indexes by round.
- Tests: `tests/long-fights.test.ts` (a 20-round fight kept, the ceiling exact, 51 and 100 dropped, the fold with real fights ended in rounds 12, 14, 18 and 3); three mutations (no fold, ceiling back to 15, no ceiling) each fail it.

## 152. The vendor email, drafted in one piece (round 80, 2026-10-05)

- `docs/boxing-data-api-vendor-email.md`: one email for the owner to send (rankings licence and BoxingScene credit, the value lists for `outcome`, `weight_class` and `scheduled_rounds`, `scores` order, fighter totals, and the hourly limit), replacing the two earlier follow-up drafts. Docs only; not sent.

## 153. CI fails on a repeated PLAN number (round 92, 2026-10-05)

- `tests/config-docs.test.ts`: `planClashes` lists any `## N.` heading number used more than once in PLAN.md, outside a code block, with the line numbers; the old repeats (94 and 110 to 120) are tolerated exactly twice each, so a third, or any new repeat, fails. It lives in the test a docs-only change already runs in CI, so a PLAN.md-only pull request is checked too (the collisions of 133, 136, 138, 148 and 150 were all in PLAN-only or mixed merges).
- Tests: the real PLAN.md has no new repeat; a second test pins the checker on small fixtures (a repeat, an old repeat twice and a third, 120 against 121, a heading inside a code block). Mutations: adding a duplicate heading, tolerating everything, and tolerating the old repeats without limit each fail a test.
- What it does not do: it cannot stop two sessions picking the same number at the same time; the later merge fails CI and renumbers its own section, which is the rule at the top of the file.

## 163. A failed model call is logged, paused for a minute, and not remembered (2026-10-05)
Before, any error from the model (a refused key, an outage) was swallowed: the plain answer was shown and, for scouting reports, previews and searches, cached for the rest of the day. A key added after the fact, or an outage that ended, changed nothing until tomorrow, and nobody saw why.
- `lib/ai-guard.ts`: `AiFailed`, `notRemembered(e)` (a refused or failed call), `aiPaused()` and `noteAiFailure(reason)`. After a failure no call is made for a minute (no waiting, no hammering of a broken key), and the cause goes to the server log once per distinct reason (`[ai] model call failed ...`), never to a page.
- `lib/ai.ts` `claude()` turns every failure into `AiFailed` with a plain reason (401 says the key was refused, 404 names the model). Search, scouting reports, previews and Ask treat it like a refusal: the rules answer is shown and not cached. `/api/ask` still reports `limited` only for a real limit.
- Visitors see no change. Test: `tests/ai-guard.test.ts`.
- The Wikidata staging table and what the enrichment wrote survive a second, fuller vendor load (ids stay, no staged row is dropped): `tests/reload-keeps-staging.test.ts`. Noted, not changed: a reload does not update a fighter's `weight_class`, `country`, `birth_year`, `turned_pro` or `nickname` (they are not in the upsert's update list), so a value read from a partial first load stays after the complete one.

## 110. The next main event, counts of coming cards, and a belt's kind (round 62, 2026-10-04)
Twenty questions about cards and titles put to the live Ask page. Four were wrong or missing: "what is the next main event" had **no answer** although "who headlines the next card" does; "how many cards are scheduled this month" (and "how many events are upcoming", "how many cards are coming up") was **"There have been 610 completed cards"**, the count of the past; "who is the interim champion" was **every champion**, and "how many times has the IRA bantamweight title changed hands" was **a search of bantamweights**. Now "next / upcoming / coming main event, headliner or big fight" is the next card; a count of coming cards (and "how many fights does the next card have") is no answer, since the events list has no such count (a count of fights coming up is still the list of coming cards); a question about interim, stripped or unified belts, a belt "changing hands" or "how many times has the title…" is no answer. The comparisons of two named fighters ("who is older A or B", "who has the better record A or B") already answered with both facts and were right. Checked: types, lint, 792 tests (791 pass, 1 skipped as before; 3 new in `tests/ask-cards-titles.test.ts`, including the questions that had an answer and keep it), the Ask battery 100%, build, smoke 211/211, 8 deliberate mutations: 6 killed; two cannot differ ("vacant" and "how many titles" are already no answer by another route). Not done: "which fighters are on the October 12 card" and "who is favoured in Morishita vs Hartmann" (surnames) still have no answer; the number of title changes of a belt could be worked out from the lineage and is not.

## 111. The Arabic twins of the fighter facts (round 63, 2026-10-04)
Twenty Arabic questions put to the live Ask page. The facts added in round 61 had no Arabic wording, so "متى احترف X" (when did he turn pro), "ما أسلوب X" (his style), "من مروج X" (his promoter), "كم نزالا فاز بالنقاط X" (his decisions), "هل X معتزل" (is he retired) and "من يدرب X" (who trains him) were the **profile**; "ما لقب X" (his nickname: "لقب" is both a nickname and a title) was **whether he holds a belt**; and "ما أطول سلسلة انتصارات لـ X" (his longest winning streak) was **his height** (the "طول" inside "أطول"). Also: "من الأكبر سنا A أم B" (who is older) was a **prediction of who would win**; "من هو البطل المؤقت" (the interim champion) was **every champion**; "من هو أفضل ملاكمة" (the best female boxer, singular) was **the best of all 966**; "أفضل ملاكمين في وزن الوسط" (the best in the middle weight) **lost the division**; "من هو أكثر ملاكم نزالا" (who has fought the most) had no answer.
- Each fact now has its Arabic wording, the streak is read before the height ("أطول" with "سلسلة" is the longest run; between two fighters it is still the taller), "ما لقب" is the nickname while "حزام" and "الألقاب" stay the belts, "أكبر / أصغر سنا" between two fighters is the age of each, "مؤقت / شاغر / تغيّر حامل" is no answer as in English, the singular "ملاكمة" is a woman, "وزن الوسط" is the middleweight, and "أكثر ملاكم نزالا" sorts by fights.
- Checked: types, lint, 795 tests (794 pass, 1 skipped as before; 3 new in `tests/ask-arabic-facts.test.ts`), the Ask battery 100%, build, smoke; 13 deliberate mutations, all killed (the first run of the tests before any mutation was clean, which matters: a failing baseline would make every mutation look killed). Not done: "هل التقى A و B من قبل" is answered with the prediction (it also says "they have never met", as in English); "كم بطاقة قادمة" has no answer.

## 112. Who a trainer trains, and money the lists cannot give (round 64, 2026-10-04)
Twenty questions about trainers, money and weight put to the live Ask page. Wrong in a way that looks right: "how many fighters does Saud Al-Dosari train" was **his profile** (he is a fighter too); "which trainers have trained champions" was **the champions**; "how many pay-per-view events were there" was **the biggest pay-per-view** ("Most pay-per-view buys: …"); "who missed weight the most" was **the fighters who missed weight, by rating**; "how much did Marcus Larkin earn" was **his profile**; "who is the top earner in 2025" was **the all-time top earners** (the year dropped). Now "how many fighters does X train / coach / manage / promote" is the team filter (the trainer by his own name, even when he is also a fighter), and no answer for a name nobody has (it had been every fighter); no answer for a count of money items (pay-per-views, gates, purses), for what one fighter earned or is worth, for a year on the money lists (they are all-time), for who missed weight the most (no tool counts the misses per fighter), and for which trainers trained champions. The lists keep their answers: the highest earner, the biggest purse, the largest gate, the fighters who have missed weight. Checked: types, lint, 810 tests (809 pass, 1 skipped as before; 3 new in `tests/ask-money-team.test.ts`), the Ask battery 100%, build, smoke 211/211; 8 deliberate mutations, 7 killed and one cannot differ (the gates and purses in the count refusal are already no answer by another route). Not done: what a named fighter earned could come from the earners list's own totals and does not; "which gym produced the most champions" and "who manages the most fighters" still have no answer (no tool).

## 113. "Rated above 1600", sort words, and rank movement (round 65, 2026-10-04)
Twenty questions about rankings, ratings and predictions put to the live Ask page. Wrong in a way that looks right: "how many fighters are rated above 1600" was **all 966 fighters** (there was no rating filter, so the number was dropped); "rank the welterweights by knockouts" was sorted **by rating**; "who climbed the rankings the most" and "who dropped out of the top 10" were **the pound-for-pound ranking** (no history of ranks is kept); "who is the lowest rated champion" was **every champion**; "who is rated highest" had no answer. Now the fighter search has `minRating` and `maxRating`, the rating as shown (a whole number: the filter is on the rounded rating, so "above 1600" is 1601 and up as the page prints it), read from "rated above / under / at least / between A and B", "a rating of …", "Elo over …" and in Arabic from "تصنيفهم أكثر من 1600", "بتصنيف 1650 فأكثر", "تصنيفهم بين …" (chips in both languages, the model's JSON, the tool's arguments); "rank / sort / order / list X by knockouts, wins, rating, age, height, reach, fights" is that order; "rated highest" is the rating order; rank movement and "the lowest or worst champion" are no answer. "Who is the best champion" is still the champions list. Checked: types, lint, 818 tests (817 pass, 1 skipped as before; 3 new in `tests/ask-rating.test.ts`), the Ask battery and the sentence battery 100%, build, smoke; 2 Arabic strings; 14 deliberate mutations, all killed (three needed a test for the singular, "rated highest" and the second wording of rank movement). Not done: the average rating of a division ("average rating of welterweights") has no tool; "predict Morishita vs Hartmann" (surnames only) has no answer.

## 114. Typos, short names and qualities the data does not hold (round 66, 2026-10-04)
Twenty questions typed carelessly ("whos the best welterwieght", "larkin's age", "is larkin retired", "show me KO artists", "best defensive fighter") put to the live Ask page. Most were fine (a misspelled division, "top 5 heavys", "who's the champ at heavy", "whos fighting next", the US as a country). Wrong in a way that looks right: **"is larkin retired" was the list of all 545 retired fighters** (the surname alone names nobody, so the sentence became a filter); **"best defensive fighter" was the best-rated fighter**; and "show me KO artists" had no answer. Now a question of the form "is / was / has X retired / active / undefeated / a southpaw / a champion", with one word that names nobody, is no answer (a plain list, and "is everyone retired", still are lists); a superlative of a quality the data does not hold (defence, footwork, hand speed, stamina, conditioning, heart, toughness, chin) is no answer; "KO artist(s)" is the knockout-artist style. I tried reading a surname alone as the fighter (when only one fighter has it) and took it out again: in the demo league no surname is unique (89 surnames, each shared), and in any league a unique one would make a wrong guess possible for the people a fan most expects to find by surname. Checked: types, lint, 821 tests (820 pass, 1 skipped as before; 3 new in `tests/ask-unnamed.test.ts`), the Ask battery 100%, build, smoke; 5 deliberate mutations: 4 killed, one cannot differ in a test (the quality words other than "defence" are already no answer by another route). Not done: "larkin's age", "how many wins does larkin have" and "tell me bout morishita" still have no answer (no name to ask about); a full-name question with a slip is already forgiven (round 38).

## 115. A record as it is written, a draw fought, a year without a fight (round 67, 2026-10-04)
Twenty-four combined search sentences put through the plain-English search with the filters and the count printed. Four qualifiers were dropped without a word, so the result was a bigger set than the sentence describes: "welterweights who are 5-0" was **all 63 welterweights** (a record as it is written had no reading); "active fighters who haven't fought in a year" was **all 420 active fighters** (round 53 read "over / at least a year", not the bare "a year"); "fighters who have fought a draw and never been stopped" **lost the draw**; "undefeated in 2025" was **"unbeaten in the last 2025 fights"**, then, once that was refused, all the undefeated; "rated 1500-1600" lost its upper limit. Now: "N-M" and "N-M-D" are exactly those wins and losses (and draws), read last so that a counted range ("5-10 wins"), a span of years ("2015-2018", "2015-18") and a date ("12-10-2024") are not taken for one; "haven't fought in a year / 2 years" is a year without a fight (at least that long); "have fought a draw", "fought to a draw" is a draw on the record; "undefeated or unbeaten in 2025" is no result rather than everyone undefeated (no loss within a year is not a filter we have); a rating range with a hyphen is a range. Measured with a new group J (16 sentences) in the sentence battery: **3 of 16 on the search as it was (19%; the three are the rating sentences that already worked), 16 of 16 now**; one of my first sets of sentences had records that nobody in the league has (every answer was empty, which an ignoring search would pass), and the guard that no answer key is empty or everyone caught it before the measurement counted. Checked: types, lint, 825 tests (824 pass, 1 skipped as before; 3 new in `tests/search-record.test.ts`), the Ask battery and all ten sentence groups 100%, build, smoke; 9 deliberate mutations, 8 killed on the first tests and the lookbehind needed another ("2015-18"). Not done: no second unseen batch this round (the round is a set of gaps found by reading, not a new family); "fighters who won by knockout in their last fight" (how the last fight ended) is still no result; "fighters ranked in the top 10 at heavyweight" in the plain search is the division by rating (the Ask page answers it from the rankings).

## 116. One fighter's fights, by year and by kind (round 68, 2026-10-04)
Twenty questions about fights, methods and one fighter's bouts put to the live Ask page. Wrong in a way that looks right: "who did Marcus Larkin fight in 2025" was **all 592 fights of 2025** (the fighter dropped); "how many fights did X have in 2024" was **his whole record** (the year dropped) and "how many decisions did X have in 2024" **his career decisions**; "list X's fights" and "show X's last 5 fights" were **his profile**; "how many split decisions" was **every decision** (4,195); "how many knockdowns in the last card" was **the latest card**; "list X's upcoming fights" was the profile. Now a question about one named fighter's fights is the `bouts` tool for that fighter ("list / show / last N fights", with the limit), with a year it keeps the fighter and the year ("who did X fight in 2025", "how many fights, title fights, decisions did X have in 2024"), "split / majority / unanimous decisions" are those methods, "his upcoming or next fights" is the next-fight fact (plural was missed), and two questions have no answer because the list would mix what they ask: a count of knockouts in a fighter's year (the bouts list holds his losses by knockout too) and knockdowns within a card. One fight ("his last fight", "his next fight") is still the fact. Checked: types, lint, 829 tests (828 pass, 1 skipped as before; 4 new in `tests/ask-fights-of-one.test.ts`, each against the league's own fights), the Ask battery 100%, build, smoke; 11 deliberate mutations, all killed. Not done: "which fights went the distance", "how many upsets happened in 2025", "what percentage of fights end in a knockout" and "longest fight by rounds" have no answer (no list or ratio for them); the bouts list for a fighter shows fights of both outcomes, so "how many fights has X won in 2025" is still his fights of the year, not his wins (the answer's own lines say who won).

Now a grouping of the league (each / every / per division, stance or country; "which division / stance has the most / oldest / …"; "the division with the most …"), a share (what percentage, what proportion), "the most common …" and "unranked" are no answer (no tool groups or shares); "the champions of every weight class" and "rankings in every division" keep their lists; "debuted / turned pro this year" is the year of the debut, counted from the league's own date (and "last year" the same way). A count of one group ("how many fighters are in the heavyweight division", "how many southpaws") is still a count. ## 118. A signed number in Arabic text (round 70, 2026-10-04)
Found by looking at the Arabic fighter page at 390 px and measuring where each sign sits: **"+12.5 رطل" (rehydration) is drawn "12.5+ رطل"**: after Arabic words a sign that begins a number is drawn after its digits, so a gain of 12.5 lb reads like "12.5 or more". Round 55's check looked only at records. Four Arabic strings had the sign in their text ("+{n} رطل", "+{n} أخرى", "إعادة الترطيب +{n} رطل", "{name} (+{n} رطل)") and one sentence ("… الملاكم الذي مدربه +30 …" on the trainers page); each now has a left-to-right mark in front of the sign. A signed number put into an Arabic sentence as a value is isolated like a record (`isolateNumeric`), and the smoke check `flippedRecords` also fails an Arabic page that has a bare sign (+ or −) in front of a number after Arabic words: it found the trainers sentence on its first run, and no page now. Checked in the browser on the rebuilt fighter page ("+12.5" has its sign first), types, lint, 833 tests (832 pass, 1 skipped as before; 1 new in `tests/flipped-records.test.ts`, which also reads `i18n/ar.json` and fails on a bare sign), build, smoke on all five feeds; 5 deliberate mutations, 4 killed on the first tests and one (the minus sign) after another test. Not done: other marks the right-to-left order can move (a percent sign, a plus sign after the number as in "20+ wins": "20+" is drawn "+20" after Arabic words and is the next thing to measure); GitHub Actions is still not starting (the account's payment or spending limit).
