## 120. The other orders: lowest rating, lowest knockout rate, best record, most losses, draws and times stopped (round 72, 2026-10-04)
Twenty "who has the most / lowest / best" questions put to the live Ask page. Wrong in a way that looks right: **"lowest rated heavyweight" was the highest rated** (only the descending orders existed); "lowest knockout rate among fighters with 10 wins" and "best record among fighters with 20 fights" were sorted **by rating**; "who has the most losses", "most draws", "fighter with the most draws" and "who has been stopped the most" had **no answer** or were sorted by rating; "who has fought the most rounds" was **the most fights**; "most knockouts in a single year" was **the all-time knockout list**. The fighter search now has six more orders (`sort`: `lowRating`, `lowKoRate`, `winRate`, `losses`, `draws`, `stopped`; the sort chips, the answer's "by …" and the tool's arguments name them in both languages, 6 Arabic strings): "lowest rated / lowest rating / lowest Elo", "lowest knockout rate" (a fighter with no wins has no rate and goes last), "best record / best win rate / highest winning percentage", "most losses / defeats", "most draws / drawn the most", "most knockout losses / most losses by knockout / stopped the most / most times knocked out". The record lists keep their own questions ("who has the most wins", "the highest knockout rate"). Rounds fought (most, fewest, total), and "in a single year", "in a year", "per year" with a superlative, are no answer (no tool counts either). Round 53's expectation that "the most draws" has no answer is changed: it is now this order. Checked: types, lint, 837 tests (836 pass, 1 skipped as before; 3 new in `tests/ask-sort-more.test.ts`, each order checked against the league's own numbers), the Ask battery and all ten sentence groups 100%, build, smoke on all five feeds; 21 deliberate mutations over three runs, all killed in the end (the first tests missed the "rating / Elo" wording, "win rate", the plural of "rounds" and "per year"). Not done: "worst record" and "worst fighter" have no answer (the meaning is not one order); "who is the heaviest" (no weights in the data); the fastest or longest of anything except knockouts.

## 124. Importer hygiene and three ways to choose the N fighters (round 73, 2026-10-04)

From the first real fetch (5,000 fighters: 1,189 validator errors, 219 conflicts, 3.6% of records adding up): the errors were partly the importer's own doing, and keeping the N most recent left most records short.

- **Hygiene** (`mapFight`, `load`): a fight of a fighter against himself is skipped; KO/TKO with no winner becomes a no result (`stoppageWithoutWinner`); a stoppage round past the scheduled rounds lengthens the fight (`roundsRaisedToEnd`); fighters with no weight division are dropped together with their fights (`fightersDroppedNoDivision`, `boutsDroppedNoDivision`), so `sanitizeFeed` no longer cascades them into `bad_reference`.
- **Selection** (`chooseByMode` in `lib/vendor-selection.ts`): `recent` (default), `opponents` (`--with-opponents`: the N plus every opponent of the N, not opponents of opponents) and `groups` (`--whole-groups`: whole linked groups, newest first, taken only if they fit in N). `--plan` prints what each would ask for at each size. Both flags require `--fighters`, and not together.
- Writing the test found a real bug: `opponents` chained, adding opponents of opponents depending on the order of the fights, because the set it tested against grew while it looped.
- Tests: `tests/vendor-selection-modes.test.ts` (modes, plan block, end to end on the mock vendor: complete records, no conflicts, never more than N), hygiene test in `tests/boxing-data-api.test.ts`; four mutations killed.
- Not shown: how the user's real data behaves. That needs their `--check` with the new flags (their key and cache), not run here.

## 125. Why a record conflicts, and draws the vendor never recorded (round 74, 2026-10-04)

The first real load was refused for 219 conflicts ("loaded 0-0-1, vendor 18-0-0"). The importer turned every finished decision with no winner into a draw; the feed also leaves the winner out of fights it has not settled, so draws appeared that the vendor never recorded.

- **Draw fix** (`demoteUnsupportedDraws`, run at the end of `load()`): a drawn fight is kept only if each fighter's career record has a draw to spare, counted fight by fight; otherwise it becomes "no result yet" (`drawDemoted`). A fighter with no career record cannot be checked and keeps the draw. Which of two draws is kept for a fighter with room for one is arbitrary.
- **Explainer** (`explainConflicts`, `describeConflictReport` in `lib/vendor-verify.ts`): for every conflict, the cause read from the fights themselves: `draw`, `repeat` (same pair twice within 30 days), `same-day`, `recent` (the surplus goes away without the last 14 days), else `wins`/`losses`, else `unexplained`. `--check` always prints the tally when there are conflicts; `--explain-conflicts [--show N]` adds the fights behind the first N fighters.
- Not shown: whether the draw guess accounts for most of the user's 219; that needs their `--check --explain-conflicts` on their cache. Conflicts that are not draws (e.g. a loaded win the vendor lacks) are not fixed, only explained.
- Tests: `tests/vendor-verify.test.ts` (causes, report), `tests/boxing-data-api.test.ts` (unit and through a real load); six mutations killed.

## 126. Fetch pacing when no --per-hour is given (round 75, 2026-10-04)

The user's full fetch (30,000 fighters uncached) ran without `--per-hour`, sent about 200 fighters in two minutes and was refused by the Mega plan's hourly limit; the progress line then promised "4110 min to go".

- A run that was given no `--per-hour` adopts 400 an hour after the first rate-limit refusal (`autoPerHour`, `pacing()`), and says so. A run with `--per-hour` keeps its own.
- Before the fighters are fetched the run counts those not in the cache and says so, with a warning when there are more than 300 and no `--per-hour`.
- The progress line says how many are left to fetch, and estimates from the last 100 (waits included, never faster than the pace set) instead of from the whole run.
- One existing test asserted that the only sleep after a 429 was the Retry-After; the requests after a refusal are now paced, so it asserts the first sleep.
- Tests: two in `tests/boxing-data-api.test.ts`; three mutations killed (a fourth did not apply and was redone: killed).


## 130. --cached-only: look at a part-way fetch without a request (round 76, 2026-10-05)

The user's full fetch takes about 68 hours at 400 an hour, and a `--check` beside it is refused (one run per key, one hourly allowance). The cache already holds thousands of fighters, enough to see whether the draw fix and the conflict explainer (§125) do their job.

- `--cached-only` (with `--check` or a load, and `--cache-dir`) makes no request: `cachedOnly` in the adapter turns a cache miss into a 404 (so a missing schedule or rankings page is "unavailable" like on a plan without them), and `load()` leaves the fighters not in the cache out like fighters outside a selection (`boutsOutsideSelection`, not "could not be fetched", which would stop a load). An empty cache says the fight list is not in the cache.
- No API key needed (a placeholder the adapter never sends), no backfill lock (it shares no allowance) and no "storing provisionally" warning. Not with `--plan`, `--update` or `--refresh`.
- Tried against a throwaway cache with fighters removed: the user's running fetch's lock was left alone.
- Tests: two in `tests/vendor-selection-modes.test.ts` (nothing requested, missing fighters left out and the rest load; empty cache); three mutations killed, one (the pre-filter) only through the log line, since the loop's 404 would have skipped the same fighters.
- **Fix to round 75's start-up line:** "N fighters to fetch, about X minute(s) at P an hour" printed seconds under the word minutes (27,159 fighters at 400 an hour read "244431 minute(s)"; the true figure is 4,074). Found from the user's paced run; now `N * 60 / P`, with a test that pins 320 fighters at 450 an hour to 43 minutes. The progress lines already gave the right figure.

### Load-day rehearsal at the user's size (2026-10-05, while the real fetch ran)
`npm run vendor:rehearse -- --fighters 35000 --fights 44000 --years 10` against the stand-in vendor, on a temp database and cache, with its own key (so it shared nothing with the real fetch): `--plan` 6 s / 181 MB, `--check` (32,647 requests answered instantly) 71 s / 206 MB, the load 2.8 s / 246 MB with 0 requests, the daily `--update` 1.5 s / 178 MB, a kill-and-resume asked only the unfinished requests. All checks passed except one that was the check's own fault: a SIGKILL during a cache write leaves a stale `<name>.<pid>.tmp` (written then renamed, so never read as an answer); the check counted it as an unreadable answer. It now separates stale temp files (reported) from unreadable answers (failure); reproduced twice at full size and once at 3,000 fighters.
- Not exercised: the stand-in's career records always add up, so conflicts, `--allow-partial`, `--complete-only` and `--explain-conflicts` were not tested at scale by this run (they are covered by unit tests only).
- `docs/real-data-runbook.md` gains section 2c, the load-day decision (conflicts first; partial: `--allow-partial` or `--complete-only`; a new database; check by hand).

## 133. Rehearsing the load-day paths on a league that does not add up (round 77, 2026-10-05)

The round 76 rehearsal passed everything because its stand-in vendor always adds up; conflicts, partial records, `--complete-only` and `--cached-only` had only unit tests. `degradeWorld` (`lib/vendor-mock.ts`) lays the first real fetch's faults over a clean league (earlier careers the list does not reach; fights the vendor does not count; fights listed twice) and `npm run vendor:rehearse -- --realistic [--prior 0.04]` runs the real command four ways on it, then a second league with only unposted results.

At 35,000 fighters / 44,000 fights (stand-in vendor, temp folders, its own key), all checks pass:
- A (faults): `--check` refuses (95.4% add up, 170 conflicts); the draw guess is demoted (132) and not left as conflicts; the fights listed twice are named as repeats; `--cached-only` asks for 0 requests and gives the same report in 3 s; `--complete-only` keeps 783 of 32,175 fighters (every record in it right); `--allow-partial` is refused while conflicts stand and writes nothing.
- B (unposted results only): the load goes through (99.9%+ add up), with no new request. Not always 100%: a fighter with a real draw and an unposted fight cannot be told apart, so the wrong one of his two draws can be kept (a short record, never a conflict). The 2-in-3,000 residue is that.
- `--complete-only` is small however much is fetched: a group is kept only if every fighter in it is right, and one short fighter sinks the group (runbook section 2c).
- The B step's check and load took 10 and 9 minutes against 70 s and 4 s for the same work in A: the Mac went to maintenance sleep (07:28-07:37) in the middle; not a slowdown in the code.
- Found on the way: `BOXING_API_KEY=` (set, but empty) was not treated as unset by `--cached-only`; it now is.
- Tests: `tests/vendor-degrade.test.ts`; the rehearsal's checks are the end-to-end test (run by hand, not in `npm test`: minutes at full size).
- **Found by the user's first `--cached-only` on their real cache (2026-10-05):** "/v2/fights/ is not in the cache". A fight list longer than a page number can reach (477 pages) is read in date windows, halved from 1900-01-01 to *today + 60 days*, and each window's cache file is named by its end date, so the next UTC day every window missed: a run resumed after midnight read the whole list again (an hour of the plan's allowance at 400 an hour), and `--cached-only` could read nothing. A cache that already holds the list is now read with the window end it was made with (`cachedWindowEnd`, logged: "reading the fight list from the cache as it was made"); `--refresh`, `--refresh-lists`, `--since` and an empty cache use today as before. Test: a list filled on one day is read on the next with 0 requests (and by `--cached-only`); `--refresh` ends its windows today; two mutations killed.

## 137. What the first real cache showed, and four repairs (round 78, 2026-10-05)

The user's `--check --cached-only` on 9,700 fighters of their real fetch: 261 of 8,414 records add up (3.1%), 223 conflicts (50 extra wins, 107 extra losses, 46 the same fight twice, 44 two fights one day, 24 trailing totals), and the draw guess gone from the tally. The fights behind the examples showed the vendor holds the same bout under two id generations (`671c...` and `696...`, one record a day later, sometimes KO against TKO, a few with different winners), amateur and exhibition bouts in the list, and totals trailing the last days' results. The user chose all four repairs.
- **Merge duplicate fights** (`mergeDuplicateFights`, in `load()` before the draw check): the same two fighters within a day are one fight; agreeing copies keep the one with a result (then scores, then lowest id), disagreeing copies leave one as "no result yet" (never a guessed winner). Counted (`duplicateFightsMerged`, `duplicateFightsDisagree`). Not merged: months apart, two days apart, cancelled, undated.
- **Lagging in a load**: `reconcileFeed(feed, vendor, {today, days})` (as `reconcileDb` already did for the update): a conflict that disappears without the last 7 days' fights is `lagging`, not a conflict (script: `LAG_DAYS`, `VENDOR_LAG_DAYS`). The explainer takes the same window, so its tally agrees with the records line.
- **`--drop-conflicts`** (`dropConflicted`; `--dropped-file path.csv`): the conflicted fighters leave with their fights and are listed; removal can only shorten opponents' records (partial), never create a conflict. Not with `--allow-conflicts`, `--complete-only` or `--update`.
- **`fightsSkipped` split** by its first reason: no id, no fighter id, no date, same fighter (the total stays).
- Tests: `tests/vendor-round78.test.ts` (six), degrade faults in `tests/vendor-degrade.test.ts`, the rehearsal's scenarios A (wrong totals + duplicates + disagreeing duplicates: `--check` refuses, the tally names only the wrong totals, `--drop-conflicts --allow-partial` loads without exactly those fighters), C (duplicates only: the load passes) and B; seven mutations of the importer and verifier each fail a test (one needed a new case: the copy with a result must win over the one without). Two existing tests assumed three fights between the same two fighters on one day were three fights (pagination test, hostile-league accounting).
- Not repaired (cannot be, from the fights): amateur and exhibition bouts the vendor's totals leave out; a vendor total that contradicts its own fight list (a fighter with a win and a record of 0-12-0); both are what `--drop-conflicts` is for.

- Rehearsed at 35,000 fighters / 44,000 fights on the stand-in vendor (temp folders, its own key): with 88 duplicated fights, 29 disagreeing, 132 unposted results and 66 wrong totals, `--check` reads 95.5% complete with 64 conflicts left (all wrong totals; the other 2 fighters were short as well, so partial, not conflicting), and `--cached-only --drop-conflicts --allow-partial` leaves out exactly those 64 and loads the rest in 3 s / 312 MB; the duplicates-only league passes the gate (88 of 88 merged); every base check passes.
- Found while testing: two paging tests assumed fights between the same two fighters on one day were separate fights (a random league does that by chance); `mergeDuplicates: false` (provider option, tests only) keeps their counts exact.

## 139. What a visitor sees after a partial load: record answers use the record the page shows (round 79, 2026-10-05)

Loaded a simulated real league (3,000 fighters with the first real fetch's faults, `--drop-conflicts --allow-partial`) into a separate database and served it on its own port, as the runbook's "check the result by hand" step asks. The fighter pages were right (a part-held career shows the supplier's total, labelled: "Ringside holds 19 of those 20 fights"), but "undefeated super featherweights" listed Fighter 797 first, with "(14-1-1)" beside the name: the nine fights held hold no loss, the career has one. On the real feed ~94% of careers are held in part, so every record question would have been answered from the held fights.
- `careerCounts` and `knockouts` (`lib/career.ts`): the counts a filter or sort key compares are the career as `careerView`/`koView` show it. `applyFilters` (undefeated, win/loss/draw/fight counts, winning/losing record, knockouts, KO rate, times stopped) and its sort keys use them; a part-held career with no supplier knockout totals has unknown knockouts, which never satisfy a filter on them and sort last (the repo's rule for unknown facts). With no supplier totals nothing changes (the demo league, the batteries: 100%).
- The Ask facts say the same record as the page: "is active and unbeaten ({record})", "has had {n} fights", "{kos} knockouts in {wins} wins", the KO columns of the fighter lists and the KO rate in the fight preview, and the fight posters' record line (`Poster.tsx`).
- Left as they are, on purpose: the all-time lists ("counted from the fights on record; earlier careers are not counted", as the page says), the ratings, streaks, form and the country/stance analytics (all built from the fights held, which the fighter page already says).
- Tests: `tests/partial-career-answers.test.ts` (four, fake fighters: part-held with and without supplier knockouts, whole, no supplier), `tests/partial-career-world.test.ts` (a 500-of-3,000 league ingested through the importer: nobody listed as undefeated has a loss on the page, nobody with none is missing); four mutations killed, one needed a sort test whose tie order cannot hide it.

## 138. Lag in a load is 14 days; the explainer names suspected exhibitions (round 80, 2026-10-05)

From the user's `--check --cached-only --drop-conflicts` on abc026f: 200 conflicted fighters dropped (Mayweather, Nasukawa and Jalolov among them), only 3 totals lagging at the 7-day window, while 24 conflicts had vanished at 14 days in the first tally; Hugo Grau's 09-24 loss and Chad Lebaron's 09-26 loss were still missing from the vendor's totals 9 to 11 days on.
- `lagDays("update" | "load")` (`lib/vendor-backfill.ts`): the daily update keeps 7 (`VENDOR_LAG_DAYS`: its fighter records are fresh); a load reads a cache that may be days old and allows 14 (`VENDOR_LOAD_LAG_DAYS`, else `VENDOR_LAG_DAYS`, else 14). Nonsense values are ignored.
- The conflict explainer prints each fight's scheduled rounds and names a new cause, `short`: the surplus goes away without the fights scheduled for 3 rounds or fewer (an amateur or exhibition bout the vendor's career total leaves out; professional bouts are 4 or more). It only reports; whether to leave such fights out of a record is a decision for after the next real tally shows how many there are.
- Not built, awaiting the user: keeping the 3,413 fights against opponents with no profile id as name-only fighters (it would add many one-fight fighters and some duplicates of real profiles).
- Tests: the cause and the rounds column (`tests/vendor-verify.test.ts`), `lagDays` (`tests/vendor-round78.test.ts`); two mutations killed.

## 140. The site at the size of the real feed (round 81, 2026-10-05)

Loaded a 32,152-fighter / 43,889-bout league (the stand-in vendor with the first real fetch's faults, `--drop-conflicts --allow-partial`) and served it: start-up 1.2 s, pages 11-100 ms, 117-135 pages a second at 30-50 at a time, no failures; 761 MB resident uncapped after 2,000 requests, 565 MB with the heap capped at 400 MB, and an out-of-memory failure at start with a 250 MB cap. `docs/deploy.md` has the numbers and the advice (1 GB, or a 400 MB heap cap in 768 MB).
- The load test counted 5% "failures" that were its own: the default paths name two demo-league fighters, which are 404s on any other league. It now takes two fighters that exist (`withSlugs`, from `/api/fighters`) when the demo ones are missing, and says so; 0 failures after.
- Nothing in the site needed fixing for size.
- **From the user's check on 33d6f61 (2026-10-05):** the 14-day window moved lagging from 3 to 21 and the fighters left out from 200 to 183 (the cache also grew); but the "why" block did not print, because `--drop-conflicts` explained the feed after the conflicts had been dropped (none left). It now explains them before dropping them. New cause `flipped`: reversing the winner of ONE counted fight would remove the conflict (the dropped list shows Julian Duran 1-0-0 against a vendor 0-1-0, Dymond Harris 0-1-0 against 1-0-0, Martin Molina Salvador 0-1-0 against 0-0-2): a winner flag in the fight list, or the vendor's total, may be reversed; the fights cannot say which. Reports only. Rehearsal step f now checks the tally appears with `--drop-conflicts`; one mutation of `flipped` killed.

## 141. Which side is wrong: flip evidence, and fights under two profiles of one opponent (round 82, 2026-10-05)

The user's real check on d296f66: of 183 conflicts, 99 would clear with one reversed winner (`flipped`), 14 had the same opponent twice within 30 days and 11 two fights on one date, and `short` was 1 (exhibitions are not detectable by scheduled rounds: the Olatunji exhibition is 12 rounds). Mayweather's 53-0-0 against 50-0-0 includes Canelo Alvarez on 2013-09-14 and 2013-09-15 under two fight ids that my merge did not catch, because it paired fighters by ids and the two records name two PROFILES of Canelo.
- **Flip evidence** (`explainConflicts`): a one-fight reversal that would clear a fighter's conflict is also asked what it does to the other fighter: `mutual` (it clears his conflict too: the list's winner flag looks reversed), `open` (no record to check, or partial and stays partial) or `contradicted` (he adds up exactly now and would be broken, or a partial record would become a conflict: that winner looks right, so the vendor's total is the odd one). Three lines under `flipped` in the tally; reports only. The first reading of 99 was permissive (one extra win with spare loss room is "fixed" by any flip); this is the test that separates a wrong flag from a wrong total.
- **Merge across profiles** (`mergeDuplicateFights(…, names)`): the pair is the two fighters' names (case, accents, spacing ignored) where known, so the same fight under two profiles of one opponent, a day apart, is one fight (`duplicateFightsAcrossProfiles` counts those copies). A same-named pair of different people fighting the same opponent within a day is not a case.
- Tests: three for the evidence, one for the merge across profiles, one through a real load, a `reversed` fault in `degradeWorld` and rehearsal scenario D (24 reversed winners: 48 conflicts, 48 flipped, 48 mutual); four mutations killed.
- GitHub Actions did not start for 873377f and d296f66 (the billing message again, 0 steps run): the fresh-clone verification is the check until the user fixes Billing.

## 142. `npm run vendor:status` (round 83, 2026-10-05)

The same questions kept costing round trips during the first real fetch: is a fetch running, is the key set in THIS tab (twice the placeholder was typed instead), how much of the league is cached, how long is left, which command is next (and from which folder). `scripts/vendor-status.ts` / `lib/vendor-status.ts` answer them from files on this machine only: the cache directory counted (fighters, fight-list pages, rankings, stale temp files, how many fighters arrived in the last hour and six), the fight list read from the cache for the total (`cachedOnly`, no request), the backfill locks with a live process, the key described (never printed: set, length, plainly a placeholder), storage confirmation and `DATABASE_PATH`, and a next command for the state (leave it running / set the key / restart the paced fetch / the cache is complete: check, then runbook 2c). Time left uses the recent pace, else 400 an hour. Tested on a throwaway mock cache; three unit tests (counts, locks and key, the report and each next step).

`docs/boxing-data-api-data-findings.md`: a draft email (not sent; for the owner to send) with the inconsistencies found in the first ~11,300 fighters of the real fetch, each with the vendor's own fight ids so they can be looked up and fixed at the source: the same bout under two id generations and two profiles of one opponent (~120 copies), career totals that contradict the fighter's own fights (172 fighters, ~2%; losses under-counted twice as often as wins; 38 cases where the opponent's exact record corroborates the list), exhibition and amateur bouts counted as professional, 3,413 fights where a fighter has no `fighter_id`, 6,059 finished fights with no result, and the smaller oddities (504 KOs with no winner, 499 stoppages past the scheduled rounds, etc). It answers the question in `boxing-data-api-vendor-email.md` section 3 (are the `stats` totals kept in step with the fights?) and is meant to follow it. Every figure and id was taken from output the owner pasted; nothing was read from their cache. Docs only.

## 144. A network outage is waited out, not skipped through (round 84, 2026-10-05)

Found by the user's first `npm run vendor:status`: the real fetch ran at a steady ~380 fighters an hour from 19:00 to 05:00 UTC, then fell to 70, 6 and 89 an hour. Its terminal showed "network error ... (fetch failed); retrying", four retries ten seconds apart and then "fighter ... skipped: unreachable", fighter after fighter (the laptop had slept and woken with the network down; the same hour a `git fetch` to GitHub was reset). Each skipped fighter is not cached, so it costs the fetch another request later and, if it were never fetched, a hole that the check refuses.
- With `--patience-min` (the runbook's advice for the long fetch: 240) a network failure is now an outage to wait out: 10 s, 30 s, 1, 2, 5, then 10 minutes at a time for that one request (`NET_STEPS_MS`), carrying on when it is back; after the whole patience it throws `NetworkError`, which stops the run (as a rate limit does) with "The network looks down: run the same command again", and what is fetched is cached. Without patience the old behaviour (four quick retries, then skip) is unchanged.
- `vendor:status` takes the six-hour pace for the time left (one bad hour no longer reads as 12 days) and says SLOWED when the last hour is under half of it, pointing at the fetch's own terminal.
- The user's running fetch has the old code in memory, so it keeps skipping until restarted (the same command, resumable; a skipped fighter is fetched on that run).
- Tests: waiting out five failures (none skipped, the waits 10/30/60/120/300 s), a network that never returns (stops after 220 s of a 5-minute patience, as `NetworkError`), no patience (skips as before); three mutations killed (the rethrow, the steps, the patience limit); the SLOWED report and the steadier pace in `tests/vendor-status.test.ts`.

## 145. `npm run vendor:fetch`: the key from a file, not from the terminal tab (round 85, 2026-10-05)

The fetch's key was handled badly again and again: the hidden `read -s` prompt typed into a chat command box (six times; the box cannot hide input, so the key may have been recorded), the command run in tabs that did not have the key, placeholders exported. `scripts/vendor-fetch.ts` / `lib/vendor-fetch.ts`:
- `--setup`: a hidden prompt (raw-mode, no echo) that saves the key to `~/.ringside-key` with mode 600; it refuses to run without a real terminal (stdin and stdout must be TTYs) so a chat box cannot appear to take the key.
- the fetch: reads the file (refusing a file other users can read, an empty one, and one that is plainly a placeholder: `looksLikePlaceholder`, deliberately narrow: nothing but placeholder words and separators, so a real key containing "here" is not refused), starts `vendor-backfill` with `--check --per-hour 400 --patience-min 240` and the cache folder (`~/ringside-real/vendor-cache` when it exists), the key in the child's environment only, `caffeinate -i -w <pid>` on a Mac; the user's options win over the same default; `--no-check` for the load.
- `vendor:status` shows the key file's state and, with no key in the tab, suggests `npm run vendor:fetch` (or `--setup`), and its placeholder test is the same narrow one (it used to flag any key containing "your", "paste" or "here").
- Tests: the file's mode and refusals, the narrow placeholder rule, the key never in a report, the arguments, the status next steps, and the wrapper end to end on a stand-in vendor (the key from a file, the fetch into the cache with 25 fighters, the key not printed, a second run with 0 requests, no file: how to make one, `--setup` refused outside a terminal and nothing written); four mutations killed.
- Not done by me: creating the key file. That is the owner's, in a real terminal tab.

## 148. `npm run vendor:load`: the load, guided (round 88, 2026-10-05)

The load is the one step of the real-data plan that writes the league the site will show, and it was described as a command with environment variables. `scripts/vendor-load.ts` / `lib/vendor-load.ts` put the decisions where they can be seen: it prints the cache (and how many fighters it holds), the database (new or existing) and the dropped-fighters file; runs the check with the conflict report (`--drop-conflicts --allow-partial --explain-conflicts`, the policy the real cache needs, replaced by `--complete-only` or `--allow-conflicts` if given); and **writes nothing until `LOAD` is typed exactly** (`--yes` skips it; a non-interactive terminal refuses without it; `--dry-run` stops after the check). The key comes from the key file, never printed. The vendor's written permission to store its data is the owner's to state: `BOXING_API_STORAGE_CONFIRMED=1` or `--storage-confirmed`, never assumed by the tool (an explicit `=0` wins). After a load it points at the by-hand check (runbook section 4) and the daily update. `vendor:status` now suggests `vendor:load -- --dry-run` when the cache is complete.
- Tests: the plan (defaults, the replacements, passthrough, the wrapper's own flags not passed on, the exact confirmation) and the wrapper end to end on a stand-in vendor with 6 wrong totals: refused without the storage statement (nothing fetched, nothing written), `--dry-run` shows the check and the conflict tally and writes no database, no terminal and no `--yes` refuses after the check, `--yes` loads (the database holds 300+ fighters, the dropped ones are listed beside it, the key never printed); five mutations killed.

## 149. The "disputed" label: conflicted fighters kept, marked, and shown with the vendor's total (round 89, 2026-10-05)

The user asked for it after the first real check: ~2% of fighters (172 of ~8,700, Mayweather among them) have a loaded record above the vendor's own career total, and the evidence (flip evidence, §141) says the fights cannot decide which side is wrong. Dropping them was the safe default; this keeps them honestly.
- **Data**: `boxers.record_disputed` (new nullable column, `ADDED_COLUMNS`), `ProviderBoxer.recordDisputed` (true marks, false clears, undefined leaves, so the daily update never touches it: `COALESCE(excluded…, boxers…)`), `Boxer.recordDisputed` in the world.
- **Load**: `vendor-backfill --keep-disputed` marks every fighter whose loaded fights exceed the vendor's total (after the 14-day lag allowance) and lets the gate pass for them (not with `--drop-conflicts`, `--allow-conflicts`, `--complete-only`, `--update`); prints them (first 20) and `--disputed-file` lists all; with `--explain-conflicts` the why-tally comes first. `vendor:load` now defaults to it (`--drop-conflicts` is one flag away) and writes `disputed.csv` beside the database.
- **Display**: `careerView` has a third source, `disputed`: the vendor's total (and the vendor's knockouts with it), `total` = the total's fights, `held` = what Ringside holds. The fighter page shows it with "{n} fights in all", the existing "Worked out from the {held} fights Ringside holds" line, and a new note: "The data supplier's career total ({total} fights) and its own fight list disagree: the list holds {held} fights, with more wins, losses or draws than that total allows. The record shown is the supplier's total; the fight list, rating and rates on this page are built from the fights held." (Arabic written to match the existing partial-career note; reviewed by no native speaker, like the rest.) Filters, sorts, the Ask facts, the scouting text and the lists use the shown record, as for a partial career; rankings and common-opponents count it among the careers not held whole. The Data page has a row counting them. A smoke route visits one when the league has any.
- **Not done**: the mark is not recomputed by the daily update, so a fighter fixed at the vendor stays marked until the next load.
- Tests: `tests/record-disputed.test.ts` (the rule, the database round trip with true / undefined / false, the flag combinations), `tests/vendor-load.test.ts` (the new default and the end-to-end load marking exactly the listed fighters); seven mutations killed. Checked by hand on a simulated league (3,000 fighters; 10 kept and marked instead of dropped), English and Arabic.

## 150. `npm run vendor:enrich`: the steps after the load, guided (round 90, 2026-10-05)

The runbook listed the enrichment as five separate commands with two environment variables, to be run after the load. The user asked for it guided, like `vendor:load`. `scripts/vendor-enrich.ts` / `lib/vendor-enrich.ts`: six steps in the runbook's order (`staging` the Wikidata boxers, `enrich` link ours to them: no network, `champions` the four title-reign lists, `venues`, `headshots`, `entities`), each pointing at the existing script; it prints the database (and how many fighters it holds), your contact and every step with what it does and that **these call Wikidata, Wikipedia and Wikimedia Commons**, and runs nothing until `ENRICH` is typed (`--yes` skips; a non-interactive terminal refuses; `--dry-run` only lists, even without a contact).
- The contact is the owner's: `WIKIMEDIA_CONTACT` or `--contact`, an email address or a web page, never chosen or defaulted; the database must hold the loaded league (it refuses an empty one, saying to load first); `--steps`, `--skip` (an unknown step is an error) and `--media-limit`; a failed step stops the run and says how far it got.
- I did not run any step: they call third-party services, and nothing here asks them anything. Tests cover the plan, the steps (each script exists, the arguments are the runbook's), selection, the contact rule, the stop-at-first-failure runner, and the script's refusals (no contact, no loaded database, no typed confirmation) and its dry run; five mutations killed (a mutation of the database guard was deliberately not run, because the mutated script would have started the real steps).

## 151. CI that uses fewer Actions minutes (round 91, 2026-10-05)

CI stopped starting on 2026-10-05 05:59 UTC: "The job was not started because recent account payments have failed or your spending limit needs to be increased". The repository is private (free plan: 2,000 included minutes a month, spending limit $0) and 333 runs between 3 and 5 October (181 pushes to main, 152 pull-request updates, from several sessions pushing at once) used about 1,940 minutes of run time, more than the allowance once the jobs running in parallel are counted. The user must still raise the spending limit or add a payment method (Settings, Billing and plans); this change makes the minutes go further. A full run billed ~10.9 minutes (verify 7.9, of which the tests are 5.3; docker 2.0; audit 1.0).
- `.github/workflows/ci.yml`: a `changes` job (about 1 billed minute) lists the changed files through the API and `scripts/ci-changes.mjs` (plain Node, nothing installed) classifies them. A newer push to the same pull request cancels the older run (main pushes are never cancelled). A change to Markdown and `docs/` only runs `tests/config-docs.test.ts` (the one test that reads the docs) instead of type check, lint, the suite, build and smoke. The image is built on every main push and on a pull request only when the Dockerfile, `.dockerignore`, the package files, `next.config.ts` or CI itself changed; the audit likewise (main, or the package files). The five extra smoke variants run on main, not on pull requests. The Next build cache is kept. `workflow_dispatch` added. Anything the classifier is unsure of means run everything, and main runs everything whatever changed.
- Expected: a code pull request ~8 billed minutes instead of ~11 (-25%); a docs-only one ~3.5 (-65%); superseded pull-request runs stop early. Most of a run is the test suite (5.3 minutes), which is not reduced.
- Not verifiable here: the workflow cannot run until billing is fixed. `tests/ci-workflow.test.ts` parses it and pins the structure (the jobs and outputs it refers to exist, main runs everything, the savings apply only to pull requests and docs-only changes, the classifier's cases and its stdin/stdout form), but GitHub's own validation happens on the first push. After billing is fixed, open a docs-only pull request to see the cheap path.

The runbook points to it. `tests/load-day-doc.test.ts` fails if a command on the page stops existing, if the page covers fewer than the four wrappers, or if any command puts the key on a command line; `tests/config-docs.test.ts` already checks the settings it names. Docs and tests only.

## 157. First dry run on the real cache: three small fixes (round 95, 2026-10-05)

The owner's first `vendor:load -- --dry-run` (13,862 fighters chosen from the cache, 12,450 loaded, 15,436 bouts, exit 0, nothing loaded) showed the whole pipeline working on real data: 408 of 10,674 records add up (3.8%), 9,990 are partial, 257 conflicts (2.4%) kept and marked disputed (84 extra wins, 177 extra losses; 129 reversible by one flip, of which 2 mutual, 70 open, 57 contradicted: the fight list's winners look right, the vendor's totals the odd side, as before), 19 lagging, 135 duplicate fights merged (25 across two profiles of one opponent), 1,412 fighters and 1,840 fights dropped for having no division. ## 158. Cancelled fights are cancelled, not "no result" (round 96, 2026-10-05)

Found by looking at why Hunter and Nadim showed "NC / no result" bouts dated in the future in the conflict listing, then counting the statuses in the owner's cache (counts only): of 47,687 fights, 36,708 are FINISHED with a result, 5,557 FINISHED with none, 5,220 CANCELLED, 127 NOT_STARTED in the future, 24 NOT_STARTED in the past, 36 LIVE in the past. The importer never read CANCELLED: a cancelled fight was loaded as an ordinary dated fight with no result, so fighters showed fights that never happened (as "no result yet"), and 202 "fighter booked twice on one night" warnings were mostly a cancelled fight beside its replacement. `mapFight` now gives a CANCELLED fight `status: "cancelled"` (no result, even if the feed carries stray fields) and counts it as `cancelledFights`. Everything that already understood cancelled bouts (record check, duplicate merge, double-booking check, upcoming list) now sees them.
On the owner's cache (dry run): 5,210 cancelled fights, double-booked warnings 202 to 15, duplicate fights merged 135 to 130 (a cancelled fight is no longer merged with its replacement). The 258 conflicts are unchanged: cancelled fights were not their cause. The 5,557 FINISHED fights with no result (6,059 with duplicates) are left as "no result yet": the feed gives no way to tell a real no-contest from a result nobody entered, and neither is guessed. The 24 past NOT_STARTED and 36 past LIVE fights also stay as they are.
Test: `tests/boxing-data-api.test.ts` (cancelled fight with stray winner and outcome keeps neither; a finished fight is unaffected); mutation (drop the status) killed. Docs: `docs/real-data-readiness.md` notes table.

## 159. How much of "no result yet" is the vendor's gap, not lag (round 97, 2026-10-05)

The owner's cache (counts only): 5,557 of the vendor's FINISHED fights carry no winner and no outcome, almost all in 2025-26. By month the share is under 4% until October 2025, then 23% (December), 32%, 41% (February 2026) and 23-36% every month since, so most are not results still to come. Real no-contests carry their own outcome (`NC` 137, `ND` 18) and every short code in the feed (`D`, `NC`, `ND`, `TD`, `RTD`, `PTS`) already maps (checked). New note `resultMissingOld`: a finished fight with no result more than 30 days old (`RESULT_LAG_DAYS`); on the cache 5,091 of the 6,059 (with duplicates) `resultMissing`. Nothing about the loaded data changes: these stay "no result yet", never guessed, never counted in a record. What changes is that a load now says how much of it is the vendor's gap, and the findings draft (`docs/boxing-data-api-data-findings.md` item 5) now carries the monthly figures and asks whether `FINISHED` only means the date has passed. Runbook and readiness notes tables updated. Test: recent and old no-result fights in `tests/boxing-data-api.test.ts`; two mutations killed.

## 160. Division-less fighters: measured, and not worth guessing (round 98, 2026-10-05)

The open question from the first real dry run was whether to place the 1,412 fighters dropped for having no division from their opponents' divisions. Counted on the owner's cache (14,359 fighter profiles): 9,276 (65%) have no `division` on the profile; for 8,077 of them one of their own fights carries a division (the importer already uses it: `divisionFromFight`); that leaves 1,199 with none anywhere. Of those, only 322 have opponents in exactly one division, 20 in several, and 857 have no opponent with a division at all. So guessing from opponents would place at most about a quarter of the dropped fighters, each an inference from the fighters they happened to meet. Decision: not built; they stay out and are counted (`fightersDroppedNoDivision`). If the vendor fills in the division on profiles (asked in the findings draft, item 6, now with these figures, replacing the stale "about 1,080"), they come in on the next load with no change. Docs only.

## 161. A card of nothing but cancelled fights is not a card (round 99, 2026-10-05)

Follow-up to §158. Once cancelled fights were kept as cancelled bouts, a card whose every fight the vendor lists as CANCELLED would have stayed as an event page showing only cancelled bouts (on the owner's cache 460 of 13,676 events are all-cancelled, 2,319 more have some fights cancelled). The load now leaves out an all-cancelled card and its bouts (new note `cancelledCardsLeftOut`, kept out of `eventsWithoutFights`); a card with at least one fight that was not cancelled stays, its cancelled bouts shown as cancelled. Dry run on the cache (13,132 fighters read): 406 cards left out, `eventsWithoutFights` 4,726 as before in kind. Test: a real load with an all-cancelled card and a mixed one (`tests/boxing-data-api.test.ts`); one mutation killed. Readiness notes table updated.

## 162. Amateur and multi-sport bouts are not professional fights (round 100, 2026-10-05)

The conflict explainer kept pointing at bouts that are not professional: Bakhodir Jalolov's "loss" to Joe Joyce on the day of the Rio Olympic final stood on his pro record (loaded 9-1-0 against the vendor's 17-0-0), found only when the bout was scheduled for 3 rounds or fewer. The vendor's own event titles name them ("Rio Olympics: Boxing Day 4", "Glasgow Commonwealth Games: Boxing Day 7", "Aichi-Nagoya Asian Games: Boxing Finals", "Russian National Amateur Boxing Championships 2019: Day 2": 73 distinct title shapes, about 700 of the 47,687 fights, read from the owner's cache by title only). `mapFight` now skips a fight whose EVENT title matches `AMATEUR_EVENT` (Olympics, Olympic Games, Asian / Commonwealth / Pan American / European Games, Youth Olympic..., Universiade, amateur, AIBA) and counts it (`amateurBoutsSkipped`, not in `fightsSkipped`, which stays the count of unusable rows). The venue is never read, so a pro card at the Olympic Auditorium or an Olympic Stadium stays, and "Olympic" alone does not match. Dry run on the cache: 497 skipped among the chosen fighters; conflicts 277 to 261, exactly-matching records 446 to 462 (of 11,389 to 11,501 checked), fighters dropped for no division 1,447 to 1,337 (amateurs with no pro division). Test: titles that match, titles that must not (Olympic Auditorium, "Olympus", an Olympic Stadium venue), two mutations killed (switch off; match the venue and the bare word "olympic"). Readiness and runbook notes tables, findings draft item 3 updated.

## 164. The first look at real pages: countries, belts and a stricter check (round 101, 2026-10-05)

A QA crawl of the site on a copy of the owner's real database (`smoke --database ... --crawl 150`: 1,136 pages in both languages) had no failing page on the English side and nothing but Arabic leaks on the other: 18,399 pieces of Latin-script text. Almost all are the supplier's proper nouns, which have no Arabic form until the enrichment step finds one, so on a real league they were noise burying the rest. Three real things sat underneath:
- **Countries.** `nationality` is a country name for most fighters and a demonym for 900 of 15,021 ("Mexican" beside "Mexico", "English", "Slovak Republic", "Croatia (Hrvatska)"), so one country was two on the Countries page, with a white flag and no Arabic name. `nationOf(nationality, code)` (importer): a name the app can place is spelled its one way; one it cannot is read through the feed's own `nationality_code` when that is a real country; the home nations (England, Scotland, Wales, Northern Ireland, by name or demonym, whatever the code says) stay themselves; anything else is kept as given and counted (`nationalityFromCode` 764 on the cache, `nationalityUnplaced` 2: "Western Australia", "Kurdistan"). Distinct countries 318 to 192, unplaced fighters 900 to 2. Found on the way: `lib/format.ts` named the retired code YU "Serbia" and it won over RS, so Serbian fighters had a wrong flag and no Arabic name; one alias fixes it.
- **Belts.** The real database had no organisations at all, and all 952 title bouts had no body, so every belt read "Unsanctioned" (Titles pages, belt lineage). A belt now belongs to the body its title name starts with (WBA, WBC, IBF, WBO, The Ring: all five of the 121 distinct names on the cache), and the provider's `fetchOrgs` carries exactly the bodies a loaded belt names, under the full names the picture and Wikidata steps look for (`lib/bodies.ts`). A belt of any other body would be counted (`titleBodyUnknown`, 0 on the cache). Only the first of a fight's titles is read, as before (219 fights have two or more).
- **The check.** `smoke --database` now blanks the supplier's names (fighters, nicknames, events, venues, cities, belts, people, organisations: `knownNames`, up to 14 words, without case) before looking for English on an Arabic page, so what is left is text that really is untranslated. The demo league's check is unchanged (strict, no names passed).
Not changed: what is left after this on an Arabic real-league page is the supplier's own strings (belt names such as "WBC World Super Welterweight Champion", "Pay Per View"), which have no Arabic form; a template for belt names is possible and not built. Tests: `nationOf` table (names, demonyms, home nations, no code, Unknown), the Serbia flag and code, title bodies and the bodies the load carries, the name-aware leak check; seven mutations killed. The owner's site shows all of this after the next load.

## 165. A fetch that survives a closed terminal (round 102, 2026-10-06)

The owner's real fetch (35,012 fighters, about 2.3 days at 400 an hour) stopped overnight at 44.8% (15,687 fighters) with no error: the terminal panel it ran in was closed or restarted, and a process in a terminal tab ends with the tab. The status could only say "not running" and "0 fighters in the last six hours". `npm run vendor:fetch -- --background` starts the same fetch (same key file, same defaults) detached: its own process group and session, no terminal (checked: parent process 1, own process group, no tty), output appended to `fetch.log` beside the cache and the process id in `fetch.pid`, kept awake with `caffeinate` as before. It waits a few seconds to catch an immediate refusal (a second fetch for the key, a bad key file) and says so with the end of the log rather than leave a dead process behind a hopeful message; a fetch that is finished by then says that. `--stop` ends a background fetch, touching only a live process whose command is a backfill (a recycled process id belonging to something else is never signalled). `vendor:status` shows the log's last line and age, so "why did it stop" has an answer, and its "next" line for a stopped fetch now offers `--background`. A Mac that sleeps or restarts still stops it; the same command starts it again where it left off (the cache is the checkpoint). Without `--background` nothing changes. Tests: the wrapper end to end on a stand-in vendor (detached run, log, no key in the log or the output), `--stop` on nothing / on this process / on a live backfill-named process, the status lines; three mutations killed (no command check, no log line, no log redirect). Load-day and runbook commands updated.

## 166. `vendor:site`: the real database on screen, started the same way every time (round 103, 2026-10-06)

Looking at the first real load took about eight failed starts: a tab in the wrong folder ("Missing script: build"), a port still held by the previous server (three times), a build run over the folder a running server was using, a server that ended with its terminal tab. `npm run vendor:site -- --start [--build]` serves the real database (`~/ringside-real/real.db` or `DATABASE_PATH`, `BOXING_PROVIDER=licensed`) on port 3480 (`--port N`), detached (own process group, no terminal: checked on a real run, parent process 1), with `site.log` and `site.pid` beside the database. It refuses, in one line, a missing database, a port something else holds (with the command that shows what) and a site already running; it builds first when asked or when there is no build; it waits for `/api/health` and reports the fighters and bouts served, or the end of the log if the site did not come up. `--stop` and `--restart` act only on a live process whose command is a Next server (a recycled id is never signalled); a bare run reports what is running. It never sets the vendor key or the storage statement (serving a database needs neither) and sets no site address, so the page still says not to expose it. Shared with the fetch wrapper: `livePid(pidFile, kind)` (the check that a process id is still the thing we started). Tested without a build (CI tests before it builds): the plan (defaults, files, no key in the environment, bad ports), the port and health probes, the command's refusals and its status, a pid file naming this process left alone; three mutations killed (key in the environment, port never busy, any process stoppable). Run by hand on a scratch copy of the owner's database: build, start, status, refused second start, restart, stop, port freed. Load-day (new step 5) and runbook updated.

## 167. The status of a fetch that has only just started (round 104, 2026-10-06)

Right after the owner restarted the fetch, `vendor:status` said "19,206 to fetch: about 6.7 days at 119 an hour" when the real pace was about 370 an hour (about 2.2 days): the fetch had run four minutes, and its 119 answers were being read as an hour's work. The six-hour average, the better figure for a long run, was no help either: it counts an earlier run and the gap before this one. For a fetch running less than six hours the pace is now its own work since it started (`cacheState` counts the answers newer than the start of the running fetch, `fightersSince`) over the time it has run; in the first five minutes, or with fewer than ten answers, it is too soon to measure and the line says so ("it started 2 minutes ago: too soon to measure, the plan's 400 an hour assumed"). "SLOWED" is judged only for a fetch older than six hours (a younger one has nothing to be compared against); an older run, and one that is not running, are read exactly as before. On the owner's cache the line now says 2.2 days at 360 an hour, matching the pace measured by counting files over two minutes (6 a minute). Tests: the new pace in five situations (two minutes in; half an hour with an earlier run in the last hour; a slow start reported as slow; an hour and a half in with a six-hour count that includes the earlier run; young versus old for "SLOWED"), and the count itself on files of known age; four mutations killed (ignore the young-run rule, use the last hour instead of the run's own count, "SLOWED" for a young run, a wrong start for the count). Status only: nothing the fetch does changes.

## 168. Name-only opponents: measured, and not worth keeping (round 105, 2026-10-06)

The last open question from the first real dry run: 3,413 fights are skipped because one fighter has no profile id (`fightsSkippedNoFighter`, 7.7% of the list), the opponent being named only. Whether to keep them as name-only fighters was counted on the owner's cache (14,359 fighter profiles, with the fights as read on 2026-10-06; counts only): 10,876 fighters have a career total higher than the fights held, missing 149,878 fights in all; of the skipped fights, 2,051 of 2,798 (finished ones) touch a fetched fighter; they could fill at most 2,003 of the missing fights, 1.3% of the gap, would shrink 518 records and complete 12, and the opponents are 1,640 distinct names (545 appearing more than once, which would need matching by name with all its false merges) that would become profiles with no division facts, no birth year, no stance, no stats. The gap is the early careers the fight list does not reach, not these fights. Decision: not built; the fights stay out and are counted. If the vendor adds ids to those opponents (item 4 of the findings draft, now carrying these figures), they come in on the next load with no change. Docs only: findings draft item 4 and the runbook notes table.

## 170. Enhancement plan: calendar, career strip, watchlist digest, Arabic belts, public API, embeds (2026-10-06)

Six additions to the platform, chosen from a review of what the site has and what the real vendor data adds. Common opponents were on the list and are already built (compare page, `lib/common-opponents.ts`), so they are dropped. E-mail and push alerts are left out on purpose: they send messages to real people and need the owner's choice of provider and consent text first. Built on one branch (`claude/enhancements`), one local commit per feature in this order, each with tests, English and Arabic strings, docs and a PLAN section; nothing is pushed until the owner says so. Order: the ones with no new risk first (1 to 4), the two that redistribute data last (5, 6).

1. **Calendar feeds (.ics).** `lib/ics.ts` builds RFC 5545 text from pure inputs (CRLF lines, folding at 75 octets without splitting a multi-byte character, escaping, all-day events because the data has a date and no start time, a stable `UID` per bout so a re-fetch updates rather than duplicates, `STATUS:CANCELLED` for a fight that fell off, a `DTSTAMP` from the data's age). `GET /feeds/calendar.ics?slugs=a,b&lang=ar` for a watchlist, `?event=ID` and `?bout=ID` for one card or fight, nothing for the next 60 days of cards; absolute links from `SITE_URL`. Surfaces: an "Add to calendar" link on event, fight and preview pages and a "Subscribe" link (and a `webcal://` one) on the watchlist page. Tests: the builder (escaping, folding with Arabic, CRLF, UID stability, cancelled), the route over a league (filter, limit, locale, unknown slug), a small parser in the test to prove the output re-reads, the smoke list.
2. **Career in the data.** A strip on the fighter page for a career held in part: debut year to last fight, the years with fights held marked, the stretch before the first held fight shaded as "not in the data", and one sentence ("16 of 27 fights, 2014 to 2024"). `careerStrip(b, bouts, today)` is pure (years, counts, the unheld span, whether the supplier total is disputed); an SVG drawn on the server, right-to-left aware, with a text alternative. Tests: the pure function across a whole career, a partial one, a disputed one and one with no debut year; the page in both languages through the smoke crawl.
3. **Watchlist digest.** "Since you last looked": for the fighters starred on this device, results since the last visit, rating moves since then and the next fight. `watchDigest(world, slugs, since, t)` is pure; `GET /api/watch/digest?slugs=&since=&lang=`; the page keeps the last-visit time in the browser (per device, no account needed, as the picks recap does) and marks it seen on leaving. Tests: since in the past, in the future, a fighter with nothing new, a cancelled fight, a very long list (capped), both languages.
4. **Arabic belt names.** A template for belt titles (`WBC World Super Welterweight Champion` becomes Arabic from the body, the modifier words and the division's own Arabic name) used where a name has no stored translation, so it never overrides a reviewed one. Unknown shapes are left as written. Marked as machine Arabic in the docs: it needs a native reviewer, like the rest. Tests: a table of real title shapes (every shape in the first real cache), an unknown one, a stored translation winning, no Latin letters left except the body's code.
5. **Public read-only API.** `/api/v1/` fighters (list with paging and search, one fighter), rankings by division, events (upcoming and recent, one event), and `/api/v1/openapi.json`; plain JSON, `Cache-Control: public`, CORS for GET, a small per-address rate limit, public fields only (never anything from accounts or picks). **Off by default for a licensed feed** (`PUBLIC_API=1` turns it on, and the vendor's terms must allow redistribution: that is the owner's to state, as with storage); on by default for the demo league. Known to the doctor and `.env.example`. Tests: the envelope, paging bounds, 404s, CORS, the gate in both directions, a scan that no private field name appears, the OpenAPI paths matching the routes.
6. **Embeds.** `/embed/fighter/[slug]` and `/embed/rankings/[division]`: bare pages with no site chrome, a credit and a link back (and the vendor credit when the feed is licensed), the locale in the path, with a snippet generator on a developers page. Framing is allowed on `/embed/` only; every other route keeps its frame-ancestors rule (tested both ways). Same gate as the API. Tests: the headers on both kinds of route, the render, the locale, the gate, the vendor credit.

Cross-cutting: every new string through `t()` with an Arabic version; `npm run i18n:check` clean; each feature added to `lib/smoke.ts` routes; new settings added to the doctor and `.env.example` (the config test insists); the privacy page and sitemap checked for anything new that stores or sends; docs under `docs/`. Risks: redistribution of vendor data (the gate above), framing (headers tested for both directions), calendar clients' handling of folded Arabic (tested with a re-parse), PLAN numbering and `i18n/keys.json` collisions with other sessions at merge (renumbered then). Done means: lint, tsc, the whole suite, the build, the five smoke runs, and a fresh-clone run, with the owner's say-so before any push.
